# FORMATS.md — format support matrix

Status after M0–M72 (v0.44.0). "Oracle coverage" = fixtures with
byte-exact differential manifests (built from The Unarchiver's `unar`,
and macutils' `macunpack` where `unar` fails; see TESTING.md); every one
passes, plus 10 corruption variants each in the robustness suite. "Peak
RAM" = allocator high-water mark across the whole corpus for that format
(engine allocations only, via `macextract t --stats`). "400 KB budget" =
the corpus under `--budget 409600` (`tools/run_budget.py`; TESTING.md, *M9 additions*).

## Contents
One line per `## ` section, in file order. Regenerated by
`tools/check_docs.py`, which fails CI if it drifts from the headings
below it. Per-format detail is under *Table notes*.

## Table notes

Keyed to the markers in the table above. Where a format also has a
section of its own further down, the note points there.

### DiskDoubler ✦

In scope since 2026-08-15 (M12); all eleven methods since 2026-08-21
(M20). Three containers — a single compressed file (`0xABCD` and the
header's own SIZE, 84 or 48 — *The 1990 header*, below), `DDAR`, `DDA2`.
Method 8 is Compact Pro's two stages in a DiskDoubler container at a
0xfff0 Huffman block (*Method 8's Huffman block*, below); 10 (DDn) is a
64 KB-window LZSS whose literals, offsets and lengths are three Huffman
streams back to back. Delta-1 preprocessing is applied where the header
asks for it, outside the checksum, as the reference does. Reasoning:
DECISIONS.md 2026-08-15, *DiskDoubler in scope*; 2026-08-20; 2026-08-21
(the Salient disk; methods 3, 4 and 5).

**Peak heap 8 KB–392 KB**, the same on Linux and on a Mac Plus at 4 MB
and 1 MB (`macextract t --stats`; *DiskDoubler and Now Compress on the
Mac (2026-08-15)*):

| method | peak | why |
|---|---|---|
| 3 | 8,192 B (8,208 B on the 68000) | two buffers, no state |
| 4 | 9,476 B (9,508 B on the 68000) | two buffers and one Huffman table |
| 7 | 10 KB | a 2 KB window |
| 1 | 17 KB on an 11 KB fork | sized from the input, like every `unc_lzw` caller |
| 10 (DDn) | ~100 KB | window sized from the entry up to 64 KB, plus a buffer sized from each block's header |
| 2 | **101,888 B** on the specimen (60 contexts in 564 bytes) | 1,536 B per distinct context byte, built on first use |
| 5 | **401,408 B** on `synth-dd-m5.dd` (all 256 contexts); **405,520 B (396 KB)** on a 68000, 2026-08-22 | the same |

`synth-dd-m5.dd` passes at 4 MB, at the **1 MB floor** (597,036 B for
the engine — *Resident memory, stack and launch*, `Budget`) and on
System 7: the largest single allocation this engine asks a Mac Plus for,
and the only thing in this format outside the 400 KB budget column.

#### The 1990 header

**`0xABCD0054` is the word `0xABCD` and a 16-bit header SIZE**: 84 in all
195 file headers in the corpus, **48** in a file DiskDoubler ß05 (1990,
an INIT with no application) wrote here (DECISIONS.md 2026-09-22, *the
header that was a length*). The first 48 bytes are the same fields; a
short header ends before the checksums at +48, `info2` at +52 and the
delta distances at +54, and is followed by the two forks and **no header
trailer** (3.x's, not the format's).

Read since 2026-09-22 for **64 B** on a 1 MB Mac Plus (48 code, 16 A5).
`unar` answers "format not recognized", so the grade is the bytes
DiskDoubler was given (`tools/vmac/diskdoubler.py --beta-verify`, both
forks byte-exact); `ci.sh` carries the shape as a unit test with stored
forks and two negative controls. **No corpus fixture has one**, so the
differential suite does not cover it.

What writes each method found since M12, and how it is graded
(DECISIONS.md 2026-08-20; 2026-08-21, *the Salient disk*, *methods 3, 4
and 5*):

| method | what writes it | specimens | how graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1, Unix compress | DiskDoubler 3.7, "Method A (fastest)" | `subject-A.dd`, both forks; one more on the 3.1.2 disk | the bytes that went IN, on the emulated Mac; `unar` agrees |
| 7, Stac LZS | the Sigma Designs card DiskDoubler 3.x drove | the two THINK C libraries on the 3.7 disk, 2,226 B each, **and 22 forks on the 3.1.2 disk, up to 97,906 B in 8 chunks**; in the corpus as `dd37-IACUtilities-A4.lib.dd` (`harvest.py`'s `derived:hfsvol:` recipe) | the fork's own XOR checksum; `unar` agrees byte for byte, naming the method `Stac LZS` |
| 2, splay Huffman | DiskDoubler 1.x-3.1.x | **one**: `XCMD.h` on the 3.1.2 disk, 564 → 386 B (2026-08-21) | the fork's own 16-bit sum, and the output is legible 1990 Salient C |
| 3, RLE90 | DiskDoubler 1.x-2.x | **none** | Salient's 1991 expander, on forks this project encoded (below), at run lengths 3-255 and at **2**, the shortest the format expresses |
| 4, StuffIt Huffman | DiskDoubler 1.x-2.x | **none** | the same, on two trees: one 2-11 levels deep and one over all 256 symbols with **a leaf at depth 1** |
| 5, splay Huffman, tree count in the stream | DiskDoubler 1.x-2.x | **none** | the same, at **every count the format allows** — 256, 128, 64 and 32 — and DiskDoubler 4.1.1 agrees on 256 |

Methods 3, 4 and 5 are graded without a specimen: `tools/vmac/ddencode.py`
writes forks with our encoder and Salient's own 1991 DiskDoubler reads
them back byte-exact under Mini vMac. Measured that way, and in neither
reference:

- **the 0x5a XOR applies to methods 1, 2 and 5 but NOT to 3 and 4**, on
  headers carrying `info1 = 0x2a` (every real file of that era). macutils
  agrees; **XADMaster's case 4 does not**, marked untested by its author;
- **DiskDoubler 4.1.1 has dropped methods 3 and 4** (*Unknown compression
  method…*);
- **method 5's tree count is 32, 64, 128 or 256 and nothing else**:
  `CODE` 11 of the 1991 `DiskDoubler™ App` checks it at 0x1dfc, turns 0
  into 256 at 0x228c, and masks the next context with `count - 1` at
  0x1fbc (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-28, *the tree count*).

What the 1991 expander accepts, twelve forks in one boot, 2026-08-29
(DECISIONS.md 2026-08-29, *methods 3, 4 and 5, graded across the format*):

| asked | answer |
|---|---|
| method 5 at 32 and 64 trees | **byte-exact**; all four legal counts now exercised, where only 256 and 128 ever had been |
| method 4 over all 256 symbols, skewed | **byte-exact**; a leaf at depth 1 and leaves at depth 10, where the older subject's tree is 2-11 |
| method 3 with `ESC 2`, the shortest run | **byte-exact** — no encoder writes it, because at 2 the escape costs what it saves, so nothing had ever graded our shortest run |
| method 3 with `ESC 1`, at offset 0 and 1,800 bytes in | **refused, both times** — so it is the token and not an escape with nothing to repeat |
| a fork whose compressed size EXCEEDS its original | **refused** — the control, using no token the accepted case does not |

The control separates a size check from a codec rule: both `ESC` forks
were first refused because they had grown. **`ESC 1` cost a divergence**:
`dd_rle` was the only one of six RLE90 call sites passing `strict = 0`;
it now refuses as Salient's expander does, and all 91 DiskDoubler
fixtures stay byte-exact (BLOCKERS.md; DECISIONS.md 2026-08-29).

Method 2 is order-1 adaptive: 256 splay trees selected by the previous
byte, each starting complete over 256 leaves and rotating the emitted
leaf toward the root; method 1's 0x5a XOR under the same
`info1`/`info2` condition. Method 1 is `unc_lzw` behind three bytes of
its own, the 0x5a XOR over the DECODED bytes and header bytes, the sum
adjusted by those three bytes. Method 7 is a 2 KB-window LZSS whose bit
stream AND output are stored complemented, match lengths unbounded by the
window (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-20).

**Still missing**: a real specimen of methods 3, 4 and 5 (OPEN_ITEMS.md).
The **11 forks across 2 fixtures** neither oracle adjudicated — all 33
forks of both archives — are byte-exact against DiskDoubler's own
expander under System 7 outside the resource header's OS-stamped bytes
16..127 (`tools/vmac/diskdoubler.py --adjudicate`) and against `unar`
everywhere when handed one fork with the declared length one byte larger
— its DDn off-by-one (`tools/run_ddforks.py`; DECISIONS.md 2026-08-22,
*one byte, and the eight forks that had no oracle*). The masking window
has independent evidence: `DiskTester™` 3.7.7, compressed on the Norton
DiskDoubler Pro 1.1 floppy and uncompressed in Fifth Generation's 4.0
StuffIt archive, matches our decode over all 36,104 bytes except 46, all
inside 16..127 (`tools/vmac/ndd.py --grade`).

**A `DDA2` entry of type `0x1000` carries no `0xABCD0054` header.**
DiskDoubler Pro writes it **exactly when the data fork is stored AND the
file has no resource fork** — all four cells measured 2026-08-21
(`tools/vmac/ndd.py --stored`; DECISIONS.md 2026-08-21, *the Symantec
release*); size is no factor (40 B and 20,000 B). 45 records in the CC0
corpus have the full header against 30 headerless. So **no `0x1000`
record carries a resource fork**, `+86` is the data fork's checksum, and
`+54`/`+58` are the creation and modification dates (measured with
subjects stamped 1990 and 1995). Read since 2026-08-21; **`unar` 1.10.8
still stops** at the third entry of five of the nine DiskDoubler Pro
4.1.1 archives in `ssokolow/diskdoubler-test-files` (*Archive parsing
failed!*) and reads all four 3.7.7 ones (measured).

That corpus is CC0 with the **originals** published beside the archives
(`tools/run_ddtf.py`):

| what | count | result |
|---|---|---|
| single-file `0xABCD0054` containers (7 files × 9 methods) | 63 | byte-exact |
| `DDAR` / `DDA2` archives (methods 1, 6, 8, 9, 10 and stored) | 9 | byte-exact |
| self-extractors, plain and prompt-for-folder, and the same BinHexed | 20 | byte-exact |
| **forks compared against the published originals, in all** | **456** | **exact** |

The TIFF in each set has no published original and comes out identical
from all nine compressions. 115 of these became fixtures in M17
(TESTING.md).

#### Method 8's Huffman block, pinned by the forks' own checksum

**0xfff0, measured 2026-10-06.** A method-8 fork states a CRC-16/ARC over
its decoded bytes and a wrong block size yields the right number of
wrong bytes, so the file adjudicates. `tools/derive_dd8.py` sweeps eleven
sizes over every method-8 fork in reach (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-06, *the
other half of that citation*):

| forks | sizes that satisfy the stored CRC |
|---|---|
| the 7 in `ddtf-dd377ddb-*`, up to 44,549 B | every size at or above 0x1000 — these streams never reach a flush |
| **`dd8-hermes-docs.dd`**, 210,944 B from 84,057 | **0xfff0 alone**, of eleven |
| **`dd8-list-of-all-files.dd`**, 242,237 B from 87,646 | **0xfff0 alone**, of eleven |

The two were found by the key `detection=abcd0054*`: 15,454 DiskDoubler
compressed files, 2,046 over 40 KB, **612 method-8 forks** up to
6,212,378 B compressed (TESTING.md, *Searching somebody else's index by
content*). `derive_dd8.py` fails if the oracle's sha256 and the forks'
CRC ever disagree on any of the 9 forks × 11 sizes; one flipped CRC bit
was shown to make it fail.

### Now Compress ✧

In scope since 2026-08-15 (M12). A flat entry table nested by a
per-directory entry count, then one stream per file; each stream a table
of blocks of up to 32 KB of output against a 32 KB sliding history, in
one of five shapes (stored, LZSS, Huffman, Huffman then LZSS, and a
"new" LZSS with its own length/offset tables). Each file's stream holds
exactly its resource fork then its data fork, so this reader cuts no
solid stream and runs in memory fixed by the format (DECISIONS.md
2026-08-15, *Now Compress without the solid stream*). Peak heap: 131,072 B
measured on a 9.9 MB output; **235,568 B with four block slots since
2026-09-19**, falling back to two (170,032 B) (DECISIONS.md 2026-09-19,
*the slide that ran every block*). Extracts fully under a 400 KB budget;
byte-exact on an emulated Mac Plus at 4 MB and 1 MB (2026-08-15).
Format description: `docs/formats/now-compress.md`.

**The two real fixtures** (block loop instrumented, and reproduced from
the file since 2026-09-21):

- `derived-Expand_Now_1.0.1.sea`: three blocks, "new" LZSS twice and
  Huffman-only once, zero padding, no offset delta, no length past a
  nibble, deepest code 12.
- `derived-SyntheticReferences2.0.sea` (1995): a folder, a two-fork file
  and a 9,949,696-byte FileMaker database out of 2.9 MB, in two streams
  of 306 blocks, all "new" LZSS.

**Every member carries one stream-restart record**, at the boundary
between its resource and data forks (153 members of 153;
`docs/formats/now-compress.md`, *A member's blocks*). The other three
shapes, a 17-bit code and both routes into the offset-delta search are
covered by 14 generated archives adjudicated by `unar` (TESTING.md),
byte-exact there and under qemu-m68k.

**Now Compress itself reads an archive written here** (2026-09-21): its
own extractor, under the emulator, produced both members of a
`synth_now.py` archive byte-exact. Opening needs a **16-byte trailer**,
**32,768-byte blocks**, and every block on a **word boundary** (the
first two are refusals, the third a wrong decode). The fourteen
`unar`-graded archives keep the old shape, because an aligned region can
leave `unar` fewer than the four spare bits it needs (TESTING.md, *The
rungs*).

**`unar` cannot read the 1995 archive**: 129 of its 306 blocks end
within 7 bits of their buffer and XADMaster's bit reader raises
end-of-buffer there (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-16). Its four forks were
adjudicated by the decoder inside the self-extracting archive, which
agrees on every one (DECISIONS.md, *The archive that carries its own
oracle*).

**Block shapes from the encoder itself** (`tools/vmac/nowcompress.py`,
Now QuickFiler 5.0.1 under System 7 on an emulated Mac Plus):

| what | block flags | shape |
|---|---|---|
| Compress ▸ Faster | 0x10 | LZSS only |
| Compress ▸ Smaller, text | 0x1f | LZSS only |
| Compress ▸ Smaller, mixed content | 0x3f | Huffman then LZSS |
| Compress ▸ Smaller, incompressible | 0x00 | stored |
| Archive ▸ Smaller (a `.sea`) | 0x100 / 0x140 / **0x120** | stored / new LZSS / **Huffman alone** |

Seven specimens, all byte-exact through this decoder and `unar`; with the
1993 fixture, all five shapes on real bytes. The mode word is layers:
low five bits LZSS, 0x20 a Huffman code over it, 0x40 the archive coder,
0x100 inside an archive; **0x20 alone is unframed Huffman** (the 1993
fixture's middle block).

**The archive coder** (2026-09-21, matches planted with `--case tokdist`
and `--case toklen`): a reader written from `docs/formats/now-compress.md`
alone decodes **306 of 306** blocks of the 1995 archive, **3 of 3** of
the 1993 one, and 76 of 76 harness-made; a block's last four bytes are a
32-bit sum of the rest; the longest match is 257 bytes and the offset
alphabet ends at 65,791 (DECISIONS.md 2026-09-21, *the pair that never
repeats*). The encoder REFUSES a wholly incompressible file, so a stored
block appears only inside a file that compresses elsewhere, and a stored
block may carry flag 0x100, which no decoder here or in XADMaster
examines (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-16).

### Alysis SuperDisk! ✧✧

In scope since 2026-08-20. An archive is eight unread bytes, an optional
chain of 68k decompressor modules, then a record stream: `0x29` opens a
folder, `0x2a` closes one, `0x28` is a file, `0x27` ends the archive. A
file's forks follow its record inline — **data fork first** — each a
chain of blocks of at most 32 KB of output, each block's length counting
itself, bit 15 marking a stored block. The method table is read out of
Disk Expander 1.2's own `Cmpr` resources, one module per method
(DECISIONS.md, *SuperDisk!, read out of SuperDisk!*).

| method | name | shape |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | " Faster" | context predictor, bitmap and literals stored |
| 2 | " Tighter" | bitmap Huffman-coded, literals stored |
| 3 | — | bitmap stored, literals Huffman-coded |
| 4 | — | both Huffman-coded |
| 5-8 | — | 1-4 again, over a 16-bit context instead of a 13-bit one |
| 9 | "30% Solution" | Huffman alone, its region starting at the block's LENGTH word (*Method 9's method word*, below) |
| 10 | "SlowFastA" | LZSS: a 32-bit control word per 32 items, matches of `nibble + 2` bytes at `out - offset + 1`, 12-bit offsets with a 16-bit escape (*Method 10*, below) |
| 11 | "SlowFastB" | LZSS, lengths 2-282, offsets 1-49120 |
| 13 | "SlowFastC" | **declined per entry** — its module only COMPRESSES (*Module 13*, below) |
| 20 | "Tennessee" | **not a block method**: its module is an ENCODER, and what it writes declares method 11 (*Module 20*, below) |

#### Method 9's method word

**The method is the low BYTE of the word.** Method 9 keeps its Huffman
`startbits` in the high byte (a real block reads 0x0809; refused until
2026-08-25, BLOCKERS.md). Source: the reference dispatches on `a2@(3)` —
CODE 2 @0x6ba — and calls the Huffman routine with `a2` unchanged for
method 9 (@0x712) where the 1..4 family first advances four bytes
(@0x812).

**Every method 1..9 has a second implementation on Linux**: Disk
Expander 1.2's `Cmpr` Add-In Modules, 68k with no Toolbox, lifted out of
the corpus fixture and run under `qemu-m68k` by `tools/sdmodule/`, in
`./ci.sh`:

| what | through both readers |
|---|---|
| corpus blocks (method 11) | 4, byte-exact |
| specimens Alysis's own compressor wrote (methods 1 and 6) | 22, byte-exact |
| synthetic blocks, methods 1-9 | byte-exact, the refill boundary included since 2026-08-28 |
| method 10, against its module and the bytes behind it | equal three ways |
| method 11 blocks written by Alysis's own encoder (module 20) | 5, byte-exact |

**The one disagreement was this engine's defect, fixed 2026-08-28**: when
the coded flag bitmap ends exactly on a 32-bit refill, the literals were
read four bytes early. Graded on blocks Alysis's 1991 panel wrote at 160
and 256 flag bits (`sdcompress.py --boundary`): wrong from byte 0 before,
byte-exact after. It reaches about one block in 32 of any method 2, 4, 6
or 8 fork; the corpus's archives use 7 and 11 (BLOCKERS.md;
DECISIONS.md 2026-08-28, *the refill boundary*).

**The single-file container** (2026-08-22): "Make File" output — a
30-byte header, the name as a Pascal string padded to an even offset,
then the same block chains, data fork first. Derived from five specimens
Alysis's 1991 control panel wrote under Mini vMac (a resource fork,
dates 1990/1995, a 20-character name, over 32 KB); **all five decode
byte-exact against their inputs**, dates, type, creator and Finder flags
included (`tools/vmac/sdcompress.py --specimens`, `--verify`;
DECISIONS.md 2026-08-22, *later still*).

#### Method 10

Landed 2026-08-25; nothing in reach writes one. Transcribed from `Cmpr`
21 of Disk Expander 1.2 and graded by that module under `qemu-m68k`: 20
cases equal three ways. The module settled the match length as
`nibble + 2` (the DBF's target is the second MOVEB) and the wide-offset
escape as sixteen bits (`ASL.W #8` shifts the marker nibble out); our
first reading of both matched only our own test encoder.

#### Module 20

Measured 2026-08-25: module 20 answers `badCommandError` to decompress
and compresses on command 2; every block it writes declares **method
11**, and this engine decodes all of them to their inputs (five, 4 KB of
zeros to 30 KB of text; random data declined with
`inadequateCompressionError`). There is no method 20 bitstream; the
module is a **compressor for method 11**, used by `tools/sdmodule/` to
grade SlowFastB.

#### Module 13

Measured 2026-08-29: it compresses on command 2 and writes a header of
its own — a length word, the word 13, a THIRTY-TWO-bit uncompressed
length (every other block has a six-byte header with a 16-bit one). On
decompress it executes `_Debugger` at its own 0xd4, so **no software in
reach has a method 13 decoder** (Alysis's extension hands method 9 and up
to the module of that number, INIT 32 @0x50c8). Four streams written
here read as no method this engine or the other modules knows
(`tools/sdmodule/`, phase 6). It runs only with the working buffer its
header declares — 229,618 B, the long at +0x1E checked at INIT 32
@0x51a2; at 64 KB it died, and the claim it ran is withdrawn
(BLOCKERS.md).

**"Method 13" is not one codec** (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-25 and
2026-08-29):

| product | module 13 |
|---|---|
| Disk Expander 1.2 | 5,030 B, 229,618 B working buffer; `Cmpr` named **SlowFastC**, module-name field at +0x3C **" Faster"** |
| **eDisk 1.2** (Alysis, 1993, md5 `b6e12548edd0baa5cfb79441a3cdffcd`) | resource `JC`, module name **" Copy"**, 310 B and 290 B in two builds, an unrolled `MOVE.B (A0)+,(A1)+` — a byte copier |
| **More Disk Space 1.1** (md5 `ca5c80f5ff2c6738c8ac27a8b1ce8d43`) | none in the application; its `Alysis Preferences` carries 1, 2, 9, 10, 11 twice and a `Cmpr` 26 named SlowFastC declaring 5,030 B — Disk Expander's |

**A file could carry one**: INIT 32's compress path (@0x5232) indexes an
eleven-entry table at +0xf2e by a setting word, naming methods 1, 11,
17, 2, 18, 12, 1, 11, 11, 1 and **13** (level 11); 12, 17 and 18 have no
module in this product. 13 and 20 are declined because both modules
refuse decompress; their modules are named by
`tools/sdmodule/run_sdmodule.py --list`.

**Oracle: the archives themselves.** `unar` and `macunpack` stop at the
BinHex envelope. `tools/vmac/superdisk.py --adjudicate` runs each
self-extracting archive under System 7 on an emulated Mac Plus: **5 forks
across the 2 archives, every one identical** (2026-08-20; resource bytes
16..127 masked). `tools/run_superdisk.py` replays that offline and adds
116 corrupted variants — the only robustness coverage, since `t` does
not unwrap envelopes.

**That extractor also reads streams written here** (2026-09-01;
`tools/synth_sd.py` streams put in `SD! Utilities.x`'s data fork,
`tools/vmac/superdisk.py --nest`, `--stored`; `tools/vmac/README.md`):

| asked | stream | result |
|---|---|---|
| a path that SUMS past the 255-byte cap | 10 folders of 31 characters, 11 files, declared paths 43→331 B | **11/11 made, byte-identical**, and for the 3 files and 2 folders past the cap the UNCUT path `unc_path_leaf` reports is the path the Macintosh built |
| a stored block, which the corpus has none of | the same 4,796 B fork coded and stored, in one folder | **both byte-exact** |
| a stream that OPENS with a file record | one file, no folder | made |

The second confirms `CODE 2` @0x614's stored-block encoding (a length
with bit 15 set, counting its own two header bytes); **all seven blocks
in both fixtures are coded**, methods 7 and 11 (TESTING.md).

Peak heap **74,220 B** for `SD! Utilities.x` and **85,943 B** for
`Disk Expander .X` (`macextract t --stats`); both extract fully under a
400 KB budget. The 64 KB wide context table is allocated only for
methods 5-8, and SlowFastB's window only for a method-11 block, sized
from the fork since 2026-08-24 (was a flat 98,304 B; `Disk Expander .X`
was 117,294 B).

### StuffIt SpaceSaver ✦✦

In scope since 2026-08-22; all five methods since 2026-08-25. Layout,
module table and methods: *StuffIt SpaceSaver*, below. Graded by 48
files SpaceSaver itself wrote (harvested 2026-09-26) and by Aladdin's own
`CMod` codecs under `qemu-m68k` (`tools/ssmodule/`, TESTING.md); the
encoder written from the same disassembly is a consistency check, not an
oracle.

### BinHex: multipart, in both directions ††

Since 2026-08-15 (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-15, *several BinHex documents in
one file*).

**One document posted in several parts**, each with its own mail header
in mid-stream: each LINE is judged and one holding "intermediate
garbage" skipped — the rule of `reference/macutils/hexbin/hqx.c`, which
works because the alphabet omits `n`, `o`, `s`, `t`, `u` and the space.
hexbin's equal-length half is left out (it can drop a legitimate short
final line; hexbin ships `-c` to disable it); a junk line built only of
alphabet characters is caught by the per-fork CRC as a per-entry error.
Both halves are asserted in `tools/run_multihqx.py` and fail with the
rule removed. **DeHQX's part separator** (since 2026-10-06; a per-entry
CRC failure before): a line beginning `--- end of part` opens a gap and a
line that is exactly `---` closes it — DeHQX 2.0.1's rule, checked by
`tools/derive_dehqx.py` against Peter Lewis's documentation and asserted
both ways by `run_multihqx.py`; **+0 B resident** (`ovl_binhex`;
DECISIONS.md 2026-10-06, *the separator that was in the alphabet*).

**Several documents in one file** — all extracted: after each banner,
':', stream, ':', the decoder looks for the next banner, as DeHQX 2.0.1's
documentation says its decoder does ("continues scanning the file for the
next '(This file...'"). A banner with nothing usable after it is an
error. Graded by `hexbin` and by the same fixtures decoded one at a time
(TESTING.md, *Several BinHex documents in one file*); the differential
suite cannot see it, because `unar` takes the first document too. The
179 fixtures are single-document and all still byte-exact.

\* **StuffIt method 1 occurs once and is oracle-adjudicated** (since
2026-08-08): the 470-byte resource fork of `I Want To Buy Some Cheese` in
`derived-New_Python.sit` (470 → 389), byte-identical to `unar`, visible
only through per-entry manifests (TESTING.md, *When the oracle fails on
an entry*). Runs of `0x90`, the 255-byte maximum token and output ending
inside a run are covered by `synth-sit-rle90.sit` (`tools/synth_sit1.py`),
also oracle-adjudicated.

### StuffIt classic: method 6 ‡

**"Fixed Huffman", implemented and oracle-adjudicated since 2026-08-08.**
In the corpus once: the 34,818-byte resource fork of `INITLoader` in
`derived-INITLoader.sit`, two Huffman segments, byte-exact. The tree's
SHAPE is a constant of the format; only each leaf's byte is declared per
segment.

**The tree is 26 bytes, built per fork, since 2026-09-21** (was 1,028 B
of tables from macutils in `src/sit6_tables.h`; now deark's thirteen
(count, length) pairs plus an assignment rule, `sit6_build`). Measured
with `tools/build_mac.sh`: **code +408 B, A5 −1,000 B**, so **−592 B off
every extraction's budget on a 1 MB Mac Plus**; **+1,028 B transient heap**
while a method-6 fork is read; engine ticks 3 → 3; `run_tiers.py` still
69/69 at 1 MB and 66/69 at 512 KB (the tallies of that day). Peak on the
fixture **40,982 → 42,010 B**; the segment buffer is sized from the
segment (31,806 B here), capped at 64 KB. `tools/check_sit6_tree.py`
holds the two trees against each other over **all 8,192 bit paths of
depth 13**, and the synthetic fixture regenerates byte-identical.

Branches the real fork cannot reach — a *stored* segment, PackBits
fillers, longest and shortest runs, a one-byte alphabet, twelve segments
in a fork — are covered by `synth-sit-fixhuf.sit` (`tools/synth_sit6.py`),
oracle-adjudicated, 10 forks over 5 entries.

**The framing is `docs/formats/stuffit-method-6.md`'s since 2026-09-22**,
at 0 B and 0 ticks: nothing executable changed. Across the corpus's 11
forks: **27 segments — 13 Huffman and 14 stored**; the largest PackBits
stream declared is **31,806 B**; code counts 1, 10, 11, 17, 129, 191,
254, 255 and 256; **97 distinct PackBits control bytes**, a one-byte to a
128-byte literal run, repeats of 2 and of 128, and the `0x80` no-op. deark
stops at the declared length, macunpack reads to the stop code; **in 13
Huffman segments of 13 the symbol after the declared length is the stop
code**, so `fmt_sit.c` requires both (DECISIONS.md 2026-09-22, *the
framing that needed no rewrite*).

**Oracles: macunpack and deark** (since 2026-09-21). XADMaster names the
method and refuses it (`//case 6: fixed huffman`,
`reference/XADMaster/XADStuffItParser.m`); macunpack is the reference the
decoder is transcribed from; deark agrees on all **11** method-6 forks
(ten in `synth-sit-fixhuf.sit`, one in `derived-INITLoader.sit`). deark
credits macunpack as documentation, so they are independent code, not
independent readings (TESTING.md, *A third oracle*; BLOCKERS.md).

### StuffIt classic: per-entry method coverage ‡‡

**Over the 227 fixtures of the census** (229 since 2026-10-06 — *Two
optimized archives nobody here made*), from the manifests: LZ+Huffman
(13) 970 entries in 115 fixtures, Compress (2) 330/90, Huffman (3) 38/24,
None (0) 13/7, Fixed Huffman (6) 6/2, LZAH (5) 1/1, MW (8) 3/1. Method 1
(RLE90) has no oracle coverage but occurs — `synth-sit-rle90.sit` and one
real fork.

**The method byte's unused values**: implemented are 0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 8,
13 and 15; **4, 7, 9–12 and 14 are a per-entry error**. Nothing in reach
names them (XADMaster and deark call 14 *"Installer"* and decode neither;
macunpack's `sit.h`: *"all other numbers are reserved"*). **Every corpus
fork is one of the eight**: 227 archives, 2,512 forks, methods
0/1/2/3/5/6/8/13 only (entry-header walk, 2026-09-28).

### StuffIt 5: the Arsenic block size †

**Every real Arsenic block is 512 KB.** The size is `1 << (v+9)` with `v`
from the stream, but **all 484 Arsenic streams** of the 31-fixture corpus —
one per compressed fork — declare 512 KB (`v` = 10, blockbits 19),
measured by instrumenting where `sit_arsenic.c` reads `v`.

Peak heap is modelled by

    peak  =  ~28 KB parser state  +  block capacity  +  3 × actual block fill

Only the block is allocated at its declared size; the inverse-BWT vector
is sized to the entry's fill (`transform = numbytes * 3`, in
`ars_bwt_prepare`), so peak heap cannot recover the declared block size
(the inferred "128 KB (14) / 256 KB (9) / 512 KB (8)" figures are
withdrawn, BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*). The parser state lost
**4,572 B** on 2026-09-18 (a symbol field the nine models dropped) and
the vector went from four bytes an entry to three on 2026-09-19
(DECISIONS.md 2026-09-19). At the 1 MB budget of 648,100 B that puts
`derived-Troi-ClipSave-Plug-in1.1.sit` (531,968 B) and
`derived-AuthoRize_2.0.sit` (612,943 B) on the vector instead of the
sampled index and takes 16-20 % off the nineteen already there; the ten
wanting 1.4-2.1 MB stay on the index. Price: **+4.4 % of instructions on
the ISBN fixture and +1.9 % on `derived-a2basic.dmg.sit`** (Linux, fast
path only). **The Mac tiers were not re-run for either change**: an on-Mac
StuffIt 5 peak in this file older than 2026-09-18 is 4,572 B high, and a
fast-path one older than 2026-09-19 a further block-fill high.

Measured peak over the corpus (`macextract t --stats`, 2026-09-19): **min
0.13 MB / median 0.47 MB / mean 0.74 MB / max 2.03 MB**, the maximum
being entries that fill a 512 KB block (30 KB + 512 KB + 1536 KB);
**exactly two fixtures reach 524,257 B of fill** (that figure was once
applied to three fixtures it did not describe — BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn
claims*). These are fast-inverse-BWT peaks; under a cap the decoder takes
a sampled occurrence index (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-07) and the table below
applies.

**Under a cap: 25 of the 32 fixtures extract completely and byte-exact
under a 400 KB budget, and all 32 fit the ~700 KB a 1 MB Mac Plus has
free** (`tools/run_budget.py`, 2026-10-25). Peak under a budget is not the requirement — the decoder spends
what the cap allows — so the figure is the bisected minimum:

| fixture | needed before | 2026-08-07 | minimum now (2026-09-18) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `derived-RWAsm_Folder.sit` | 160,704 | 71,996 | 68,241 |
| `derived-Troi-ClipSave-Plug-in1.1.sit` | 660,200 | 167,455 | 163,886 |
| `derived-a2basic.dmg.sit` | 1,031,463 | 245,897 | 242,166 |
| `derived-Library_ISBN_Barcoder.sit` | 1,682,080 | 393,028 | 389,301 |
| `derived-Domino_v1.4.sit` | 2,657,700 | 565,062 | 561,441 |
| `derived-EasyFind.dmg.sit` | 2,657,700 | 565,062 | 561,441 |

The last column is the model table's 4,572 B less the sampled index's
granularity: 3,569 to 3,755 B per fixture, bisected on Linux. Before
2026-08-02 the peak was min 0.63 / median 0.97 / mean 1.23 MB with
**every** entry over a 400 KB budget; four allocation changes
(2026-08-02, -03, -06, -07, one DECISIONS.md entry each) brought it to
the floor. Per-fixture outcomes on the Mac: the Mini vMac sections below.

### Unix compress: the `.Z` peak §

**Sized from the input, not a flat 274,432 B** — measured 2026-08-03 and
2026-08-30. `compress(1)` writes `maxbits=16` whatever it compressed. The
first bound was `symbols <= 256 + reserved + insize * 8 / 9`
(DECISIONS.md 2026-08-03); the second walks the code-width schedule
(DECISIONS.md 2026-08-30, *a byte buys fewer symbols the further in it
sits*):

| fixture | in | flat | 8/9 bound | width schedule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `synth-exo-Z.Z` | 488 B | 274,432 B | 15,044 B | 15,044 B |
| `synth-exo-tar-Z.tar.Z` | 5,465 B | 274,432 B | 32,740 B | 29,373 B |
| `a2ps.V4.3.tar.Z` | 30,255 B | 274,432 B | 120,880 B | **86,080 B** |
| `popper-1.7.tar.Z` | 59,323 B | 274,432 B | 224,232 B | **147,796 B** |

The last column is the on-Mac peak: `popper-1.7.tar.Z` went from a
refusal to byte-exact on a 512 KB Macintosh (*512 KB machines*). StuffIt
classic method 2 is bounded the same way from the entry's compressed
size: 50 fixtures improved, none regressed. On-Mac figures agree with
Linux to a constant 32 B of shell overhead. Why the table is sized once
rather than grown: DECISIONS.md 2026-08-03.

### Zip: the peak's lost floor ¶

**No 66,000 B floor since 2026-08-09**: the end-of-central-directory
record is scanned backwards through a 515-byte stack window (a
66,000-byte read spans two parts of a segmented set). The peak is the
12,288 B decode buffer, plus a deflate window where there is one, plus
the central directory, read once:

| fixture | was | now |
|---|---|---|
| `4acalc.zip` | 12,288 | 12,493 |
| `4alnch11.zip` | 41,702 | 12,554 |
| `derived-MUSFMT10.ZIP` | 53,892 | 53,892 |
| `synth-exo-zip-deflate.zip` | 52,216 | 52,551 |
| `synth-exo-zip-store.zip` | 12,288 | 12,623 |

The directory costs 46 bytes plus a name per entry, unbounded in
principle, so its buffer is optional: if the allocation fails the reader
fetches one record at a time, same bytes, which `ci.sh` checks with a
budget one byte under the run's own peak (TESTING.md).

### StuffIt classic: method 13's window ‖

**Sized from the entry, not the format's 64 KB**, measured 2026-08-06: an
instrumented corpus run counted **0 back-references past produced
output** (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-06). 34 fixtures improved, none regressed;
the corpus maximum stays 76,944 B (some forks exceed 64 KB) —
`derived-MacGzip.sit` 76,944 → 13,456 B, on-Mac `derived-Export.sit`
76,984 → 19,640 B.

### uuencode: three alphabets, two of them not the standard one ✦

**UUTool uses the standard alphabet only when "use original coding" is
set.** By default it uses its `HEXA` 129 "UNIX" table, which moves low
values past `0x5f` so none encodes to a space, in two generations:

| table | where it is | values moved up | character range | `(c-0x20)&0x3f` reads it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| "PC" | `HEXA` 128, all versions | 0 | 0x20..0x5f | yes |
| "UNIX", 2.0.4 | `HEXA` 129 | 30 | 0x3e..0x7d | yes |
| "UNIX", 2.3.2 and 2.4 | `HEXA` 129 | 27 | 0x3b..0x7a | yes |
| the standard alphabet as **Unix `uuencode`** writes it — not a UUTool table | 4.2BSD's `uuencode.c` onwards, and GNU sharutils': `ENC(c) = (c) ? ((c) & 077) + ' ' : '`'` | none, but the value ZERO is `` ` `` rather than a space, so a mailer cannot eat it | 0x20..0x5f **and 0x60** | yes |

Read out of the application by `tools/derive_mu.py --tables`, which finds
both tables in UUParser 1.72 too and checks the layout literals and the
manual's section order — **21 witnesses across three versions of UUTool
and UUParser 1.72, all holding** (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-06, *the alphabet
nothing here had seen*).

**The reader admits all four spellings and narrows a document to one as
its lines are read** (`unc_uu_line_is_data` returns the mask of halves a
line fits; `src/fmt_uu_probe.c`); `0x60` belongs to both halves. The
fourth spelling is graded by `synth-uu-plain-classic.uu` since
2026-10-06, against CPython's `binascii.a2b_uu` and the payload
(BLOCKERS.md; TESTING.md, *ARC and uuencode*; DECISIONS.md 2026-10-06,
*the spelling the tables did not have*).

**The two wild specimens**, one per generation, in the differential suite
since 2026-10-06:

| fixture | shape | graded by |
|---|---|---|
| `uu-wild-dictcol.uu` | the MU layout — 5,161 B data fork, 648 B resource fork, `TEXT`/`R*ch` — 2.0.4's table, LF endings, a quoted name, mode `0755` in the first line where both sections keep the literal `0700` | `hexbin`, both forks byte for byte |
| `uu-wild-terrainman.uu` | one section, 184,340 B, 2.3.2's table, CR endings, an unquoted name (the UNIX naming preference, which replaces the characters the manual calls dangerous) | the StuffIt archive its data fork decodes to: **27 forks out, CRC-checked against the archive's own**. `hexbin` cannot read this one — its `mu()` walks to the first `"` in the begin line and there is none. The posting on that CD is cut, which the manifest asserts |

### uuencode: a document posted in several parts ★★

Joined like BinHex's, with a stronger test: a uu line's first character
declares how many bytes it encodes, so a line whose length disagrees is
stepped over. **uuencode cannot notice a missing MIDDLE part** — no length
or checksum anywhere — and decodes a short file; a missing LAST part is
caught (the terminator and `end` live there). Asserted in
`tools/run_uu.py`; OPEN_ITEMS.md.

### ARC and uuencode ★

In scope since 2026-08-15 (M13). Archives under test are generated and
graded by a second implementation — `unar` for ARC, `hexbin` for the
UUTool shape (TESTING.md) — plus the two wild uuencode specimens above.
Methods: 1/2 stored, 3 RLE90, 4 squeeze (a Huffman tree of int16LE node
pairs, then RLE90), 5/6/7 crunch (12-bit LZW, table addressed **by hash**),
8 (12-bit compress LZW + RLE90) and 9 (13-bit), the last two over the
shared `unc_lzw.c`; CRC-16 per entry. **0x0a (Crushed) and 0x0b
(Distilled), since 2026-08-25, are PAK's** (NoGate Consulting's, not
PKARC's — BLOCKERS.md). Crushed is LZW over 8,192 symbols with a
usage-count replacement policy and a code framing that switches,
unannounced, on how many of the last 500 symbols were strings; Distilled
is LZ77 with a stored prefix code, a fixed 64-entry offset code, an
offset width growing with the output, and an 8 KB window **pre-filled
with spaces**. Container and coders: `docs/formats/arc-container-and-coders.md`.

Peak heap per method: stored and RLE90 4,096 B, squeeze 5,124 B, the
compress methods 6,660–6,664 B, crunch **32,768 B** (a 4096-entry table at
6 B plus a 4 KB stack), Distilled 14,764 B, Trimmed 20,628 B, Crushed
**46,520 B**, the format's ceiling (five 8,192-entry arrays plus a
500-byte window). uuencode is a flat 4,096 B: it decodes a line at a time.

**On an emulated Mac Plus, byte-exact at 4 MB and at 1 MB** — the same
peak at both sizes, Linux plus the shell's constant 32 B:

| fixture | what | peak |
|---|---|---|
| `synth-arc-mixed.arc` (2026-08-15) | all nine methods, 9 files | 32,800 B |
| `synth-uu-mac.uu` (2026-08-15) | both forks and its type, 1 file | 4,112 B |
| `arc2pak1.pak` (2026-08-25) | 0x0a Crushed, 3 files | **46,546 B**, the format's largest |
| `derived-INTRECHO.pak` (2026-08-25, `--out-kb 1600` for its 918,631 B) | 0x0b, 10 files | **14,790 B** |
| `derived-ARCDOS.arc` (2026-08-25) | 0x0a as ARC 7 means it, 3 files, 232,668 B out | **20,642 B** |

All but `derived-INTRECHO.pak` are in `tools/vmac/sample.txt`. The
`derived-ARCDOS.arc` run grades the six entries `unar` decodes wrongly
against deark's manifest.

#### Method 0x0a is two methods

**Which one is the ARCHIVE's property** (2026-08-25). PAK calls it
Crushed; ARC 7.x ("ARC Plus", SEA) calls it **Trimmed**: LHarc's `-lh1-`
coder with a stop code, match lengths 3..59, a window of zeros rather
than `-lh1-`'s preload, and RLE90 after it. `src/unc_lzah.c` serves three
formats: StuffIt method 5, LZH `-lh1-` and this.

`arc_dialect()` decides once per archive on deark's two signals: an
informational item (0x14/0x15) or subdirectory (0x1e), which ARC 6/7
write and PAK does not; and PAK's extended records after the
end-of-archive marker (`0xfe` and a byte ≤ 4). **Trimmed only when the
first is present and the second is not**; neither means Crushed (PAK 1.0
wrote neither). A wrong answer fails the entry's CRC.

**The ARC 7 container**: every archive ARC+Plus writes opens with a 0x14
item with an empty name and uncompressed length 0, which `unar` rejects,
so **XADMaster cannot open an ARC 7 archive at all**. The items are
skipped; they carry a version string ("ARC 7.12 by SEA, Inc.") and
per-file notes.

**How the three methods are graded:**

| grader | covers | reaches |
|---|---|---|
| the CRC-16 PAK stored per member | every member of every archive, real or generated | 27 members: 6 Crushed, 21 Distilled |
| the bytes that went in | the generated corpus only | 7 archives, `tools/pak.py` |
| deark (0x0b) and `unar` (0x0a) | byte-for-byte, independently implemented | 13 members of the two real specimens, all 7 generated archives |
| PAK 2.51 itself, extracting under DOSBox | the period-accurate case: the writer reads it back | the 9 members of the PAK distribution, byte-identical |
| a plain copy of the same file from a different CD | the case with no decoder in it at all | `XARC.EXE`, 21,174 B, Trimmed in ARC+Plus's own distribution and uncompressed in `swsii/072/XARC.ZIP` — byte-identical |

`unar` is wrong about 0x0b and cannot open Trimmed's container; deark
does not implement Crushed (BLOCKERS.md). The real specimens,
`arc2pak1.pak` (3 members, 0x0a, 1988) and `derived-INTRECHO.pak` (10
members, 0x0b, six adjudicated by deark), are in the differential suite.

**What only the generated corpus reaches**: neither real archive fills
Crushed's 8,192-symbol table; `pak-crushed-big.pak` (300 KB, one member)
fires the replacement scan **49,751 times** and the same-length-code mode
**13,009 times**. Distilled matches into the space-filled window — what
separates a correct decoder from XADMaster's — occur 10 times in
`derived-INTRECHO.pak` and twice in `pak-distilled-text.pak`.

### ArcMac ★★★

**ArcMac** — `0x1b`, then the ordinary ARC header 59 bytes in — **extracts
both forks since 2026-08-16**. One stream holds them, **data fork first**,
CRC over the concatenation; the two fork lengths are **little-endian**
(ARCMAC.DOC, *ArcMac File Structure*; both real archives confirm). Neither
fact is in `arc.h`, and the order was measured by having ArcMac 1.3c
archive a two-fork file under Mini vMac (`tools/vmac/arcmac.py`;
DECISIONS.md). Wild archives read end to end: `ARCMACRC.BIN` (6 entries,
methods 8 and 9, 1988) and `ArcPop.Arc`. `Packed.ARC` (method 8, 4,230 +
333) extracts **byte-exact on an emulated Mac Plus at 4 MB**, **8,000 B
peak**, both fork CRCs equal to what went into ArcMac. `unar` cannot
recognise the format, so the encoder is the oracle.

### AppleLink packages ★★★★

In scope since 2026-10-05 (M56); everything — directories, folder levels,
stored bits, codec, grading — is *AppleLink packages: SqueezePlay*,
below. Every path the format has is reached by a real package since
2026-10-06.

Peak heap **8,576 B** on the one package whose only fork is stored, and
**41,344 B at the ceiling** (41,360 B on a Mac Plus, the shell's 16 B
more; *Run on a Macintosh*, below). The ceiling is the format's: the code
width stops at 13 bits, so the table cannot pass 8,192 symbols × 4 B;
three real packages and `synth-sqp-clear.pkg` fill it. Nothing allocates
per entry, so the 31-member package peaks lower than a one-member one
whose table fills.

## StuffIt SpaceSaver

In scope since 2026-08-22, the layout read out of Aladdin's own engine
before any specimen was found (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-22, *SpaceSaver, the
layout*; *SpaceSaver, the codecs*). `tools/vmac/spacesaver.py --layout`
prints every figure below out of the installed software's bytes, with
nine witnesses that fail on a different build.

The writer is `SSEn 1` in `StuffIt Engine™`; the control panel's `ACMP 1`
only reads eight bytes and compares the two magics. A compressed file has
type `SPSV`, creator `SIT!`, an EMPTY resource fork, and a data fork
rounded up to a multiple of 256 (the extension's screen before it opens
anything).

| off | size | field |
|---|---|---|
| +0 | 4 | `'SIT!'` |
| +4 | 4 | `'DFD3'` |
| +8 | 4 | version, 3; 1 and 2 are converted in place on read |
| +12 | 4 | header length, 84 |
| +16 | 4 | flags; `0x00000041` in everything this engine writes |
| +20 | 16 | fork record — data fork |
| +36 | 16 | fork record — resource fork |
| +52 | 4 | the file's `fdType` before compression |
| +56 | 4 | its `fdCreator` |
| +60 | 2 | its `fdFlags`; six bits are cleared on compress and restored from here |
| +62 | 2 | zero |
| +64 | 20 | reserved, zeroed on every write |

| off | size | fork record field |
|---|---|---|
| +0 | 4 | compressed length |
| +4 | 4 | original length; 0 = fork absent, and the two bytes below are 0 |
| +8 | 4 | offset of the stream from the start of the data fork |
| +12 | 1 | parameter set — indexes the engine's `PrAm` resources; always 0 here |
| +13 | 1 | compressor index, bit 7 = the PowerPC module |
| +14 | 2 | zero |

Record 1 is always the data fork and record 2 the resource fork, but the
LARGER fork's stream is written first, at offset 84: read each at its own
offset.

| idx | modules (`CMod`) | runs on | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 1 "Default Compressor" | any | stored, implemented |
| 1 | 3 "Original Fast Compressor" | any | **implemented** — LZSS, 12-bit distance |
| 2 | 4 "Original Slow Compressor" | any | **implemented** — a 4,096-entry position table |
| 3 | 10 "Alg 3", 11 decompress-only, 15 PowerPC | 68030 and up / any / PowerPC | **implemented** — Huffman + LZ77 |
| 4 | 20 "Alg 4", 21 decompress-only | 68000-68020 / any | implemented |

Any other compressor byte fails the entry, not the file. The index is the
module's own byte at +0x20 (low byte a revision; the higher wins); +0x24
is a CPU mask, bit 31 − Gestalt `cput`. **The default is the CPU's**:
index 4 below a 68030, 3 from a 68030 up; the decompress-only modules
accept every CPU. **The 142 real files found on 2026-09-26 bear that
out**: version-3 headers carry method 0, 3 or 4 only, and the two method-4
files are the only two from a hard-disk backup rather than a pressed
CD-ROM; version-2 headers carry 1 and 2. Methods 1, 2 and 3 were
implemented on 2026-08-25, graded by `tools/ssmodule/` running Aladdin's
own compressors and expanders under `qemu-m68k` (71 round trips in 3
seconds; 44 forks read back byte-exact; TESTING.md).

**Methods 1 and 2 share a framing and nothing else.** Both read an 8-byte
block header — a "stored" byte and a 32-bit big-endian payload length —
until the fork's declared length is produced; both spend 16 flag bits at a
time, LSB first, and encode a match in two bytes: `(B1 & 0x0F) + 3` long,
`B1`'s high nibble and `B2` making twelve more bits, which mean:

| | method 1, `CMod 3` | method 2, `CMod 4` |
|---|---|---|
| the twelve bits | a distance | an index into a 4,096-entry table of positions |
| the table | — | 512 buckets of 8, filled by a 3-byte hash of the output, one rotating slot shared by the whole table |
| an entry never filled | — | copies `CMod 4`'s own last 18 bytes, a copyright string, and real streams DO reach it |
| the flag word | a counter, 16 items | a sentinel bit above the word, tested once per item |
| window | 4 KB is enough (12-bit distance) | 64 KB: the table's positions are anywhere in the block |
| peak heap | 4 KB + 4 KB I/O | 64 KB + 16 KB table + 4 KB I/O |

**Method 3 is a Huffman LZ77, deflate-like without being deflate.** The
framing is method 4's — a 16-bit block count, a width byte, blocks of a
6-byte header — and inside each block:

| | |
|---|---|
| alphabets | 308 literal/length symbols and `4 * wbits + 3` distances, both LSB-first |
| a match | `4 + length`, from base tables the module's init COMPUTES: length extra `i<4 ? 0 : (i-4)>>2` from 0, distance extra `i<3 ? 0 : (i-3)>>2` from 1 |
| the code lengths | a **recursive meta-coder**: a control byte gives a 2–5 bit raw width, an added constant, an escape meaning "length 0", and a bit saying the symbols are themselves Huffman-coded — by the same routine on its own alphabet. The top symbol repeats the previous length `3 + next` times |
| the code assignment | canonical, bit-reversed, **in the order a non-stable quicksort leaves the lengths in** (`CMod 11` @0x15a) — so a decoder has to reproduce that sort, which this one does iteratively because the engine forbids recursion |
| peak heap | 32 KB window + ~5 KB of tables + 4 KB I/O = **41 KB** on a 120 KB fork |

On that fork the five methods cost **4 KB, 8 KB, 84 KB, 41 KB and 36 KB**
and compress it to 120,000 / 81,140 / 67,412 / 48,636 / 66,936 bytes —
Alg 3 is the best, which is why a 68030 selects it. Methods 1 and 2 never
read the parameter block past +52 (measured); method 3 needs the preset
dictionary as method 4 does (its module faults on a null pointer).

**Method 4 is an LZSS with a preset dictionary.** Three bytes of stream
header (block count, 16-bit little-endian, then the window width in bits),
then blocks of a 6-byte header — compressed length including those six
bytes, then uncompressed length, both 24-bit little-endian — and an
LSB-first bit stream: `1` and eight bits is a literal, `0` and five bits
of length plus *wbits* of distance a copy of 4 + length bytes. The window
slides across the whole fork. **Every field is confirmed by Aladdin's
ENCODER too** (`CMod 20`; DECISIONS.md 2026-08-23, *the encoder agrees*):
it always writes width **15** (a 32,768-byte window) and a block covers
**65,536 bytes of output**; the decoder reads the width byte rather than
assuming either.

**It starts loaded with 9,679 bytes of Aladdin's `PrAm` -4048**, placed
before the first output byte. **Those bytes ship**, in `src/ss_dict.h`
(Aladdin's; `NOTICE`; DECISIONS.md 2026-08-23); without exactly them a
file decodes to the right length and silently wrong bytes. The record's
byte at +12 chooses: 0 is -4048, 1 is `PrAm` -4047 (four spaces); both
built in, anything else refused per entry. `macextract --preset FILE`
overrides it. The resource is byte-identical in the control panel and
`StuffIt Engine™`, either a source for `tools/gen_ss_dict.py`.

**Every method runs on a 68000**, byte-identical to the Linux CLI, **at
4 MB and at the 1 MB floor** (System 6.0.8, `tools/vmac/run.py --expect`):

| file | written by | out | peak on the Mac |
|---|---|---|---|
| stored | `tools/run_spacesaver.py --emit` | 1,040 B data + 512 B resource | **4,112 B** |
| method 4 at SpaceSaver's width | the same | 14,524 B | **36,896 B** (32 KB window + 4 KB input) |
| method 4 reaching into the dictionary | the same | 7,000 B | **36,896 B** |
| method 1 "Original Fast" | `tools/ssmodule/run_ssmodule.py --keep` | 76,800 B | **8,224 B** (4 KB window + input buffer) |
| method 2 "Original Slow" | the same | 76,800 B | **86,064 B** (64 KB window, 16 KB table, input buffer) |
| method 3 "Alg 3" | the same | 76,800 B | **42,112 B** (32 KB window, ~5 KB tables, input buffer) |

The first three re-measured 2026-08-23, the last three 2026-08-25. A
SpaceSaver file that has travelled alone needs nothing else — no
SpaceSaver, no flag — on Linux or the Macintosh application.

## LZH / LHA

✵ In scope since 2026-08-23 (M28). The Macintosh corpus contains no
`.lzh` (measured; DECISIONS.md 2026-08-14); the specimens are
`reference/lhasa`'s **182 archives written by fourteen LHA
implementations** — MacLHA 2.24, LHa for Amiga/OS-2/OS-9/OSK/X68000,
LHarc 1.13, LArc 3.33, LHMelt, LHArk, UNLHA32 — and `reference/lha`,
LHa for UNIX 1.14i, compiled here as an ENCODER. Header walk:
`docs/formats/lzh-header.md`.

**Three graders, not interchangeable** (TESTING.md), and since 2026-09-19
which is independent depends on the method: `-lh4-`..`-lh7-` and LArc's
`-lz5-`/`-lzs-` are written FROM lhasa, so `unar` is the independent one;
the header walk, `-lh1-` and `-lh2-`/`-lh3-` follow `XADLZHParser.m`, so
lhasa is. LHa 1.14i is independent of both and writes archives of known
plain text. 137 archives are graded against `unar`, 142 against lhasa, 12
generated sets against their own input.

| what | `unar` | lhasa | settled by |
|---|---|---|---|
| the 18 bytes of an -lz5- window the preload does not cover | zeros | (rotated: zeros) | LArc 3.33's own `initial.lzs`, an archive whose CONTENTS are the initial window — zeros. LHa 1.14i's `decode()` space-fills a dictionary its `decode_start_lz5` never touches |
| an -lzs- window before anything is written | zeros | spaces | the stored CRC: **spaces**. Zero-filling fails `lzs.lzs` and passes `long.lzs`, which is what a difference that only shows on some files looks like |
| LHa for OS-9/68k's level 2 header, two bytes short | fails all three of its archives | compensates by OS byte | lhasa, followed here |
| a 1.2 MB -lh7- file | "Attempted to read more data than was available" | decodes | lhasa, byte-identical to this engine |
| LHArk 0.4d's "-lh7-", which is a different algorithm | fails | decodes it as `-lk7-` | refused per entry here: LHArk marks itself with a space as the OS byte |
| `-lzs-` at all | **cannot open one** — its recogniser omits `-lzs-` while its own parser has a decoder for it | reads it | this probe accepts `-lzs-`; the divergence from the oracle is deliberate and measured |

**MacLHA compresses a MacBinary stream**: a Macintosh entry decodes to a
128-byte header, the data fork padded to 128, then the resource fork, and
name, type, creator and dates are the Mac header's. The unwrap is for OS
byte `'m'` only; a level 0 header has no OS byte, and `unar` reads
MacLHA's level 0 archives as one 18,304-byte file where the level 1 copy
is 18,092 plus metadata — both readings reproduced here. lhasa uses the
same trigger and discards the resource fork; this engine keeps it.

**MacLHA 2.00 needed one more rule than either reference has.**
`tools/vmac/maclha.py` ran MacLHA 2.00 under Mini vMac on a two-fork file
(none of lhasa's sixteen MacLHA archives has a resource fork): its
MacBinary header has **both dates zero**, which XADMaster's MacBinary-I
heuristic and lhasa both refuse, handing back the whole 2,304-byte
stream. This engine accepts on arithmetic: the Mac header's name must be
the entry's, and the two fork lengths padded to 128 plus the header must
equal the uncompressed size. Both forks then come back byte-exact (1,760 B
and 333 B, `tools/run_lzh.py`); all 182 archives and 8 fixtures are
unchanged.

**Dates are not converted** (MS-DOS at levels 0 and 1, Unix at 2 and 3;
reported as none, as zip's reader does); a MacBinary payload's dates are
used. **Symbolic links** (`link|target`) are named and refused per entry,
as tar's are.

**Peak heap 4–74 KB** (`macextract t --stats`): 4 KB stored, 8 KB LArc,
16–18 KB -lh5- and -lh1-, 41 KB -lh6-, and **74,128 B** for a -lh7- entry
needing the whole 64 KB window (`lh7_long.lzh`, 1.2 MB). The window is
sized from the entry, as StuffIt method 13's is (the corpus's -lh7-
fixtures cost 41 KB), and is half what lhasa allocates, whose ring doubles
as its output buffer.

**`-lh2-` since 2026-08-25, with this project's encoder.** Both codes
adaptive — `-lh1-`'s literal/length tree plus a position tree that GROWS
with the output, one leaf per 64 bytes. Transcribed from
`XADLZHOldHandles.m` (`LHAdecode_start_dyn`, `_c_dyn`, `_p_dyn`),
cross-read against LHa's `dhuf.c`, in `src/unc_lzdyn.c`. Nothing writes
one, so `tools/build_lha_lh2.sh` teaches the maintained LHa fork to, and
its own decoder verifies each archive first: **15 `-lh2-` entries across
three header levels in `ci.sh`**, the run failing if fewer than three
entries of an archive used the method (TESTING.md).

**`-lh3-` since 2026-08-25**: STATIC, rebuilt per block, so
`tools/run_lzh.py` writes the streams and LHa's decoder vets each. A block
is a 16-bit symbol count, a literal/length tree as one 4-bit length per
symbol, then a position tree in the same shape or the format's fixed one;
both escapes (a single-symbol block, an explicit position tree) are
exercised. Six entries per run.

PMarc (`-pm0-`/`-pm1-`/`-pm2-`), `-lhx-` and the self-extracting archives
are out of scope (OPEN_ITEMS.md, part 1).

**On the Mac** (2026-08-23), five `.lzh` fixtures byte-exact on System
6.0.8 at 4 MB, at the **1 MB floor** and on System 7.0.1: peak 4,112 B for
the smallest, 41,364 B for the -lh7- one, 16,788 B for the five-entry
Amiga archive — Linux plus the shell's constant 32 B.

## btoa, the other ASCII armour

In scope since 2026-08-23: the fourteenth entry of StuffIt Deluxe 3.5.1's
Translate menu (*The paid box*, below), inside the brief "everything
StuffIt Expander 4.0.2 expands" (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-15). Paul Rutter's
btoa: four bytes into five base-85 characters, 25% overhead against
uuencode's 33%.

◆ **Two framings.** `xbtoa5` lines carry a checksum character and the
three whole-stream checksums cover the ENCODED characters; the older
`xbtoa Begin` has no per-line check and its checksums cover the DECODED
bytes. Both are read. `z` is four zero bytes and, in version 5 only, `y`
four spaces. The declared length ends the file: `(len - 1) & 3` says how
many bytes of the last five-character group are real.

**CR, LF and CRLF all end a line** (a file saved on a Mac is
CR-terminated; CRLF's LF reads as an allowed blank line). Skipping CR, as
the code did until 2026-08-23, made such a file one unmatched line
(BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*).

**No graded corpus file, and no second reader.** Re-measured 2026-10-11
over all 983 corpus files: one sniffs as btoa, `synth-os9-btoa.btoa`,
written for the Mac OS 9 tier with no manifest. `unar` and macutils do not
read the format, so the grader is the original: `tools/fetch_references.sh`
fetches btoa 5.2.1 (Paul Rutter, Joe Orost and Stefan Parmark, 1996, from
`archive.debian.org`), `src/fmt_btoa.c` is transcribed from its `atob.c`,
and `tools/run_btoa.py` compiles it as an ENCODER and requires our decoder
to reproduce its output — eight subjects across both framings, a
mail-wrapped one, a flipped character, a truncated archive and prose
containing the word.

The `R` checksum is a 32-bit rotate the reference computes in a `long`;
the low 32 bits are compared. **Both widths measured**: the Macintosh
specimen below ends `R fcd84997` where this machine's reference writes
`R 273efff1fcd84997` for the same payload.

**A stream written by Aladdin, 2026-08-26.** `tools/vmac/sitcrypt.py
--btoa-send` has StuffIt Deluxe 3.5.1's `btoa/atob Translator` encode a
subject on an emulated Mac Plus (`tools/vmac/README.md`):

- it writes the **older `xbtoa Begin` framing** (the Unix reference's
  `-o`);
- it **MacBinary-wraps first**: `Subject` becomes `Subject.MBin.ascii`,
  640 bytes of MacBinary in 732 bytes of armour, and comes back
  byte-exact through both layers in one pass;
- against btoa 5.2.1 `-o` on the same payload, the streams are
  **identical but for the width of `R`** — twelve lines, one difference.

## base64 and MIME, the armour that replaced the others

In scope since 2026-08-26 (M36), on request (`.b64` and `.mime` are in
the Macintosh Garden forum thread *Archive formats*'s list of what a
downloader meets). Three shapes:

- **A bare base64 body**, the whole file, no marker: four or more full
  lines of one width between 40 and 76 characters, every character in
  the alphabet, a last line that closes the stream; the LAST probe
  `unc_sniff` runs. ◇ **A file too short for that shape is not
  recognised**, which `tools/run_mime.py` asserts.
- **`begin-base64 <mode> <name>` … `====`** (sharutils' `base64` and
  `uuencode -m`), several documents in one file read.
- **A MIME message**: `multipart/*` to four levels, `base64`,
  `quoted-printable` and unencoded parts, the filename from
  `Content-Disposition` then `Content-Type`. A `text/…` part with no
  filename is the sender's note and is not written out (munpack's rule).

**A file pasted into a part comes out** (since 2026-08-29): a `text/…`
part with no filename that is a uuencoded document is decoded and named
from its `begin` line — mpack's `handleUuencode()` test at every depth;
`Content-Transfer-Encoding: x-uuencode` changes nothing. A BinHex or btoa
document there is written out undecoded. `unc_sniff` claims a message
that declares a multipart with a boundary before the uuencode, btoa and
BinHex probes; a posting with no boundary still belongs to those
(DECISIONS.md 2026-08-29, *a part that hid its message*).

**multipart/appledouble (RFC 1740)**: the `application/applefile` part
carries the resource fork, real name and Finder info, and the pair comes
out as ONE file with both forks, type and creator; with no data part it
is a resource-only file and extracts as one.

**No recursion**: an explicit stack of four boundaries, the whole 2 KB
walker in the same heap block as its buffers. The deepest chain is under
1 KB (`unc_mime_extract` → `mime_emit` → `mime_dec_run` →
`mime_dec_next`) against a 4 KB budget; the probe's frame is 904 B, a
leaf.

◇ **Peak heap is flat: 5,097 B for base64 and 8,322 B for MIME, on a 4 MB
message as on a 40-byte one** (`macextract t --stats`): an I/O window, a
line, a header and the 512-byte prefix of an applefile part, nothing
sized from a field.

**On the Macintosh, 2026-08-26**: a two-fork multipart/appledouble
message extracts on an emulated Mac Plus (System 6.0.8) at 4 MB **and at
the 1 MB floor**, identical to the Linux CLI on every field of the grid —
one file, data CRC 0xB0C0DF2A over 768 B, resource CRC 0x76D73227 over
340 B — peak **8,288 B** against 8,322 B on Linux (four-byte pointers in
the walker). With no oracle manifest `run.py` reports FAIL and the grid
is the result (`tools/vmac/README.md`).

**No graded corpus file.** Re-measured 2026-10-07 over all 966 corpus
files: one sniffs as MIME and none as base64 — `synth-os9-mime.mim`, for
the Mac OS 9 tier, no manifest. Every case is generated and graded by
munpack 1.6 and deark (TESTING.md, *base64 and MIME*).

**A message a Macintosh wrote — 2026-08-29, 18/18.** mpack's Macintosh
port (`reference/mpack`): `encode_applefile()` and `encode()` compiled
unmodified for a 68000 and run on System 6.0.8 wrote a 1,912-byte
multipart/appledouble from an HFS file with a 407-byte resource fork
(`tools/vmac/macmime.py`). Magic `00051607`, version `00020000`, five
entries in macfile.c's order (rsrc, name, comment, dates, Finder info),
name `Subject`, Finder info 32 bytes of `TEXTttxt` + flags `0x0100`, the
resource entry equal to the fork; `macextract` returns both forks
byte-exact and munpack reads the same file.

**`message/partial`**, a message split across several files, is under
*Segmented archives* (the host opens the parts, the joiner sequences
them).

◇ **Line endings are not settled by that specimen**: it is LF-terminated
because Retro68's newlib does no `\n`→CR translation. `tools/run_mime.py`
grades CR, CRLF and LF separately.

## StuffIt 2.0's pluggable modules, and what an "Optimizer" was

**Aladdin's Read Mes warn about archives nothing since can open** —
Expander 4.0.2: *"StuffIt Expander will not expand StuffIt archives
created using 2.0 Optimizers. You will need either the StuffIt Deluxe or
the StuffIt Lite application, and the 2.0 optimizer used to compress the
archive."* (3.5.1 through 4.5 repeat the first sentence). The modules are
in hand since 2026-09-28, off StuffIt Deluxe 2.0.1's two floppies
(`stuffit-deluxe-v2.sit`, md5 `45b940a528fe2bc18560bf0b2ae60f7a` —
DiskCopy images whose 84-byte header comes off, as
`spacesaver.setup351` does) and `deluxe-2-optional.sit`.

**An Optimizer is a per-file-type preprocessor, not a compressor**
(Aladdin's `Deluxe 2.0 Addendum`, disk 2): `Text 1` *"alters or disposes
of this unneeded data without removing essential text"* (leading spaces
to tabs, trailing whitespace stripped, resources deleted); `MacPaint 1`
*"works on a file by removing data that is not required ... will never
remove artwork"*; both by Robert Gibson.

**Five ship, numbered**: the 2.0.1 installer's `Optimizers` folder holds
`Sound 1`, `Photoshop 1`, `Photoshop:JPEG Lossy`, `MacPaint 1` and
`Text 1` (`SITe`/`SIT!`), each with a **`COMP`** resource (stuffing) and,
except `Text 1`, a **`DCMP`** (unstuffing), at IDs **1, 2, 3, 4 and 5**.
**Encryptors use the same mechanism**: `PassLock` is `COMP`/`DCMP`
**514**; `deluxe-2-optional.sit` adds `NewDEx` **515** and `DESex`
**576**.

**The resource ID is `(class << 8) | id`**: class **0** Optimizer
(1..255), **1** Compressor (256..511), **2** Encryptor (512..767) — the
2.0.1 module manager walks installed modules with `GetResInfo` and
matches `class << 8 | id`.

### Where an archive names the module — read 2026-09-28, six bytes

Aladdin's Get Info pane hands one fork's method byte and three id bytes
to the module manager, once per fork; the same call takes the lengths
from 84/92 and 88/96, which pins the header base:

| header | is | resource ID of the module |
|---|---|---|
| method byte bit **`0x40`** | an Optimizer ran on this fork | — |
| **106** / **108** | Optimizer id, data / resource fork | `id` |
| method nibble **7** | the fork used an external Compressor | — |
| **46** / **47** | Compressor id, data / resource fork | `256 + id` |
| method byte bit **`0x80`** | encrypted (already known) | — |
| **107** / **109** | Encryptor id, data / resource fork | `512 + id` |

`XADStuffItParser.m` names 46/47 `SITFH_DATACHAR`/`SITFH_RSRCCHAR` and
106..109 `DATAUNK1`/`DATAUNK2`/`RSRCUNK1`/`RSRCUNK2`, with *"always 4 for
encrypted?"* — **4 is the built-in scheme that replaced the modules**:
three archives StuffIt Deluxe 3.5.1 wrote here carry `07 03 00 04 00 04`
at 104..109. 2.0.1's Get Info (STR# 261) names encryptor id 1 **DES**,
built into 2.0.1, not a module (BLOCKERS.md, withdrawn 2026-10-02).

**"Replaced", measured on 3.5.1** (2026-10-10, `tools/vmac/sitenc351.py`),
with 2.0's three encryptors installed in both module folders:

| asked for | 3.5.1 wrote / said |
|---|---|
| script `with better des`, `… newde`, `… encrypt` | `8d`/`8d`, **4 / 4** at 107/109, all three decrypting with the one password |
| `Set encryptor "NewDEx"`, then `… encrypt` | *"< Optimizers, compressors and encryptors: No longer supported. >"* (STR# 256), then 4 / 4 |
| `Enable Optimizer "Text 1"` | the same message |
| `… encryptor "NewDEx"` / `"DESex"` / `"PassLock"` | *"No archive: NewDEx"* — read as an archive name; nothing stuffed |
| Preferences, *Default Stuffing Settings* | two checkboxes, Compression and Encryption; no cipher list |
| the Stuff dialog | *Encrypt with password*, a checkbox |

The `stuff` Apple event has only a boolean `encryption` (`aete`), and
3.5.1's module folders (STR# 267) no longer include `Compressors` and
`Encryptors`. So no 3.5.1 route writes an id other than 4 beside method
13; `sit_enc_id` ignores 107/109 on a method-13 fork, and
`tools/run_crypt.py` asserts the 4. **Not measured**: 3.0.x, 3.5.0,
StuffIt Lite 3.x and the 4.x line.

**2.0.1's method names** (STR# 261): 0 no compression, 1 RLE, 2 LZW, 3 and
**9** Huffman, 5 LZ-H, 6 FHF, **7 an external Compressor module**, 8 LZ-M,
**10 PM1100**; 4 and 11 up are *"unknown method"* (where 13 and 15 fall).

**The general corpus uses none of it**: over the **2,512 forks of 1,256
file entries in 227 StuffIt archives**, bits `0x40` and `0x80` never set,
nibble 7 never used, method bytes 0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 8 and 13 only. Bytes
46/47 are nonzero in **91** entries and mean nothing (1.x wrote buffer
contents), so the reading is anchored to the flag and nibble; the five
earlier `104..109` "exceptions" are two **version-1** archives with stale
bytes. (Optimized wild archives found since: *Two optimized archives
nobody here made*, *Two more*.)

**InstallerMaker 1.0.3 writes nonzero bytes here that are not module ids**
(measured 2026-10-10). Aladdin's *Shortcut 1.5.3 Update* (Info-Mac
1994-01, Eagle's Nest disc 1, HyperLib 1994-1 CD2) is an `APPL`/`STi0`
whose data fork is a version-2 archive with an `MKey`: all 13 forks
`0x8d`, method 13, 11 with 0 at 106..109. `Shortcut™` carries **5 / 3**
at **106 / 109** and `Viewer Engine™` **6 / 5** (corrected 2026-10-10);
both have an empty data fork, so only 109 is read. With the installer's
password `PeekAB00` (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-10, *the installer's own key*)
**both decrypt under the built-in scheme**, byte for byte what the
installer wrote to an emulated disk (standard resource-header mask):

| | 109 | the engine before | with 109 ignored on method 13 |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Shortcut™` | 3 | *archive structure is corrupt* (`NewDEx`) | 276,998 B, CRC-16 good, = Aladdin's |
| `Viewer Engine™` | 5 | *encrypted by a scheme we cannot read* | 78,014 B, CRC-16 good, = Aladdin's |

Hence `sit_scheme()` does not read 107/109 on a method-13 fork
(`sit_enc_id`, `src/fmt_sit.c`); `tools/run_crypt.py` grades the archive
against the installer's output.

**The thirteenth fork, `Read Us First!/Shortcut 1.5 Addendum`, is broken
in its compressed stream, and no decoder is at fault** (measured
2026-10-10). That it was DAMAGED rather than written bad in 1994 is a
HYPOTHESIS: the copies agree, so nothing here tells the two apart.

| | |
|---|---|
| three decoders | this engine, `unar` and Aladdin's installer agree on the first 210,162 B — the declared length — except the reserved header bytes 74..125, and all three fail the stored CRC-16 `634e` (ours computes `6ff1`); the installer writes **238,917 B** in all, carrying on past the length, where this engine stops with an error after 19,431 B more (measured off the installer's own copy, 2026-10-10) |
| where it breaks | the resource data chains through 160 records to output offset **159,173**, mid-text (*"…kept in this file. Extensions"*), then turns to LZ-shaped noise; the bytes the header calls the map at 206,785 are noise too |
| the stream | every other fork in the archive emits the end symbol `0x140` exactly at its declared length; this one has used 67,650 of its 74,056 compressed bytes there (the fork is 74,072 B with its 16-byte encryption trailer) and decodes **19,431 B** more, with no end symbol, into an error. The desync is near compressed byte 55,208, data-fork offset ~288,770 |
| the copies | Info-Mac 1994-01 and HyperLib 1994-1 CD2 carry the same data fork, byte for byte (SHA-1 `a7cc9ccc…`), so the damage is upstream of both pressings |
| one bit? | **no**: flipping each of the fork's 592,576 bits passes the CRC six times — about what a 16-bit check predicts, ~8 once the flips that cannot reach it are excluded (past byte 67,650 or failing the decode) — and all six are collisions — none ends at `0x140` or yields a map that parses (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-10, *the six repairs that were a 16-bit check*) |

### What Aladdin's own output says — measured 2026-09-28

StuffIt Deluxe 2.0.1, driven by `tools/vmac/sitopt.py`, stuffed one text
subject with no Optimizer and twice with `Text 1` at two methods; the
optimized entries differ from the control only in these bytes, lengths
and CRCs:

| | control | `Text 1` |
|---|---|---|
| method byte, data fork | `02` | **`42`** — `0x40` set |
| method byte, rsrc fork | `02` | **`40`** — `0x40` set, method 0 |
| **106** / **108** | 0 / 0 | **255 / 255** |
| 46, 47, 107, 109 | 0 | 0 |
| rsrc length | 338 | **0** — `Text 1` deleted it |

**255 means "nothing can undo this"**, not the module's id: `Text 1`
(module 5, no `DCMP`) writes it, Get Info special-cases it, and 2.0.1's
unstuffer hands back the optimized bytes (594, no resource fork). Every
module with a `DCMP` writes its own id:

| Optimizer | `DCMP` | byte 106 | method byte | what it did to the fork |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Sound 1` | id 1 | **1** | `42` | 2,528 B → 146 |
| `Photoshop 1` | id 2 | **2** | `42` | 10,495 B → 1,582 |
| `Photoshop/JPEG Lossy` | id 3 | **3** | `40` | 10,495 B → 80 |
| `MacPaint 1` | id 4 | **4** | `62` | 925 B → 477 |
| `Text 1` | **none** | **255** | `42`, `40`, `45` | the sentinel |

`JPEG Lossy` is lossy and Get Info prints 255 as *" lossy"*, but it writes
**3**. **Aladdin's own Engine draws the same line**: Magic Menu unstuffed
the `Text 1` archive and refused the `MacPaint 1` one (*"Items that were
encrypted or compressed with an optimizer or compressor will be
skipped…"*). **Bit `0x20` is unexplained**: set on both `MacPaint 1` forks
and on no other module's; nothing here depends on it. **Byte 106 means
nothing without `0x40`**: an archive stuffed while an Optimizer was merely
enabled carries its id with the flag clear and the fork untouched
(measured). `tools/run_sitopt.py` grades all eight archives, the optimized
ones against 2.0.1's own unstuffed bytes.

**An optimized entry this engine cannot undo is refused per entry and
named** (since 2026-09-28): `UNC_ERR_MODULE`, with
`UncEntryInfo.module_kind`/`module_id` set in list mode too, and the CLI
turning the pair into Aladdin's name (host-side, `cli/main.c`,
`module_name`, so no table is A5-resident on the 68000). **A correctness
fix**: an Optimizer runs BEFORE the compressor, so the method decodes and
the header's CRC agrees on the preprocessed bytes — what **`unar` 1.10.8
still writes**: given a synthetic archive with optimized entries of ids
5, 4 and 200, it writes all three preprocessed and reports success,
declining only the method-7 entry (measured 2026-09-28; TESTING.md, *When
our engine declines an entry*). 1.5.1-format archives cannot use modules
(the Addendum), and by 3.x they are gone (3.5.1: *"No optimizers are
installed"*).

**What each module accepts, read out of its own `COMP`**: `Sound 1` type
**`FSSD`** (Sound Designer II) and an `INFO` 1000 resource (it declined a
corpus `sfil` suitcase, correctly); `Photoshop 1` type **`8BIM`**
(Photoshop 1.0's, not `8BPS`) and an `8BIM` 1000 resource of ten bytes or
more whose word at +8 is colour mode 1 or 3..7; `MacPaint 1` `PNTG`. The
early Photoshop subject was part synthetic (real PSD data fork, resource
fork built to the test).

### The module ABI, read 2026-09-28, driven end to end 2026-09-29

From the application's CODE 8 (the helper at +0x882 locks the handle,
dereferences it and jumps to the module's first byte, a `bra.s` over the
16-byte header):

```
pascal void Module(Ptr buffer, long *selector, void *third);
```

Callee-pops, twelve bytes of arguments. `*selector` is the request, the
answer, and what says how to read the other two:

| `*selector` | `buffer` | `third` | the module |
|---|---|---|---|
| **-1** | a `CInfoPBRec` | a `{ long; long; }` | *will you take this file?* — writes 0 or 1 back, and keeps the file's name, volume and directory out of the record |
| **-2** | ignored | the same record | **finishes**: a `DCMP` rebuilds the file here, then closes and deletes its temp file; writes 1 back |
| **-3** | 256 bytes to fill | ignored | copies its own name in with `_BlockMove`, writes 1 back |
| **-4** | ignored | the same record | `COMP` only: runs the transform, and reports the length it produced in the record's SECOND long |
| **-5** | ignored | NULL | puts up its About alert |
| **-6** | NULL | NULL | `COMP` only: rewinds its output |
| **> 0** | that many bytes | **a `ProcPtr`** | processes them — a `COMP` FILLS the buffer and answers with the count, a `DCMP` consumes it and answers with an `OSErr` |

(-2 was read as "its kind" until 2026-09-29; BLOCKERS.md, withdrawn
claims.) At the setup selectors `third` is a `{ long; long; }` the
application fills — the LOW WORD of the first is a flag, zero making the
module accept and do nothing — and the module answers in it. At
`*selector > 0` it is a jump-table entry a `COMP` calls with the buffer
and the NEGATED count; a `DCMP` writes the file and never calls it.

**The record at -1 is a `CInfoPBRec`** (the application's `PBGetCatInfo`
result): `ioNamePtr` at **+18**, `ioVRefNum` **+22**,
`ioFlFndrInfo.fdType` **+32** (the acceptance test's field), `ioDirID`
**+48** (the `DCMP`'s parent directory) and `ioFlParID` **+100** (the
`COMP`'s) — only this record puts a directory ID at 48 AND at 100. Each
module carries its own id at +8 of the resource, after the
`'COMP'`/`'DCMP'` tag at +4. A `DCMP` tests nothing: the archive has
already named it.

**A module's globals live inside its own resource** (THINK C, A4 = the
resource's base): `MacPaint 1`'s `DCMP` keeps its param blocks, temp file
name and output position in the zero-filled tail of its 3,010 bytes, so
the handle stays locked between calls. **A `DCMP` does its own File
Manager I/O** — `_Read`, `_Write`, `_GetEOF`, `_SetFPos`,
`_HOpen`/`_HCreate`/`_HDelete` ($0200 variants), `PBGetFCBInfo` — so
running one on Linux would need a File Manager, Memory Manager and part
of the Resource Manager shimmed under `qemu-m68k`, where `tools/ssmodule/`
needs three traps.

### What the data path does, run on a Macintosh 2026-09-29

`tools/vmac/dcmptest` drives `MacPaint 1` both ways under Mini vMac,
System 6 Mac Plus (`sitopt.py --dcmpdata`): the `COMP` gets the
2,945-byte `Painting`, the `DCMP` a `CInfoPBRec` for an empty file and
the remnant.

| | |
|---|---|
| what `MacPaint 1` optimizes away | the **512-byte header** and the whole **resource fork** |
| the remnant the `COMP` made | **2,437 B** — a 4-byte length, then the document from offset 512 on, byte for byte |
| what the `DCMP` rebuilt from it | **2,945 B data, no resource fork** — the declared lengths of the original |
| its artwork from 512 on | **byte-identical** to the document, and to what 2.0.1's own unstuffer produced from the archive (`specimens/sitopt/unapp3`) |
| its first 512 bytes | **a stack frame**, below |

**The remnant's leading length is how a `DCMP` knows when to stop** (read
on the first positive-selector call; every write clamped to it).

**The 512 header bytes are the CALLER's uninitialised stack**: the `DCMP`
links a 670-byte frame and `_Write`s 512 bytes of it unassigned. Three
runs of the same probe write the same 512 bytes, a differently built
probe **135** different ones, StuffIt Deluxe 2.0.1 **421**: an optimized
MacPaint entry has **no byte-exact answer not tied to one caller**.

**The archive stores that remnant**: `unar` 1.10.8 decodes the entry's
data fork (477 stored bytes) to **2,437 bytes byte-identical to the
`COMP`'s output**, reporting *"Wrong checksum"* (it decodes until the
input runs out). Its resource fork declares 501 and stores **2** bytes,
from which `unar` produces **82 bytes** of invention (*"Error on
decrunching"*). **An optimized entry carries NO checksum**: both CRC-16
fields read `ffff` where the control's read `9939` and `0629` (the pair
`--makepaint` wrote). `*selector > 0` on a `COMP` is the read-out of its
temp file (which cannot be opened while the module holds it, -49
`opWrErr`; -2 deletes it).

### What an Optimizer did to the fork, and what this engine undoes

**All four reversible Optimizers are undone in `src/fmt_sit.c`** — three
since 2026-09-29, `JPEG Lossy` since 2026-10-02 — each read out of its own
`'DCMP'` and graded against archives StuffIt Deluxe 2.0.1 wrote
(`tools/run_sitopt.py`):

| module | id | what it does to the fork | undone | checked by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Sound 1` | 1 | **a byte-wise delta** over the whole fork, in place: `out[i] = out[i-1] + in[i]`, seeded 0 | **exactly** | the entry's own CRC-16 |
| `Photoshop 1` | 2 | the same — the two `'DCMP'`s are byte-identical apart from the name and the id | **exactly** | the same |
| `JPEG Lossy` | 3 | **a TIFF of C-Cube JPEG** behind an 80-byte header, tables and all | **inexactly** (lossy), and **byte-exact against 2.0.1's own output** | nothing — `ffff` |
| `MacPaint 1` | 4 | drops the painting's **512-byte header** and the **whole resource fork**; stores its own length, then the file from 512 on | **inexactly** | the remnant's own length, against the declared one |
| `Text 1` | 5 → **255** | tabs, trailing space, resources — and nothing undoes it | nothing to undo | the entry's own CRC-16 |
