A fork compressed by an external Compressor module (nibble 7) or an
Optimizer id outside these is refused per entry (OPEN_ITEMS.md).

### Two optimized archives nobody here made

Found 2026-10-06 (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-06, *the Optimizer that was in
somebody else's corpus*): both `Text 1`'s, off Giga-ROM CDs pressed
1992-93, fixtures with a pinned URL:

| fixture | the optimized entry | method under the bit | ids at 106/108 | `rlen` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `derived-QK_2.1_icon_family.sit` | `QK 2.1 icon info`, 1,487 B | **8** | 255/0 | 0 |
| `derived-SoundEdit_icon_family.sit` | `SoundEdit™ icon info`, 1,307 B | **3** | 255/0 | 0 |

They grade the `0x40` bit, the 255 marker and the deleted resource fork
on bytes nobody here wrote, and **method nibble 3 under the bit** (2.0.1
was only asked here for 0, 2, 5, 6 and 8). `run_diff.py` grades their
bytes against `unar` manifests; `tools/run_sitopt.py`'s lane 15 grades
the six header bytes — one byte changed (255 → 1) makes it fail twice and
the engine refuse the archive, measured. Both outer files are
MacBinary-wrapped self-extractors (`APPL`/`aust`, a 22,532-byte
extractor), so `sitopt-wild-*.sea.bin` → `derived-*.sit` exercises the
envelope chain too. Combinations no stranger's archive supplied were
asked of 2.0.1 instead (*Every other document 2.0.1 will optimize*).

### Two more, and these the engine UNDOES — found 2026-10-07

Modules this engine undoes, on bytes nobody here wrote, off one 1993
CD-ROM collection, found by asking the index what each archive CONTAINS
(TESTING.md, *Searching somebody else's index by content*):

| fixture | the optimized entry | module | method under the bit | 106/108 | data CRC | `rlen` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sitopt-wild-Phantom.sit` | `Phantom`, 366,189 B | **`Sound 1`**, id 1 | 5 | 1/0 | **`6325`** | 470, kept |
| `sitopt-wild-DNAnalyze.sit` | `DNANAL Key Commands`, 12,992 B | **`MacPaint 1`**, id 4 | 5 | 4/0 | **`ffff`** | 0, deleted |

`unar` records the entries as type **`PNTG`** and **`FSSD`** — the types
the modules' `COMP`s test, and what the sweep selected on. The CRC rule
(*The version byte*, below) holds on both.

**`Sound 1` is adjudicated three ways, none of them this engine**: the
archive's CRC-16 `6325` is the RESTORED bytes' (the optimized ones' is
`f35b`), which also catches `unar` (correct method 5, *"Wrong
checksum"*); the running sum over those bytes is byte-for-byte this
engine's output; and clearing the bit and id (header CRC at 110 repaired)
makes `t` fail a checksum (`tools/run_sitopt.py`).

**`MacPaint 1` has no oracle and is graded anyway**: `unar` fails it
(*"Attempted to read more data than was available"*), `macunpack` skips
it (*"Unknown stuffit flags: 0 65"*); both manifests record the two forks
unadjudicated. The rebuild is graded by MacPaint's own shape: 12,992 B as
declared, 512 zero bytes, then **720 PackBits rows of exactly 72 bytes
with nothing left over** — 576 x 720, the geometry
`reference/deark/modules/macpaint.c` names. deark reads it without
warning; with the 512 bytes removed or noised it reports an unrecognised
version and an image short of 51,840. The picture is a legible
keyboard-command chart. `TC.DNA`, in the same archive, is `Text 1` (255,
method **3**, a real CRC): both kinds in one archive.

### The version byte, across 16,727 wild archives

The published table calls +14 *"Version (meaning not quite clear)"*.
Over 16,727 classic StuffIt archives (`tools/scan_discmaster.py`,
2026-10-06): **14,030 carry 2, 2,600 carry 1 and 97 carry 6 — nothing
else**; **927** more drawn by content on 2026-10-07 add 466 twos, 460
ones and one six. Every 6 is an `ST65` archive (InstallerMaker 6.5's
magic): the byte tracks the writer — 1 is 1.5.1's, 2 everything from 2.0
— and **does not identify StuffIt 2.x**, so the Optimizer bit was the
sweep's key.

**The CRC field says which kind an entry is.** An exactly reversible
module leaves the ORIGINAL file's CRC-16 (`Sound 1` and `Photoshop 1`
entries carry `47a9` and `c495`, their controls' values), so undoing the
delta is verified by the archive; an inexact one writes **`ffff`** (both
`MacPaint 1` forks, `JPEG Lossy`'s data fork). Both halves hold on wild
archives since 2026-10-07 (*Two more*).

**`Sound 1` and `Photoshop 1`** are one `#define` apart, six lines in
`unc_fork_flush` ahead of the checksum, so every method funnels through
it and the CRC covers the restored bytes; `Photoshop 1`'s 115,320-byte
subject and `Sound 1`'s 8,192-byte one come back byte for byte.

**`JPEG Lossy`**, on a document Photoshop 1.0.7 wrote under Mini vMac
(`sitopt.py --add-photoshop`, `--psdoc`, `--psjpeg`):

| | |
|---|---|
| a Photoshop 1.0 document | **196,608 B of raw pixels — 256x256x3, no header at all** — with the geometry in an `8BIM` **1000** resource of ten bytes |
| how those pixels are laid out | **PLANAR**: three whole 65,536-byte planes, not interleaved triples — read as planes the restored image has a mean adjacent-sample difference of **3.1**, read as triples **10.4** |
| what `JPEG Lossy` stored | **14,127 B**, method nibble 0 — STORED, so the module's output is in the archive verbatim |
| what 2.0.1 gave back | 196,608 B and the same resource fork, **10.1 % of the pixel bytes changed** — 58 distinct values where the document had 2 |

The earlier refusal was our synthetic subject: the 80-byte header read
**channels 0, height 0, width 0** where the real document's reads **3,
256, 256**, and the module's own `DCMP` refused the 0x0 image.

**The payload is a TIFF** (`MM\0*`, 22 IFD entries) after the 80-byte
header:

| | |
|---|---|
| geometry | 256x256, `SamplesPerPixel` 3, `BitsPerSample` 8/8/8, `PlanarConfiguration` 1 (chunky), `PhotometricInterpretation` **6, YCbCr** |
| compression | **32865** — C-Cube's private JPEG code, from before TIFF 6.0 assigned 6 to it |
| its own quantisation tables | three, at private tag **33606** (type LONG: three offsets, 64 words each), **Annex K's luminance and chrominance tables in zigzag order, scaled by Q/50** — exactly Annex K at Q=50 and exactly half at Q=25, measured at both 2026-10-02 |
| its own Huffman tables | three DC at **33607** and three AC at **33608**, `BITS[16]` + `HUFFVAL`, Kraft-valid, byte-identical to the module's `DATA` 1001 |
| quality | tag **33604**, the same value at **offset 8** of the 80-byte header and in the module's `Pref` 128. 25 is the DEFAULT, not the format: the module's own dialog takes any Q (*"values of 20 to 30 are recommended. Values over 30 cause image degradation"*) and a Q=50 archive made here is 18,327 B against Q=25's 22,097 |
| the data | 230 segments, `TileOffsets`/`TileByteCounts`, about 11 bytes each |

The module's resource fork holds **`C3a` 1, 31,952 bytes, named "C-Cube
V1.0"**: the `COMP` and `DCMP` wrap a licensed C-Cube engine. Forging
`Compression` to 6 gets the payload past `libtiff`, which returns a
**solid black 256x256 frame** (one distinct byte value, measured).

**The syntax** (read 2026-09-30; `tools/ccube/decode.py`):

| | |
|---|---|
| an MCU | **2 luma blocks, one `Cb`, one `Cr` — 4:2:2**, so 16x8 pixels |
| a segment | **exactly 3 MCUs**, 48x8 pixels, ending `FF Dn` and one pad byte |
| a row of MCUs | **(w + 39) / 40 segments of (segs·16 + w − 1) / (segs·16) MCUs** — C3a+0x4366's arithmetic, matched on every width from 1 to 512 (*Every other document*, below). For w = 256 that is 7 segments of 3 = 336 pixels, the encoder coding the padding flat (corrected 2026-10-11) |
| the restart index | runs continuously **mod 8 across the whole image**, and **does not reset the DC predictors** — only the very first segment of the file carries a non-zero DC difference on a flat image |
| tables | one quantisation, one DC and one AC per component, all three from the file's own private tags |
| bits | MSB-first, `FF 00` stuffing, the last byte of a segment padded with 1s |

Two things are not JPEG's: a restart that does not reset the DC predictor
(contrary to T.81), and the padded width (decode more MCUs than a row
needs and keep those inside the image).

**C-Cube's IDCT is no standard one**: an exact float IDCT is 0.96 % of
bytes away from 2.0.1's output (every difference exactly 1), jpeg-6b's
`islow` 1.065 %, a swept integer transform at best 4.48 % of the inked
blocks (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-02, *a 1990 approximation, reproduced on
purpose*). The codec is **`CCOD` -2011, 25,754 bytes** (`C3a` is the
wrapper and TIFF writer); its only multiply constants are the colour
conversions at CCOD+0xcf8 and +0xd98, and the transform multiplies through
an A4 helper (`jsr %a4@(31482)`). **Precision is nine bits**:
`512, 100, 196, 284, 362, 426, 473, 502, 502, 473, 426, 362, 284, 196,
100`, cos(k.pi/16) at 2^9, built at CCOD+0xeaa and +0x2f3c. The 1-D
inverse at CCOD+0x2f3c, read symbolically by `tools/ccube/m68ktrace.py`,
is a rotation network whose shift is `asr.l #9` then `addq.l #1` if
negative; pass one takes columns into an array of longs, pass two its
columns; the output stage at CCOD+0x381c is `(x + 16) >> 5`, +1 if
negative, +128, clamp — all transcribed with their addresses in
`src/sit_ccube.c`. **With that it is BYTE-EXACT**: `tools/ccube/decode.py`
reproduces 2.0.1's restored file, all **196,608 bytes**, **0 differing**,
on both subjects:

| | the first subject | the second |
|---|---|---|
| what it is | five pencil strokes on white | a 6-pixel comb crossed with a 12-pixel one, and two diagonals (`sitopt.py --psdoc --drawing grid`) |
| what `JPEG Lossy` stored | 14,127 B | **22,097 B** |
| what the module changed | 10.1 % of the pixel bytes | **21.0 %** |
| this decoder against 2.0.1's own output | **0 bytes differ** | **0 bytes differ** |
| output bytes the correction gets wrong (`decode.py --rtz`) | 2,127 of 196,608 — **709** of them luma | **9,645** of 196,608 |

The last row is why the output stage does **not** round toward zero; that
attribution to +0x3826 is withdrawn (BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*).

**Everything else 2.0.1 writes, measured 2026-10-02**: `guess` (Best
Guess) is **method 5**, `none` 0, `fast` 2, `faster` 6, `better` 5,
`optimal` 8. A **1.5.1-compatible** archive reads like any other. A
**folder** is folder-start `0x20`, its entries, folder-end `0x21`. A
**self-extracting** archive cannot be made here: 2.0.1 answers *"No
Self-UnStuffer can be found that supports this archive"* (that half
installs separately).

| asked for | what the archive says |
|---|---|
| `fast`, `faster`, `better`, `optimal` — every method the script language has | the optimized fork is **always stored** (`dmeth` 0x40, nibble 0) and the payload is **byte-identical** across all four; the method applies to the fork the Optimizer did NOT touch, which came back at methods 2, 6, 5 and 8 |
| Q=50 instead of the default 25 | an 18,327 B payload against 22,097, and quantisation tables that are **Annex K unscaled** where Q=25's are half of it. `src/sit_ccube.c` is byte-exact on both, because it reads tag 33606 rather than assuming a table |
| encryption, from a script | **two of the three ways, yes.** Arming `PassLock` from Options ▸ Custom Encryption… ticks the menu and a scripted `Stuff` then writes a PLAIN archive — the menu's setting does not reach it. But STR# 257's `des` and `newde` do: each prompts for a password and writes a real encrypted archive, so **`NewDE™` and `DES` are built into 2.0.1** and `Custom Encryption…` is the plug-in route (*The three ways 2.0.1 encrypts*, below). Measured 2026-10-02 (BLOCKERS.md) |

So the engine's refusal of a non-zero method nibble on an optimized
`JPEG Lossy` fork (`src/fmt_sit.c`) is measured, and the quality is a
user setting.

**`src/sit_ccube.c` is that decoder in the engine**: the data fork comes
out **byte-identical to what 2.0.1 restored**, the RESOURCE fork identical
to the ORIGINAL document's (2.0.1 rewrites 30 bytes of its map);
`tools/run_sitopt.py` asserts both.

| | |
|---|---|
| what it costs the SHIPPED build | **0 B of resident `CODE`, 0 B of A5** — every byte is in the StuffIt overlay, 18,582 -> **23,174 B**, released for any archive that is not a StuffIt one |
| the control | 154,092 -> 158,116 `CODE`, 37,820 -> 37,884 A5 |
| the tiers | unmoved: 71/71, 67/71, 64/71 resident and 71/71, 69/71, 66/71 shipped |
| peak RAM, 256x256 | **31.7 KB**, bisected — the stored fork, one MCU row of three components, and the tables. It grows with the stored fork and with 16 B per pixel of padded WIDTH (8 for a one-channel TIFF), not with the image. 2026-10-11, `--budget` bisected through the MacBinary: 39.7 KB for the 22,097 B Q=25 subject, **51.9 KB** for a 400x300 colour one (32,027 B stored), 42.2 KB at 512x128, 26.9 KB for a 256x256 grey one, 29.2 KB for 128x128 CMYK |

### Every other document 2.0.1 will optimize — measured 2026-10-11

Until this date every `JPEG Lossy` specimen was a 256x256 RGB document of
grey content (chroma flat 128). `tools/vmac/sitcombo.py` asked 2.0.1 for
33 more, each unstuffed by 2.0.1 in the same boot: twelve drawn by
Photoshop 1.0.7 (`--psnew`), 21 coloured ones in its layout (`cdocs/`).
`src/sit_ccube.c` had been right on 2 (the two the module declined),
silently wrong on 5 (exit 0) and refused 26; **it is byte-exact on all 39
`JPEG Lossy` entries on this machine now** (`tools/run_sitcombo.py`;
DECISIONS.md 2026-10-11, *the constant was a square*):

| what the documents were | the engine before | why |
|---|---|---|
| any width but 256 — 400x300, 512x128 | **garbage, exit 0** (mean error 89 and 134 levels) | it decoded 7 segments a row whatever the width |
| 203x77, 21x13, 7x5 | *"truncated"*, *"corrupt"* | the same |
| grey, HSL, HSB, multichannel | *"method 64 is not supported yet"* | one one-channel TIFF per channel, which it refused |
| CMYK | *"corrupt"* | a three-channel TIFF for C, M, Y and a one-channel one for K |
| **any coloured content, even 256x256** | off by up to 20 levels on 56 % of bytes | chroma is INTERPOLATED, not repeated |
| indexed, bitmap | right | the module declines them; the entry is plain |

| what the format does | |
|---|---|
| the 80-byte header | colour code at +0 (1 RGB, 2 HSB, 3 HSL, 4 CMYK, 5 one TIFF per channel), channels at +2, **width at +4 and height at +6**, Q at +8, the length after the header at +10, then **each TIFF's offset from +14**, ending with the total |
| a one-channel TIFF | `SamplesPerPixel` 1, `Photometric` 1, an MCU of one 8x8 block, one table per tag holding the table's offset itself |
| `TileOffsets` | the offset of an ARRAY of segment offsets **even when its count is 1** (7x5, 1x1) |
| chroma | an odd column is the mean of its two neighbours at the module's 16-bit precision; the image's last column, at its half-width rounded up, repeats |
| colour conversion | CCOD+0xd98, which reduces to `y + (k·c2) >> 15`, `c2` being twice the chroma's distance from 128 — the old `>> 14` form agreed on grey and not on green |
| quality | the module's dialog takes **5..255** and refuses 1..3 without an alert; Q=5 stores 31,479 B and Q=255 12,250 B of the same document, both byte-exact |

The same boots, about the archive (all already read correctly):

| | |
|---|---|
| a 1.5.1-compatible archive | the Optimizer is NOT applied, and 106/108 still carry its id under a method byte without `0x40`; so do folder markers, `none`, and the forks of a mixed folder an Optimizer declined |
| method nibble **3** under the bit | 2.0.1 writes it: `Photoshop 1` over the 7x5 document came back `0x43`. *Huffman* has no script word or menu item; `fast` falls back to it |
| `NewDE` with an Optimizer | method **`0x52`** — both bits — and a stale 1 at 107/109 |
| a folder in an encrypted archive | its markers carry `0x30` |
| an encrypted empty fork | is not encrypted: method 0, length 0 |
| the password | asked once per archive, however many `Stuff`s |

**On the Macintosh** (2026-10-11): six of them — 400x300 and 512x128
colour, 128x128 CMYK, 7x5 grey, 256x256 and 1x1 colour — through the
application on a 1 MB Mac Plus under System 6.0.8, every CRC agreeing
with the Linux engine's (`tools/vmac/run.py --mem 1M --expect`). Peak
heap 50.7 KB for the 400x300.

**`MacPaint 1`**: the engine writes **512 zero bytes** where the header
was (the module writes its caller's stack) and **no resource fork** (the
module deleted it; 2.0.1 gives none back). `UncEntryInfo.module_restored`
is 2 for such an entry; the CLI prints a line per entry and per run, the
Macintosh report carries a sentence, README.md the asterisk. **The
remnant's own length is checked against the one the declared length
implies**, so a misread entry is `UNC_ERR_CORRUPT`, not a right-sized file
of wrong bytes.

### What a module gives back, and why the header already said so

**StuffIt Deluxe 2.0.1 with `MacPaint 1` does not restore the original**:
unstuffing its own archive (by script, into an empty folder, original
deleted, twice) gives artwork byte-identical from 512 on, a **512-byte
header differing in 486 bytes**, and **no resource fork** where the
original had 501. The Addendum promises the loss, and says *"the palette
and patterns will use default settings"* — which is not what the module
does: the 512 bytes are its uninitialised stack (*What the data path
does*, above).

**The declared lengths are the whole design**, visible without running
anything:

| entry | declared rlen / dlen | its control | what the header describes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Text 1`, id **255** | 0 / 594 | 338 / 732 | the **optimized** file |
| `MacPaint 1`, id 4 | 501 / 2945 | 501 / 2945 | the **original** |
| `Sound 1`, id 1 | 342 / 8192 | 342 / 8192 | the **original** |
| `Photoshop 1`, id 2 | 326 / 115320 | 326 / 115320 | the **original** |

255 means the header describes what is stored; an id means it describes a
file the stored bytes are a remnant of — `MacPaint 1` declares a 501-byte
resource fork and stores two compressed bytes. No decoder can fill that
fork, so this engine yields none for it (`src/fmt_sit.c`).
`tools/run_sitopt.py` asserts both halves of the table.

**Encryption by the 2.x modules** — `DES` (1), `PassLock` (2), `NewDEx`
(3), `DESex` (64) — is under *Encryption*, below (*The three ways 2.0.1
encrypts*).

## StuffIt 5's method 14

**Implemented 2026-10-03** (`src/sit14.c`): LZSS over a window of up to
256 KB, both alphabets canonically Huffman-coded with a fresh pair of
trees per block — 308 symbols for literals and match lengths, 75 for
distances, and a `1<<j` alphabet the code lengths themselves may be coded
in. Transcribed from libxad's `SIT_14` (inventoried in NOTICE); **no other
implementation exists** — deark names method 14 "installer" with a NULL
decompressor, and `lsar` reports *"Installer"*.

### The two subjects, and what adjudicates them

Neither is a corpus fixture: both are Aladdin's own installers, 6.6 MB and
12.4 MB of commercial software (*The last classic box*).

| payload | entries | `t` (the container's CRC-16, per fork) | against `unar` |
|---|---|---|---|
| StuffIt Deluxe 7.0.3's `StuffIt DLX 703 Classic Install` | 258 | **258/258 pass** | every one of `unar`'s 204 data forks and 77 resource forks byte-identical |
| StuffIt Deluxe 5.5's `StuffIt Deluxe™ 5.5 Installer` | 184 | **184/184 pass** | every one of `unar`'s 119 data forks and 63 resource forks byte-identical |

`unar` extracts fewer forks from both (it fails two files on 7.0.3's and
collapses repeated names); the container's per-fork CRC-16 covers the
rest.

**A fixture that needs neither**: `synth-sit14.sit`, 2,149 B, written by
`tools/synth_sit14.py` (an encoder from the same reading), graded against
`unar` on all **five** entries in every `ci.sh` run. The fifth (since
2026-10-06) found a defect: a 112-byte entry whose literal tree is a
complete code with ascending lengths, decoded wrong from its 26th byte
(TESTING.md, *The sort that dropped what it could not hold*; the fix is
-4 B of `ovl_stuffit`, +0 of stack).

**`synth_sit14.py --wrap`**, outside the fixture: a fork of **278,896 B
for 458 B of stream** (seventeen maximum matches), the only shape that
reaches `sit14_flush`'s window wrap; `unar`, this engine and the encoder
agree on all 278,896 bytes. Its 294,528 B peak would need a 512 KB tier,
so `tools/run_sit14wrap.py` grades it in `ci.sh` instead (TESTING.md,
*The fork that wraps the window*).

### What real streams actually use (instrumented build, 2026-10-03)

| | 7.0.3's payload | 5.5's |
|---|---|---|
| blocks | 566 | 277 |
| tree reads: nested / raw | **524 / 608** | 273 / 281 |
| nested alphabet, 4 / 8 / 16 / 32 symbols | 6 / 17 / 501 / **0** | 0 / 6 / 267 / **0** |
| repeat escapes / zero escapes | 8,665 / 11,088 | 3,761 / 4,612 |
| literals / matches | 7,268,970 / 2,026,122 | 4,233,171 / 973,420 |
| longest match | **16,387 B**, the ladder's ceiling | 16,387 B |
| longest distance | **262,140 B** against a 262,144 B window | 262,141 B |
| longest code | 17 bits | 17 bits |
| byte boundaries, and how many pad with a nonzero bit | 2,222 / **0** | 1,104 / **0** |

Three rows make `src/sit14.c`'s departures from libxad safe: the padding
is zero everywhere (so discarding it, unlike `xadIOByteBoundary`, cannot
be told apart), no stream nests twice, and the longest code is 17 bits
against the 32 the assignment can represent.

### What it costs

| | |
|---|---|
| peak heap, 7.0.3's payload | **276,088 B** — the 256 KB window, 4.7 KB of trees and ladders, and the I/O buffers |
| peak heap, `synth-sit14.sit` | 65,152 B (its longest fork is 17,799 B, so the window is 32 KB) |
| peak heap, `synth_sit14.py --wrap` | **294,528 B** (a 278,896 B fork, so the window is the whole 256 KB). Passes at a 409,600 B budget; refused per entry at both 512 KB tiers' StuffIt 5 grants, 293,264 and 197,864 B, short by 1,264 and 96,664 (corrected 2026-10-10 after 2026-10-09's re-take) |
| `ovl_stuffit` image | 23,670 → **26,274 B** (26,278 until the sort fix of 2026-10-06, which is -4 B), read at launch and released for every archive that is not a StuffIt one |
| resident `CODE`, SHIPPED build | **+0 B**, A5 +0 |
| resident `CODE`, the control | 158,972 → 161,596 B (+2,624; `src/sit14.c` is 2,613 B of text and no data or bss at all) |
| deepest stack chain | **360 B** — `unc_sit14_decode` 104, `sit14_tree` 84, `sit14_build` 172 — against the engine's deepest 3,350 B (*Stack*) |

The window is sized to the fork, by `sit13_winsize`'s argument (*StuffIt
classic: method 13's window*). A fork wanting the whole 256 KB is refused
per entry at the two 512 KB tiers (*What each format is granted*);
`run_tiers.py --check` moves no tally on either lane.

### Run on a Macintosh, 2026-10-03

`synth-sit14.sit` through the shipped application, four files out,
byte-exact against the engine's output, **peak heap 65,194 B** on every
tier:

| tier | result |
|---|---|
| System 6.0.8, 4 MB Mac Plus | **PASS**, 5.2 s (`run.py`) |
| System 6.0.8, **1 MB** floor | **PASS**, 5.1 s (`run.py --mem 1M`) |
| System 3.0, 1 MB | **PASS**, 18.8 s (`sys30.py --mem 1M`) |
| System 3.0, Macintosh **512Ke** | **PASS**, 18.8 s (`sys30.py --emu mv-512Ke`) |

It is not in `sample.txt`, so no tier tally moves. The big-endian half
runs in every `ci.sh`: `tests/run_mac_sink.sh` decodes the fixture under
qemu-m68k through the Mac shell's own sink (bending the decoder fails that
lane on that fixture and nothing else).

## Encryption

In scope since 2026-08-23 (M25). **Eight** ciphers across **six** formats
(table below; DECISIONS.md 2026-08-23, *decryption*). **The corpus holds
exactly one encrypted entry, and the oracle cannot see that it is one**:
`lsar -j` reports `XADIsEncrypted` 0 for every fixture, but
`fixtures/sitcrypt-wild-F7Menus.sit.bin` is encrypted — `unar` does not
know StuffIt 2.x's `NewDE` bit (method `0x12` is reported as
`StuffItCompressionMethod: 18`, `"Compress"`, extraction failing *"Error
on decrunching"*; `0x8D` on the same specimens does set the flag; measured
2026-10-07). Every other archive exercising this is generated
(`tools/synth_crypt.py`, `tools/synth_cpt.py`, `zip -P`, `arc
g<password>`) and graded by `unar -p` where a second implementation
exists; four ciphers are also graded on archives the original software
wrote, kept in the cache — StuffIt classic (`tools/vmac/sitcrypt.py`),
StuffIt 5 (`os9mouse.py`), Compact Pro (`cptcrypt.py --make`) and both of
PackIt's (`tools/derive_pitcrypt.py --make`) (TESTING.md, *the decryption
suite*).

**`PassLock` is not a cipher.** Two archives StuffIt Deluxe 2.0.1 wrote
with it armed, against unprotected archives of the same subjects
(measured 2026-10-02):

| | protected | plain | identical |
|---|---|---|---|
| a 256x256 Photoshop document, `JPEG Lossy`-optimized and STORED | 22,120 B | 22,097 B | **all 22,097** |
| its resource fork | 496 B | 473 B | **all 473** |
| a text file, method 2 (LZW), no Optimizer | 304 B | 286 B | **all 286** |
| its resource fork | 104 B | 86 B | **all 86** |

It appends sixteen bytes of key material and the method's padding and
enciphers nothing: the entry's CRC-16 passes over our output, and both
its `DCMP` and `COMP` data paths push the buffer pointer and never
dereference it. Its About box: *"PassLock provides simple password
protection for files"* (Graven Imagery, 1990). The engine extracts such
an entry without a password and says nothing (the bytes on disk are the
bytes that went in).

#### The three ways 2.0.1 encrypts, and only one of them is nominal

Measured 2026-10-02 against a KNOWN PLAINTEXT — the same `Subject` with
each scheme and with none, the control holding the 286 compressed bytes
behind every ciphertext (`sitopt.py --psdes`, `--psex`, `--enctext`):

| asked for | how the entry says so | the cipher | graded |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DES`, built in | method **0x82**, encryptor id **1** | **standard DES** — IP and FP — in **CBC** | 0 bytes differ from the control |
| `DESex`, the optional disk's module | 0x82, id **64** | the same | 0 differ |
| `NewDE™`, built in | method **0x12** — a bit of its own, **0x10**, and no id | **StuffItDES** — the no-IP variant — in **CBC** | 0 differ |
| `NewDEx`, the module | 0x82, id **3** | the same | 0 differ |
| 3.5's built-in | 0x82, id **4** or unset | StuffItDES through the **8-byte feedback XOR** | the three archives 3.5.1 wrote |
| `PassLock` | 0x82, id **2** | **none at all** | every byte already the control's, on three archives |

The key derivation is one piece of code across every row: the archive key
folded out of the password, the `'MKey'` decrypted under it with its own
verifier, and the file key and IV from the sixteen bytes at the fork's
end (our verifier passing on a 2.0.1 archive isolated the fault to the
fork transform; a sweep of 32 file-key variants × 8 transforms found both).
So **this engine decrypts all five of StuffIt 2.x's ciphers** and reads
`PassLock` entries without a password; the refusal path stays, by id, for
a third party's module.

| format | cipher | key from the password | verified before decoding? | graded by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| StuffIt classic | an eight-byte feedback XOR, keyed through **StuffItDES** (standard DES's round function with no IP/FP, and its own key schedule — the round is shared with PackIt's DES since 2026-09-19) | iterated DES over the password — **ceil(len/8) folds, measured** (below) — then the archive's `'MKey'` resource, then sixteen bytes stored at the end of each fork | **yes** — the 'MKey' block checks against itself | **StuffIt Deluxe 3.5.1 itself**, on three archives it wrote; `unar -p` on the generated ones |
| StuffIt 5 | **RC4** | MD5(password) cut to five bytes, then the fork's own five | **yes** — MD5 of that key against a hash in the archive header | **DropStuff 5.0.1 itself**, on three archives it wrote under Mac OS 9.2 (*StuffIt 5's RC4*, below); `unar -p` |
| PackIt `PMa5` | XOR against a **seven-byte** keystream, cycled, restarted at every entry | DES's PC-1 permutation of the first eight password bytes, zero-padded — PC-1 selects 56 bits, which is where the seven comes from | in effect — the entry header is inside the ciphertext and carries a CRC | **Packit III 1.3 itself**, on three archives its own code encrypted (*PackIt's two ciphers*, below); `unar -p` |
| PackIt `PMa6` | **DES**, ECB; the operation that DECODES is DES's decrypt | the same eight bytes | the same | the same three archives; `unar -p` |
| zip | **PKWARE's** three CRC-32-driven keys | the password bytes, folded in | **yes** — a check byte in the twelve-byte header | Info-ZIP wrote the fixture; `unar -p` reads it |
| zip, WinZip **AES** | **AES** in counter mode, 128/192/256, with HMAC-SHA1 over the ciphertext | PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA1, 1000 rounds over a salt in the entry | **yes** — a two-byte verifier the derivation also produces | 7-Zip wrote the fixtures and verifies them; `unar` 1.10.8 cannot open one at all |
| ARC | a repeating XOR of the password over the compressed bytes | the password itself, and `arc` 5.21q upper-cases it first (measured) | **no** — the format has no encryption flag at all | Debian's `arc` wrote the fixture; `unar -p` reads it; and a garbled archive `tools/synth_crypt.py` writes over three of `synth_arc.py`'s methods, also read back by `unar -p`, which grades the garble where `arc` is not installed |
| Compact Pro | a **64-bit CRC** register; keystream is its second byte, stepped on each plaintext byte. The stream opens with **eight bytes that are decrypted and discarded** — a salt, so two entries with one password differ | nine bytes: a length capped at eight, and the password ADDED into eight accumulators in rotation | **no** — its own message says so | **Compact Pro itself**, both directions. See below |

**The formats not in that table have no encryption** (measured: a string
sweep over DiskDoubler 3.1.2, 3.7 and Norton DiskDoubler Pro, Now
Utilities 5.0.1, the Alysis control panel and StuffIt Deluxe's engine
finds no password feature in DiskDoubler, Now Compress, SuperDisk! or
SpaceSaver; BinHex, MacBinary, AppleSingle/Double, uuencode, tar, gzip
and `.Z` have nowhere to put one). What a user meets:

- **An encrypted StuffIt classic archive that lost its resource fork is
  not decrypted**: `'MKey'` 0 is half the key material and not in the
  stream, so the entry reports "encrypted" rather than asking for a
  password (OPEN_ITEMS.md, *Limits of the formats themselves*).
- **A wrong password is reported, never decoded to rubbish.** Where the
  format does not verify (ARC, Compact Pro), the entry is decoded once
  writing nothing to find the key and again to write it.
- **A WinZip AES entry is graded by its authentication code**: AE-2 stores
  no CRC, so the ten bytes of HMAC-SHA1 are the check; AE-1's CRC is
  checked too. The 1000-round derivation takes seconds on a 68000, once
  per entry.
- **An encrypted entry does not stop the walk.** PackIt, whose encrypted
  entries do not say where they end, resyncs on the next block magic at
  multiples of eight bytes from the entry since 2026-10-05 (*PackIt's two
  ciphers*, below).

### StuffIt 5's RC4: graded by Aladdin's own writer

Measured 2026-08-26: three archives written by **DropStuff 5.0.1** on Mac
OS 9.2 under QEMU (`tools/vmac/os9mouse.py`; TESTING.md, *the decryption
suite*), kept in the cache:

| specimen | password | forks | what this engine returns |
|---|---|---|---|
| `enc-pass.sit`, 296 B | `pass` (4) | data | byte-exact |
| `enc-swordfish.sit`, 312 B | `swordfish` (9) | data | byte-exact; a wrong password is refused, not decoded |
| `enc-twofork.sit`, 378 B | `abcdefghijklmnopq` (17) | data **and resource** | **both forks** byte-exact |

`unar -p` returns the same bytes (its resource fork AppleDouble-wrapped).
StuffIt 5's key is MD5 of the whole password, so unlike the classic fold
it has no length dependence; the two-fork specimen grades the per-fork
key. Deluxe 5.0.1 itself quits on an HFS+ boot volume, so DropStuff, which
shares the engine, wrote them (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-26).

### StuffIt 5's archive flag byte, and what bit 5 is

Measured 2026-10-06: header byte 83 over every StuffIt 5 archive on this
machine then — corpus, both installer payloads and the cache, **56**:

| byte 83 | archives | what it means |
|---|---|---|
| `0x10` | 51 | the 14-byte block alone |
| `0x90` | 3 | encrypted, with the 14-byte block (*StuffIt 5's RC4*) |
| `0x30` | 1 | **a comment**, with the 14-byte block |
| `0x00` | 1 | neither (`fixtures/synth-sit14.sit`, written here) |

**Bit 5 marks an archive comment** — shown by `Sample Archive.sit`, which
StuffIt Deluxe 5.5 ships in its `Tutorial Files`. The block is **four
bytes**, two lengths, after every other optional block:

| at | bytes | this archive |
|---|---|---|
| 100 | the 14-byte block (bit 4) | `002b 0005 0118 0200 0000 0000 0000` |
| 114 | comment length, u16 | 204 |
| 116 | style length, u16 | 22 |
| 118 | the comment, MacRoman | *"This is a sample archive provided for you while learning to use StuffIt Deluxe…"* |
| 322 | a TextEdit style scrap | one 20-byte `ScrpSTElement`, so 2 + 20 = 22 |

100 + 14 + 4 + 204 + 22 = **344**, the `firstoffs` the header declares at
byte 94; a block of 0, 2, 6 or 8 bytes misses, and
`tools/derive_sit5crypt.py` checks each alternative. `lsar` prints the text
as *Archive comment*, and Aladdin's StuffIt Engine SDK 5.1.2 declares
`GetArchiveComments(archiveInfo *, Handle *commentText, StScrpHandle
*commentStyl)` — the same pair.

**The hash comes after that block** (measured 2026-10-06), on the only two
archives here setting bits 5 and 7 together (`0xb0`), written by StuffIt
Deluxe 7.0.3 on Mac OS 9.2 by Apple event (`tools/vmac/sitcomment.py
--specimen`):

| specimen | made by | byte 83 | hash at | the unshifted offset reads | what this engine returns |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `enc-comment-added.sit`, 332 B | `set comment of archive 1` on `enc-swordfish.sit` | `0xb0` | **118** | **0**, not a key length | byte-exact at `-p swordfish` |
| `enc-comment-stuffed.sit`, 3,154 B | `set password` + `stuff` into `Sample Archive.sit` | `0xb0` | **118** | **0**, not a key length | byte-exact, 3 entries, 2 forks |

118 is 100 + 14 + 4, as `fmt_sit5.c` computes; the hash there is
`51987de839`, MD5(MD5(`swordfish`)[:5])[:5], as in `enc-swordfish.sit`;
`unar -p` agrees. Bit 4's block is a Pascal string (7.0.3 writes `\x0d`
and *"••Reserved••"*), and the style scrap is optional
(`enc-comment-added.sit`: a 16-byte comment, style length **0**).

### StuffIt classic's archive key: ceil(len/8), and neither reference has it

The password is folded into the archive key eight bytes at a time;
measured on three archives **StuffIt Deluxe 3.5.1 wrote** under Mini vMac
(`tools/vmac/sitcrypt.py`):

| password | characters | folds | XADMaster master | `unar` 1.10.8 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `password` | 8 | **1** | 2 (the second all zeros) ✗ | 1 ✓ |
| `swordfish` | 9 | **2** | 2 ✓ | 1 ✗ |
| `abcdefghijklmnopq` | 17 | **3** | 2 ✗ | 1 ✗ |

The rule is `ceil(len / 8)`: XADMaster folds an extra zero block at
exactly eight and never more than two; `unar` 1.10.8 folds one. Under
eight characters all three agree.

### The one wild archive here that is encrypted — found 2026-10-07

`fixtures/sitcrypt-wild-F7Menus.sit.bin`: a MacBinary-wrapped
**version-1** StuffIt archive stamped 1991-06-03, one entry, resource fork
only, method **`0x12`** (`NewDE` over method 2), 5,944 B down to 4,440,
CRC-16 `5650`, no encryptor id — what 2.0.1 writes with `NewDE™`. If
`0x10` were the published table's folder marker, this entry could not
carry a resource fork. It carries an `MKey` in 318 bytes of resource fork
beside `sitr` and `NuNV`.

Through the MacBinary envelope the `MKey` is readable and the engine
reports *"wrong password, or none given"*; the forkless archive reaches
*"encrypted by a scheme we cannot read"*. **A refusal is the ceiling**:
the read-me beside it says the password was mailed to buyers (*"When you
upgrade, I'll send you a password"*), and a search of the text of all
**27** disc images carrying a wild encrypted classic StuffIt archive found
none — **a negative that was looked for, not one that is proved** (the
index ORs a phrase's words). What this fixture grades is in
`tools/run_crypt.py` (TESTING.md, *Decryption*). `lsar` lists the entry
as an ordinary `Compress` fork and `unar` fails *"Error on decrunching"*;
both are asserted.

### Compact Pro's cipher: read out of the application, and graded by it

No second implementation reads it (The Unarchiver's notes: "the algorithm
for this is unknown"; CptExpand "will not extract encrypted files";
StuffIt's translator: "the file is password protected"). Read out of
Compact Pro 1.51's `CODE` 3 in both directions (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-23,
*decryption*) and graded by the application (2026-08-23,
`tools/vmac/cptcrypt.py`):

| what | result |
|---|---|
| three archives **Compact Pro encrypted** (RLE-only, data forks of 96, 100 and 103 bytes) | every byte back, through our reader |
| one **Compact Pro encrypted, LZH-compressed, two-fork** archive (196 B resource + 312 B data) | both forks byte-exact — which is also the check that the register runs across the two forks without resetting |
| an archive **we encrypted**, handed to Compact Pro | it extracted all three entries, and its own CRC-32 over both decrypted forks passed |

Encryption needs a **registered** copy (*"This version of Compact Pro
cannot validate or extract encrypted files"*); Macintosh Garden publishes
a registration for 1.51. **The eight-byte preamble**: an encrypted stream
opens with eight bytes decrypted and discarded (`CODE` 3 @0x1f5e), counted
in the compressed size of the first non-empty fork (a 400-byte resource
fork is stored as 408) — a salt, found from 96→104, 100→108, 103→111.

### PackIt's two ciphers: read out of PackIt III 1.3, and run out of it

Measured 2026-10-05 (`tools/derive_pitcrypt.py`). Before it, `unar -p` —
XADMaster, the implementation both were transcribed from — was the only
grader. The writer is in the corpus: `Packit III 1.3`, APPL/`'PIT '`,
"PackIt III - Version 1.3 (6/14/87)", inside `packit.pit_.bin`; `CODE` 1
(19,058 B) carries both ciphers, `CODE` 256 (1,960 B) DES's tables.

| what | number |
|---|---|
| witnesses in `CODE` 1, each checked AT its offset | **32 of 32** — and **17** of the patterns occur more than once in the resource, so the address is half of what identifies them |
| tables in `CODE` 256 against FIPS 46-3's own PDF, word for word | **9 of 9** — IP at +0, IP-inverse +128, the L/R swap +256, PC-1 +384, PC-2 +496, E +592, P +688, the eight S boxes +752, the sixteen shift counts +1776 |
| archives its own code encrypted, read back by this engine | **3**, 890 B, 890 B and 302 B; 5 + 5 + 2 forks byte-exact |
| the same archives through `unar` | byte-exact — so the reference agrees with the application, which is a different claim from this engine agreeing with the reference |
| the same archives through `unar` with a WRONG key | *"Archive parsing failed! (Attempted to read more data than was available.)"* on all three, after the first clear entry. **XADMaster does not resync at all**, so this engine returns both clear entries where `unar` returns one — and that is why no oracle can grade the resync either way (measured 2026-10-05) |
| `PMa6` against `openssl enc -des-ecb` over the same key | identical at 64 B and at 272 B, so the pack direction is standard DES **encryption** and reading is its decrypt |

**All of it is 1.3's**; for PackIt III 1.0 to 1.2 (not on this disk) it is
a **hypothesis**.

**No wild archive to grade them on** (2026-10-05, `tools/scan_discmaster.py
--probe pit`; a `PMa4` block can only be stepped over by decoding it, so
the probe decodes):

| sweep | listed | archives walked | blocks | encrypted |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `detection=504d61*` — first bytes `PMa`, complete | 2,573 | **182** | 1,107 (696 `PMa4`, 411 `PMag`) | **0** |
| `extension=.pit`, 3,324 of 22,558 probed | 22,558 | 6 | 27 | **0** |

The sweep cannot see a file whose first 16 bytes the index never recorded,
or a MacBinary-wrapped archive (`packit.pit_.bin` would not match). The
`.pit` sweep is 15 % done; its state file resumes it.

What the application settles:

- **The fourth magic byte is arithmetic**: `'0' + 4·compressed + mode`,
  mode 0, 1 (Simple) or 2 (DES) (`CODE` 1 @0x109a..0x10c4); the unPACKIT
  manual's appendix B lists "PMa5" twice (BLOCKERS.md). Both encryption
  menu items force compression on (@0x0c7e, @0x0c9a) and grey the
  compression item (@0x0c50), so `PMa1` and `PMa2` cannot exist.
- **The `PMa5` key is seven bytes** (PC-1 selects 56 bits; the
  application allocates exactly seven, @0x3386). Output is identical
  either way.
- **Nothing is chained or salted**: the magic is written in the clear and
  the key set once per ENTRY (@0x12c0, @0x12ec).
- **The eight-byte grid is the writer's**: the final flush rounds up with
  `addq.l #7` / `and.l #-8` whenever the mode is nonzero (@0x1942), so the
  resync looks every eight bytes.

**What the grid cost**: `fmt_packit.c` 2,142 -> 2,160 B of m68k text at
`-Os`, but sharing the magic test with the archive walk made **`ovl_packit`
fall 4 B**, 2,648 -> 2,644; in the resident control code rose 20 B and A5
fell 20, so `OVL_SAVED` does not move (ceilings re-taken in
`tools/build_mac.sh`); the shipped resident size did not move. Graded:
the third specimen's ciphertext carries a false `'PMag'` five bytes in,
off the grid, and the old scan lost the entry after it. A constructed
worst case — a real `PMa5` ciphertext carries a false magic off the grid
with probability about 2^-32 per candidate offset.

### The archive comment, which Compact Pro calls a Note

**Two formats carry one**, saved: the CLI writes `<archive>.comment.txt`
and prints it on `l` and `t`; the Mac shell writes a `TEXT`/`ttxt`
document `Comments` into the destination (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-23,
2026-08-26).

| format | where it sits | ceiling | saved since |
|---|---|---|---|
| Compact Pro | one length byte at the top of the index, then MacRoman bytes | 255 | 2026-08-23 |
| Zip | a two-byte length in the end-of-central-directory record, then the bytes, at the very end of the file | 65,535 | 2026-08-26 |

`UNC_COMMENT_MAX` is 65,535 and is **not a buffer size a host may adopt**;
the engine hands over its own. A comment it cannot allocate or read is
dropped and the archive still extracts.

| what | result |
|---|---|
| the **six** corpus Compact Pro archives that carry one — `derived-DigSim_RAM_Circuit.cpt` (*"Hi there."*, 1990) and the five Compact Pro Package installers, whose notes run to 250 bytes over several CR-separated lines, one of them Shift-JIS mislabelled as MacRoman | the saved file and the printed line equal `lsar`'s `XADComment`, in `ci.sh` |
| the **one** corpus zip that carries one, `derived-MUSFMT10.ZIP` — 495 bytes of author, address and format list from 1994, twice what a Note can hold | the same, plus the Mac shell's `Comments` document byte-for-byte against the oracle on x86-64 and under qemu-m68k (`tests/run_mac_sink.sh`) |
| a **two-line note typed into Compact Pro 1.51** under Mini vMac (`cptcrypt.py --make --plain --note`) | 61 bytes back byte-exact, the line break stored as a **CR** exactly where Return was pressed, `lsar` agreeing |

No peak-RAM cost: the zip comment is allocated and freed before the
extraction buffers (`derived-MUSFMT10.ZIP` peaks at 54,856 B either way).
The saved file keeps the CR; the line the CLI prints turns it into a
newline.

## AppleLink packages: SqueezePlay, read out of Aladdin's own expander

**SqueezePlay, extracting since 2026-10-05** (M56). `src/fmt_sqp.c` is the
decoder, `src/fmt_sqp_probe.c` the sniffer, `tools/run_sqp.py` the suite
(TESTING.md, *AppleLink packages: SqueezePlay*). Every LZW fork is a Unix
`compress` code stream, decoded identically by **`gzip`** (*The codec*).
The sniffer answers
*AppleLink package* for **exactly the 7 of 996 corpus files that are
packages and for nothing else** (re-measured 2026-10-17, when
`synth-sqp-meta.pkg` made the seventh; no file's answer changed in any
format), a sweep that can fail both ways (BLOCKERS.md).

**The reference is `AppleLink Package Translator` 3.0.3, "Copyright ©
1990-1992 Aladdin Systems, Inc."** — `'Othe' 1`, 33,300 bytes of 68k with
**148 MacsBug symbols**, so every routine below is named by the binary;
shipped in the **StuffIt Deluxe 3.0.7** installer and no later one. Its
`'HBSF' 0` is `SQUEEZEPLAY 1.0`; it carries AppleLink's `'ALinkFont'` and
an `'INT ' 6000` *"line speed, bps"*. `tools/vmac/README.md` (*The
AppleLink package expander*) rebuilds it; `tools/derive_sqp.py --witness`
checks **twenty-nine byte patterns AT their claimed offsets**.

### The container

Read off `ReadFileAndBuildLinkedList` @0x2faa, with `DoSqForward` @0xb60,
`WrSQPDirectory` @0x5f80, `WrHFSDirectory` @0x5eb2 and `RdHFSDirectory`
@0x5e06 agreeing from the writer's side; the members tile each real
package to its last byte.

| offset | what |
|---|---|
| +0 | `u8` items in the SQP directory, capped at **50** (`DoSqForward` @0xc46) |
| +1 | `u8` version: **0 or 1**, and an error message for anything else (@0x3074) |
| +2 | 8 bytes, zero in both packages, read by nothing |
| +10 | **SQP directory**: *count* × **86**-byte records |
| +86n+10 | `u32` items in the HFS directory, then 8 zero bytes |
| +86n+22 | **HFS directory**: *count* × **90**-byte records — a `u16` LEVEL, a `u16` that is non-zero for a FOLDER, then the same 86 bytes |
| +86n+22+90n | the members' bytes |

**Version 0 stops at the SQP directory** (each item at level 1, none a
folder, @0x31be). Version 1 — both original packages and everything
Aladdin's writer produces — carries both, and **the expander reads the
HFS one**. **The two counts need not agree**: the SQP directory lists only
FILES, capped at 50, skipping folders and anything typed `HBSF`, `PIT ` or
`SIT!` (`DoItemsForward` @0x1d5e), name-uniquified; the header's count
locates the HFS directory, whose own header gives the item count.

An SQP record:

| offset | field | named by |
|---|---|---|
| +0 | `u32` zero — written as zero (`AddItems` @0x239c), read nowhere | — |
| +4 | `u32` **resource** fork's compressed length | `DoWork` @0x714, @0x944 |
| +8 | `u32` **data** fork's compressed length | `DoWork` @0x45a, @0x5ce |
| +12 | `u32` data fork's absolute offset | `DoWork` @0x606, `SetFPos` |
| +16 | `u32` resource fork's absolute offset | `DoWork` @0x816 |
| +20 | 16 bytes of Finder info — an `FInfo` for a file and a **`DInfo` for a folder**, so type and creator are a window RECTANGLE in a folder's record and are not reported; the Finder flags are at +8 of both, and so are the dates below (`ioFlCrDat` and `ioDrCrDat` are one field of one union) | `AddItems` @0x2378 copies all 16, whatever `PBGetCatInfo` left there |
| +36 | `Str31` name, 32 bytes fixed | |
| +68 | `u32` creation date; +72 `u32` modification date | |
| +76 | `u32` resource fork's expanded length | `GetExpandedSize` @0x13ac, node+88 |
| +80 | `u32` data fork's expanded length | same routine, node+92 |
| +84 | `u8` flags: **bit 1 = data fork stored**, **bit 2 = resource fork stored** | `DoWork` @0x618 and @0x96e, both `btst` on the BYTE |
| +85 | `u8` zero | |

The stored flag is a bit of the BYTE at +84 (a `u16` reading would test
bit 9); both original specimens carry `0x8000` there — the high bit set by
`InitSqp` @0x5d56 and tested nowhere, bits 3 to 6 the list's own state,
cleared on read (@0x312c). These readings corrected the section on
2026-10-05.

**The tolerance to question first, if a real package is reported
unrecognised, is the exact tiling**: the probe requires the forks to cover
`[directory end, EOF)` with no gap and no spare byte — what Aladdin's
writer produces, but a property of that writer, not the format.

**It is VACUOUS when every compressed length is zero** (a file of zeros:
both sides 0). What refuses that is the **record name length, 1..31** —
the HFS catalog's own name (`AddItems` @0x22ac fills `ioNamePtr`, @0x22be
is `PBGetCatInfo`, @0x2354 copies it to record+36, written by
`WrHFSDirectory` @0x5f2e). Measured: **53 records across all 8 real
packages, shortest name 7 bytes, none of length zero**; 2 files of zeros
and 2 packages of one empty file sit either side of the test in
`tools/run_sqp.py`'s lane 4 (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-06, *the directory of
empty records*; BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*).

**A folder's record carries no forks**: `DoItemsReverse` @0x1ecc branches
away before `DoWork`, so its length fields are whatever `PBGetCatInfo`
left. A folder owns the following items of greater level
(`CalculateFolderSize` @0x113c), from which the engine builds paths.

### The codec

`compress`-style LZW, off `GetCode` @0x2b98 and `Decompress` @0x2a78;
`InitLzw` @0x26b0 builds the `lmask`/`rmask` tables and allocates prefix,
suffix and stack buffers (34,396 / 17,198 / 8,599 bytes).

| parameter | value |
|---|---|
| code width | 9 bits, rising to **13** |
| table ceiling | 8,192 codes |
| clear code | **256** |
| first free code | 257 after a reset |
| packing | LSB-first, in blocks of *n_bits* bytes; a width change discards the rest of the block |

**`src/unc_lzw.c` reads it unchanged** — `unc_lzw_init(&z, al, in, 13, 1,
clen)`, ARC method 9's configuration. The claimed deviation (next free code
256 after a clear) is **withdrawn**: 256 is `compress`'s own value
(`reference/macutils/macunpack/de_compress.c`, `free_ent = FIRST - 1`),
257 deark's, differing in a slot no valid stream names (BLOCKERS.md;
DECISIONS.md 2026-10-05, *the deviation that was the reference*).

**The proof**: with `1f 9d` and a 13-bit block-mode byte in front, a
SqueezePlay fork is decoded **byte-identically by `gzip`**, on all **59**
LZW forks of the eight real packages and all 18 synthesised ones, the
36,000-byte two-clear stream included — in every `ci.sh` pass
(`tools/run_sqp.py`). **The writer differs from `compress`**: no ratio
test; `Compress` @0x288e clears only when the table FILLS, after exactly
7,936 codes of a generation, as `tools/synth_sqp.py` implements.

### What the eight real packages exercise

**Every path the format has is reached by a package Aladdin's own writer
wrote** (2026-10-06, the eight specimens `tools/fetch_specimens.py --only
applelink` fetches): **53 items, 39 of them files, 14 folders, 30 members
with a resource fork, 2 stored forks and 50 clear codes** (DECISIONS.md
2026-10-06, *the lesson that was written down and then not applied*).

| package | B | ver | items | folders | with a resource fork | stored forks | clears | widest | largest table |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MPW Shell 3.2.2.pkg` | 352,605 | 1 | 1 | 0 | 1 | 0 | **29** | 13 | 8,192 |
| `SCNPCK3.4.PKG` | 226,898 | 1 | 9 | **4** | 4 | 0 | 17 | 13 | 8,192 |
| `Sys 7 for MA 2.0.1.pkg` | 64,239 | 1 | **31** | **10** | 21 | 0 | 0 | 13 | 6,691 |
| `1-AppleTalk - Concepts.PKG` | 53,206 | **0** | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 4 | 13 | 8,192 |
| `SD! Patcher > 1.8.2.PKG` | 32,831 | 1 | 1 | 0 | 1 | **1** (resource) | 0 | — | — |
| `Iconf Display` | 18,771 | 1 | 6 | 0 | 3 | **1** (data) | 0 | 12 | 3,191 |
| `ALink.PKG` | 6,900 | 1 | 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 12 | 2,260 |
| `Read First.PKG` | 1,373 | 1 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 11 | 1,203 |

The deepest tree is `Sys 7 for MA 2.0.1.pkg`'s (folder levels to **4**);
the widest member `MPW Shell`'s resource fork, 352,407 B of LZW expanding
to 541,179 and defining 231,818 codes.

### What is graded by what, and what is not

| path | what grades it |
|---|---|
| the **clear code**, and **13-bit** codes | four real packages, 50 clears between them, **and `gzip`** on every one of those streams; `synth-sqp-clear.pkg` as well |
| a **resource fork** | 30 real members, up to 352,407 B, and `synth-sqp-forks.pkg` |
| the **stored** mode | both bits on real packages — the data fork's in `Iconf Display`, the resource fork's in `SD! Patcher > 1.8.2.PKG` — and `synth-sqp-stored.pkg` |
| **version 0** | `1-AppleTalk - Concepts.PKG`, and `synth-sqp-v0.pkg` |
| **folders** | 14 real folders in three packages, levels 1 to 4, and `synth-sqp-folder.pkg` |

**A package carries no checksum**, so the bytes are graded by three
readers — the engine, `tools/derive_sqp.py`'s independent reading, and
`gzip` through a `.Z` header — plus **each record's declared expanded
length**: **67 forks of 8 packages agree on all four counts**, 59 with
`gzip` (`tools/run_sqp.py` lane 1); the four members of the two original
packages keep a recorded sha256. A real package now stands behind every
path `tools/synth_sqp.py` generates (one generator-and-engine error, a
folder's type and creator being its window rectangle, DECISIONS.md
2026-10-05). **No package written here has been handed to Aladdin's own
expander** (MANUAL_TESTS.md).

### What the two oldest packages grade

`ALink.PKG` (6,900 B, 1992-02-11) and `Read First.PKG` (1,373 B,
1992-03-08). The sha256 of each expanded data fork, recorded 2026-10-05
from an independent reading of the translator, is `tools/run_sqp.py`'s
regression oracle:

| package | member | type/creator | packed | expanded | sha256 (expanded) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ALink.PKG` | `FileMaker Import` | `TEXT`/`MACA` | 1,855 | 3,098 | `21b65275…5602` |
| | `Package Data` | `TEXT`/`MACA` | 1,841 | 3,097 | `08854431…3f2a` |
| | `Trademark Data` | `TEXT`/`MACA` | 2,654 | 4,579 | `473810d5…6239` |
| `Read First.PKG` | `Antipiracy-Read Me First` | `TEXT`/`ttxt` | 1,175 | 1,954 | `fb90a049…8641` |

All four read as their names say; `Trademark Data` is a memo dated
*Tuesday, February 11, 1992*, `ALink.PKG`'s own timestamp.

### Two members of one package that want one file

`Iconf Display` holds `bullseye π` (empty data fork) and **`bullseye
π.rsrc`**; under `-nr` the first's resource fork and the second's data
fork are the same output path. **The engine refuses the second entry and
says which** (M9's per-entry refusal for an existing output file, reached
by a real archive), and `--forks hidden` writes all six members;
`tools/run_sqp.py` asserts both.

**Peak heap over the eight is 8,576 to 41,344 B** (`t --stats`,
2026-10-06): 8,576 B for `SD! Patcher > 1.8.2.PKG` (one stored fork, no
table), 13,396 and 17,620 B for `Read First.PKG` and `ALink.PKG`, 21,348
for `Iconf Display`, 35,344 for `Sys 7 for MA 2.0.1.pkg`, and **41,344 B
for the three whose table fills**; tables are sized by the code width,
not the fork.

### Run on a Macintosh, 2026-10-05 and 2026-10-06

All fourteen packages — six synthesised, eight real — through the shipped
application on an emulated Mac Plus (decoder from its `'OVLY'` image, the
resident probe in the real sniff path, forks written by the File
Manager):

| tier | result |
|---|---|
| System 6.0.8, 4 MB Mac Plus | **PASS** (`run.py`) |
| System 6.0.8, **1 MB** floor | **6/6** synthesised and **8/8 real** (`run.py --mem 1M`, the specimens with `--expect`; the six found on 2026-10-06 added that day) |
| System 3.0 / Finder 5.1, **1 MB** | **3/3** synthesised. `sys30.py` has no `--expect`, so it grades committed manifests only and a specimen has none |
| **all seven Mini vMac tiers** — System 3.0, 6.0.8 at 4 MB, 1 MB and 512Ke, System 7.0.1 | **8/8 real** on each, 2026-10-10, the v0.37.0 source: `run.py --expect`, and on System 3.0 `sys30.py`'s screenshots graded by `vmac_check.py --fixture`. Peak heap is identical on all seven: 8,490 B (`SD! Patcher`) to **41,274 B**, 86 B under the 2026-10-06 figures |

**A 541,179-byte member fits a 1 MB Macintosh**: `MPW Shell 3.2.2.pkg`
peaks at 41,360 B. The 31-member package writes 21 files at 35,360 B; the
four-folder one writes five. **The Mac's peak is Linux plus 16 B on
thirteen of fourteen** (2026-10-06; plus 18 B on 2026-10-10/11 for every
LZW package — 41,274 against 41,256 — measured, cause not looked for):
10,848 B for the folder tree up to **41,360 B** where the table fills. The
exception is `SD! Patcher > 1.8.2.PKG`, **8,576 B on both** (8,490 against
8,488 on 2026-10-10), the one package whose only fork is stored.

**`synth-sqp-clear.pkg` is in `sample.txt` since 2026-10-11**, passing on
all seven Mini vMac tiers and Mac OS 9.2 against its manifest; the rest
are not, so they move no tier tally (BLOCKERS.md, the tier table). Real
packages, having no committed manifest, are graded against this engine's
Linux output, whose correctness is `tools/run_sqp.py`'s lane 1.

### What 3.5's `PKG Expand Translator` is, and what it is not

**It is a `CompactPro` build**: its `'Othe' 0` is named `CompactPro`, its
`'Name' 0` `AppleLinkPKG`, and it carries no AppleLink resource (no font,
comms strings or `'HBSF'`). (`Unix Compress Translator`'s code resource is
likewise named `UUCode`.) **Its seven-way dispatcher is the engine's, and
no branch is `.pkg`**: `Othe` 0 @0x30c0 chains seven recognisers, each
handing the file to one `'Eng '` resource in `StuffIt Engine™` (fourteen
call sites, a complete 7 × 2 table):

| recogniser | test | → | what that resource is |
|---|---|---|---|
| @0x37ac | extension `.HQX` | `'Eng ' 0` | BinHex — it holds the 64-character table |
| @0x37f0 | type `PACT` **or** creator `EXTR` | `'Eng ' 1` | **Compact Pro** — `PACT`/`CPCT` is Compactor (`reference/macutils/README`), and its header parser and error numbers match `src/fmt_cpt.c` and `STR# 211` |
| @0x3834 | type `HBSF` | `'Eng ' 2` | the **StuffIt/PackIt** unstuffer — it tests node+32 against `SIT!`, `PIT ` and `HBSF` |
| @0x386a | `.ZIP`, `.GZ`, `.TGZ` | `'Eng ' 3` | Zip/gzip — it holds the gzip magic |
| @0x38dc | `.ARC` | `'Eng ' 4` | ARC |
| @0x3920 | `.Z` | `'Eng ' 5` | Unix compress — it holds `1f 9d` |
| @0x3962 | `.UU`, `.UUE` | `'Eng ' 6` | uuencode |

The claim that `'Eng ' 2` holds the AppleLink expander is **withdrawn**
(BLOCKERS.md). Over the whole 504,902-byte engine fork, `.pkg` is two
strings and no code: `STR# 1002` (*"Triggers"*: `.sit`, `big`, `.sea`,
`.cpt`, `.pkg`) and `PICT` 4001, the About picture; every other hit is
*"AppleLink: ALADDIN"*. The 3.5 translator expands into `NAME Folder`
(`.pkg` stripped, @0x2e5c), and its Standard File dialog lists **only
directories** (`ioFlAttrib` bit 4, @0x215e), so no package written here
could be selected — seven boots, each with a working control (DECISIONS.md
2026-09-01, *the package that cannot be handed over*).
`tools/vmac/spacesaver.py --pkg` re-derives its command parser, with four
witnesses.

## Private File: Aladdin's encrypted single file

**Extracts since 2026-10-09 (M66), graded by its writer.** Nothing else
reads the format (`unar`, deark, macunpack), so the specimens are three
files **Aladdin Private File 2.0's `PF Encrypt™`** wrote under Mini vMac
from fixed subjects and passwords (`tools/vmac/privatefile.py`); all three
come out **byte-exact in both forks**, through every layer, on every
`ci.sh` run where present (`tools/run_privatefile.py`; TESTING.md,
*Private File*). Read off the code first, graded on the files after
(DECISIONS.md 2026-10-09, *the specimen was one search away*).

| subject | forks | password | `.pf` | inside |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Alpha` | 593 B data | `swordfish` (9) | 825 B | `Alpha.sit`, 487 B |
| `Forked` | 400 B data + 608 B resource | `hushnow` (7) | 734 B | `Forked.sit`, 396 B |
| `Gamma` | 0 B data + 608 B resource | `correct-horse-battery` (21) | 713 B | `Gamma.sit`, 375 B |

**Through the application** (`tools/vmac/interactive.py --privatefile`):
`Forked` on System 6.0.8 at 4 MB and 1 MB (2026-10-09) and on **both
512 KB machines**, System 3.0 and 6.0.8 on a 512Ke (2026-10-10; System 3.0
launches it from the Finder): a wrong password refused, then both forks
byte-exact.

### The layout

| offset | bytes | what | |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 18 | `'CSIT'`, version 2, key derivation 1, cipher 1, key length, s16 0, u32 0 | clear |
| 18 | 308 | `'CSIT'`, Str255 name, FInfo, FXInfo, data length, resource length, created, modified | RC4, its own stream |
| 326 | data + resource | the two forks, one after the other | RC4, a second stream |
| end − 12 | 12 | `'CSIT'`, CRC-32 of the data fork, CRC-32 of the resource fork | clear |

The key is the first *key length* bytes of **MD5 of the password's bytes**
(no length byte, no terminator); both RC4 streams start fresh under it. A
wrong password shows only as the record not decrypting to `'CSIT'`. Both
CRCs are of the **plaintext**, reflected `0xEDB88320`, **started at 0 and
never inverted** (`unc_crc32` as it stands). The `.pf` is a data fork
alone, `CSIT`/`CSIT`.

**Inside is a StuffIt archive**: `PF Encrypt™` stuffs the file first
(`Forked.sit`, `SITD`/`SIT!`), so a Private File is an **envelope** here
(*Envelope auto-unwrap*), its reader in the StuffIt `'OVLY'` image.

**The key length is 5 in every file here** (the export build:
`GetEncryptionKeySize` reads `'KSiz'` 128, absent, and falls back to 5;
the domestic build used 16). The reader takes 1 to 16 from the header,
but **no 16-byte file has been seen** — the one claim here no specimen
has graded.

**Accepted is what PFEngine accepts** (StuffIt Engine 5.1's `Eng ` 10009):
magic, version exactly 2, derivation and cipher each at most 1, the s16 at
most 0. Derivation 0 and cipher 0 exist in PFEngine but no writer here
produces them, so **they are refused per entry** (*"encrypted by a scheme
we cannot read"*). PF Encrypt refuses passwords under six characters
(`cmpi.b #6`, CODE 4 @0x3e76); the reader does not.

### What it costs

| | |
|---|---|
| resident `CODE`, shipped (eager) build | **+368 B**: the probe 186, the key-setup shim 80, the reader's dispatch 46, the rest the release switch and alignment |
| A5 | **+32 B**: the engine's table row and name, a sniff slot and two host-vector slots, less 28 B of `.data` alignment that stepped back |
| so, off every extraction's budget | **400 B**, and `run_tiers.py --check` moves no tally on any tier |
| `ovl_stuffit` / `ovl_crypt` | +1,640 B / +104 B (the reader and a copy of the probe / MD5-then-RC4) (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-09, *the read nothing sent short*) |
| peak heap | **5,392 B on all three**, constant by construction: one block holding the RC4 state, the record and one 4 KB I/O buffer, nothing sized by the input; the record is decrypted in place and read again for each password tried |
| time | **RC4 is two thirds of it and CRC-32 a third**; reading the file is ~2 % and MD5 0.05 % (`tools/m68kprof.py`, a 256 KB synthetic file: 8.0 M 68000 instructions after RC4's indices moved into registers, 8.5 M before — DECISIONS.md 2026-10-09, *the specimen was one search away*). Instructions, not cycles — not priced on the tick probe, whose autotest build has no way to give a password |
| deepest stack chain | **678 B**: `unc_pf_extract` 412 → `unc_crypt_pf` 42 → `unc_md5` 120 → `md5_block` 104 |
| sniffing a file that is not one | six header bytes already read; a file that passes them pays one 4-byte read for the footer. **no file in `fixtures/` and none of 4,342 cached specimens** changed its answer |

The reader cost **+1,652 B** resident, so it lives in `ovl_stuffit`; byte
compares took the probe from 222 B to 186 (`tools/build_mac.sh`).

## Finder info in the archive: what each format's own expander puts back

**The engine reads what these tables mark restored since 2026-10-17** for
StuffIt 5, Now Compress and SuperDisk! (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-17, *the
archives' own Finder info*), StuffIt 5's only where b+2 holds. Measured
2026-10-16 and -17 by handing each format's own expander archives under
emulation and reading every catalog record back (`tools/vmac/hfscat.py`;
`tools/vmac/README.md`, *Finder info, read back*; DECISIONS.md 2026-10-17,
*the window StuffIt kept in the name*). A **sentinel** is a header byte
set to a value naming its own offset; "not restored" means it came back 0
with every byte around it a sentinel. **Get Info comments are not measured
here**: they live in the Finder's Desktop file.

### StuffIt classic, read by StuffIt Expander 4.0.2

`sitmeta.py`: 225 corpus archives (1,719 entries matched on the volume, 1
ambiguous), three sentinel boots of 21 archives. The model holds every row
below against every entry: **0 disagreements**; with gates and masks
removed (`--report --control`) it disagrees on 806 (500 file flags, 244
file locations, 46 folder locations, 9 locks, 5 folder flags, 1 view, 1
scroll). No corpus folder exercises the rectangle's gate; the sentinels
are its only evidence.

| header | becomes | read when | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| @66, @70 | file type, creator | always | 1,241 files |
| @74 | file `fdFlags`, less 0x0001, and less 0x0100 (inited) when no position is restored | always | 1,241 files, 744 nonzero |
| @62..65 | file `fdLocation` (v, h); (−1,−1) is not restored | **name CRC** | sentinels; 263 nonzero files |
| @36 bit 15 | file **locked** | **name CRC**, any version | sentinels U1–U5; 3 corpus files |
| @76, @80 | created, modified | always | every entry |
| @38..45 | folder `frRect` | **name CRC** | sentinels; 396 nonzero folders |
| @74 | folder `frFlags`, less 0x0001 | **version ≥ 2** | sentinels; 428 nonzero |
| @68..69 | folder `frView` | **version ≥ 2** | sentinels; 407 nonzero |
| @70..73 | folder `frLocation`, nested folders; the folder made at the destination gets (−1,−1) | **name CRC** | sentinels; 465 |
| @100..103 | folder `frScroll`, stored **h then v** | **name CRC** | sentinels; 357 nonzero |
| — | `fdFldr`, all of `FXInfo`, `DXInfo` past `frScroll`, backup dates, `dirFlags` | never written | every unassigned byte a sentinel, all 0 |

- **Name CRC**: @34 equals CRC-16/ARC of bytes 2..33 (the name's length
  byte and 31-byte field). **Version**: archive byte 14; version 0 is
  refused, *"This archive is damaged."* The 0x0986 at @36 gates nothing.
- **Expander walks by link**: a folder's first child at @62, a file's next
  at @54; garbage there loses the rest with no alert (S5, T5, T7). @48,
  @50 and @58 are not followed.
- **Garbage is written as it stands**: a location of (0x7fff, −0x8000), a
  rectangle whose bottom is above its top, frFlags 0xffff → 0xfffe (S7).

**Who writes them** (classes by version byte, 0x0986 at @36, and @34 being
the name CRC):

| class | archives | files | folders | files placed | folders with a rectangle |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| version 1, no name CRC | 102 | 382 | 34 | 0 | 0 |
| version 1, name CRC | 2 | 4 | 1 | 2 | 0 |
| version 2, no name CRC | 6 | 38 | 11 | 0 | 0 |
| version 2, name CRC | 95 | 682 | 406 | 166 | 374 |
| version 2, 0x0986, name CRC | 21 | 138 | 23 | 95 | 22 |

**StuffIt Deluxe 2.0.1 writes version 2 with @34 = 0** (`--writer201`):
only `frView` @68, `frFlags` @74 and `fdFlags` @74 reach the header.
**StuffIt Deluxe 3.5.1 writes version 2 WITH the name CRC**
(`--writer351`): the lock, `fdLocation` @62, `fdFlags` @74, `frRect` @38,
`frFlags` @74, `frView` @68, a nested folder's `frLocation` @70 and
(−1,−1) for the top one; no `frScroll`, `FXInfo`, `DXInfo` past it or
backup date; `fdFldr` not planted.

**This engine states exactly this table** since 2026-10-17
(`src/fmt_sit.c`, `sit_finder`): `run_meta.py --expander`, 241 archives,
6,627 fields as Expander wrote them, 0 disagreeing; the application,
`metarun.py --expander`, 24 archives, 496 catalog values, 0 (DECISIONS.md
2026-10-17, *the window Expander puts back*).

### DiskDoubler's single-file header (3.7)

`ddmeta.py`; offsets from the header's start
(`docs/formats/diskdoubler-container.md`).

| bytes | what | measured |
|---|---|---|
| +40 | Finder flags | written from the catalog by 3.7 |
| +42..45 | `fdLocation` | **written** by 3.7; **not restored** by its in-place Expand, which leaves the Finder to place the icon; **restored by this engine** since 2026-10-17, with +32..41 as the whole FInfo |
| +46..47 | `fdFldr` (hypothesis: FInfo's next field; 0 in all 70 headers) | — |
| +58..77 | nothing 3.7 writes; DiskDoubler Pro 4.1 puts 1, 2 or 3 at +62 in three specimens, unexplained | 0 in every 3.7 header |
| +82..83 | **CRC-16/XMODEM of bytes 0..81** | 70 headers of 70, and 102 of 102 more in `fixtures/` and the specimens, none 0; 3.7 refuses a header that disagrees, *"Corrupted file or wrong file type"*; this engine's sniffer refuses a nonzero one that disagrees and accepts 0 (XADMaster) |

`FXInfo` and the backup date stay on the compressed file's catalog record.
DDAR and DDA2 records are not measured here (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-25,
*the archive Symantec shipped*, for what a DDAR record carries).

### Now Compress (Now QuickFiler 5.0.1, Dearchive)

`nowmeta.py`; record offsets (`docs/formats/now-compress.md`, *The archive
layer*).

| record | file | folder |
|---|---|---|
| +38..47 | type, creator, flags | `frRect`, `frFlags` |
| +48..51 | `fdLocation` | `frLocation` |
| +52..53 | `fdFldr`: **not restored** | `frView` |
| +62..65 | **backup date** (XADMaster's access date) | backup date |
| +66..81 | **not restored** | `DXInfo`, verbatim |

A nested folder gets all 32 bytes verbatim; the top-level folder's flags,
location, view and script byte are QuickFiler's own.

### SuperDisk! (`super-disk-utility.hqx`'s self-extractor)

`sdmeta.py`.

| bytes | what |
|---|---|
| file record +18..21 | `fdLocation`, restored on both sentinel files |
| folder record, 20 bytes | `DInfo` then `frScroll`: rectangle, view and scroll verbatim, flags less 0x0001; the one folder measured sat at the top level and its v moved (0x010b → 0x00f7) |

### StuffIt 5, read by StuffIt Expander 5.5 (Mac OS 9.2)

`sitmeta.py --sit5`, on `derived-FloatingNotes.sit` (9 entries): a
sentinel copy and the control. Offsets from an entry's second block
(`src/fmt_sit5.c`); every corpus entry is version 1, so the version-3
block is not measured.

| block | file | folder |
|---|---|---|
| b+2..3 | **CRC-16/ARC of the whole second block, this word zeroed**: 272 entries of 272; Expander 5.5 refuses an entry that disagrees, *"a disk related error (-12043)"* | the same |
| b+4..19 | the whole `FInfo`: type, creator, flags, `fdLocation`, `fdFldr` | the whole `DInfo`, `frFlags` less 0x0001 |
| b+20..35 | the whole `FXInfo`; `fdScript` always comes back 0x80 | the whole `DXInfo`; `frScript` likewise |

In both runs the two files below the first folder level lost
hasBeenInited and got a location held in no header, while the four
directly in the top folder kept theirs; that depth is the rule is a
hypothesis, on two files.

### The envelopes: what each standard holds, and what reads it here

**From the standards, not measured against an expander.** Read since
2026-10-17 (DECISIONS.md, *the envelopes' own Finder info*); graded by
`tools/run_meta.py` (TESTING.md).

| format | field | where | becomes |
|---|---|---|---|
| MacBinary I/II/III | FInfo | 65..80, flags' low byte at 101 | all 16 bytes |
| MacBinary I/II/III | "Protected" | 81, bit 0 | locked |
| MacBinary I | "Locked" | 73, bit 7 | locked (isAlias in III, and not read in II) |
| MacBinary II/III | Get Info comment | length at 99; text on the 128-byte boundary after the resource fork | the comment, at most 255 bytes; dropped if the file ends first |
| MacBinary III | `fdScript`, `fdXFlags` | 106, 107 | FXInfo +8, +9, the rest zero |
| AppleSingle/Double, MIME `multipart/appledouble` | entries 4, 8, 9, 10, 12 | RFC 1740 | comment, three dates (0x80000000 left unknown), FInfo and FXInfo, lock |
| uuencode, base64 | `begin <mode>` | octal | locked without owner write (0200); not read in a UUTool document |
| UUTool | `.finfo` | 16 bytes | all of FInfo |
| BinHex 4, BinHex before 4 | — | name, type, creator, flags only (`binhex40_lewis.txt`; `fmt_hecx.c`) | nothing more to read |

**Who writes them** (2026-10-17, corpus and specimen cache): 10 of the
corpus's 237 MacBinary headers hold a nonzero `fdLocation` (two of them
(−1,−1)), 1 the Protected bit (`fractionalwidths.sit`), none a comment, a
script byte or MacBinary I's 73 bit 7; of the 14 ZipIt payloads Python's
`zipfile` reads (16 in all, `run_zipit.py`), 5 are MacBinary III (ZipIt
2.2.2), two with `fdXFlags` 0x80 and one an alias with 73's bit 7 set.

## Archives this engine does not have

**Since 2026-10-18 the engine recognises nothing it does not extract**
(M71; DECISIONS.md 2026-10-18, *the formats it only named*). StuffIt X and
the fifteen PC-side and modern families it used to name — RAR 1.3/1.4,
1.5–4.x and 5, 7-Zip, xz, bzip2, Microsoft Cabinet, Zoo, ar/`.deb`, cpio,
ARJ, Zstandard, lzip, lrzip, WIM and Apple Archive — are
`UNC_FORMAT_UNKNOWN` (*"format not recognized"*); their enumerators are
reserved, not reused (`include/macextract.h`).

**Measured: no other reader claims one.** `tools/run_foreign.py` (in
`ci.sh`) requires **26 specimens** to sniff as `unknown` and make
`macextract x` write nothing: bzip2, xz, 7z, WIM, cpio in three header
shapes and ar written by their own tools and opened by p7zip; four Apple
Archives written and read back by libNeoAppleArchive; StuffIt X, the three
RAR signatures, Cabinet, Zoo, ARJ and lzip headers identified by deark;
Zstandard and lrzip on their published magics; and `synth-os9-rar.rar`.
**No fixture changes format in
996 corpus files**: each manifest's fixture sniffs as its oracle names it,
and the only one that went to `unknown` is that RAR. The CLI before the
change fails 52 of the 97 checks (TESTING.md, *Archives this engine does
not have*).

**brotli was never on the list**: RFC 7932 §9.1 has no magic
(`reference/notes/brotli_rfc7932.txt`). A `.zipx` is a zip whose entries
are refused by method; a `.cpgz` is a gzip whose cpio is a plain file.

## Envelope auto-unwrap

`macextract x` unwraps chained envelopes by content (never by extension):
MacBinary, BinHex, AppleSingle/Double, gzip, .Z, base64, btoa, uuencode
and **Private File** layers yielding exactly one file are recursively
extracted and the intermediate deleted (`-nr` disables; depth ≤ 8). MIME
is deliberately not on the list (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-26 and 2026-08-27).
Verified chains: `.sit.bin`, `.sit.hqx`, `.tar.Z` (`tools/run_unwrap.py`,
in CI), `Subject.MBin.ascii` and a uuencoded MacBinary document
(`tools/run_btoa.py`, `tools/run_uu.py`), and `Forked.pf.bin` →
`Forked.pf` → `Forked.sit` → `Forked`, both forks
(`tools/run_privatefile.py`). A **StuffIt segment** is an envelope too:
the parts are joined and, if the file inside is an archive, the recursion
continues into it.

## Segmented archives (multi-part sets)

One logical file cut across several, one per floppy or, for
`message/partial`, one per mail message. **Six schemes**: StuffIt
segments, Compact Pro segments, spanned zip, plain `.NNN` splits,
DiskDoubler's `SPLT` and `message/partial`. They differ in how much a part
can prove about itself (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-08, 2026-08-09, 2026-08-10,
2026-08-14, 2026-08-21 and 2026-08-29).

### StuffIt segments

XADMaster's "StuffIt split file": a 100-byte header on each part — a set
identifier StuffIt draws from QuickDraw's `_Random` (XADMaster requires
`B0 56`, which StuffIt never writes; DECISIONS.md 2026-10-24, *the magic
StuffIt never wrote*, *the two bytes the writer drew*), part number, the
original file's name, 26 fingerprint bytes at offset 68 (type, creator,
Finder flags, both dates, both fork lengths), the header's own CRC at 94
and a running CRC-16/ARC at 98, held part by part in the joiner
(DECISIONS.md 2026-10-24, *the check the header carries*, *the sum each
part carries*). The parts' payloads in order are the original file —
resource fork, then data fork. `src/fmt_sitseg.c` has the layout and its
sources. The 11 sets StuffIt itself cut extract since 2026-10-24 (the
matrix row).

**Identity is content, not name** (a part belongs if its name bytes and
those 26 bytes agree — the suite's `misnamed` case joins and `unar`
cannot read it), and **the set is self-terminating** (`rsrclen + datalen`
is the payload total; no part count is stored) (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-08).
**The join is read strictly forward: 0 backward crossings**, measured over
the 3-part and 12-part cases (`macextract x --stats`). Peak heap 8 KB: one
buffer, the caller's ~1.1 KB scratch struct, one part open at a time.

**Parts inside envelopes** (`Archive.sit.1.hqx`) join on the Linux CLI
since 2026-08-13 and in the Mac shell since 2026-08-14 (a decoded part
lands on the output volume as `MacExtract part N`, deleted after the
join). The envelope comes off each part before the join, rationed:
envelopes beside the archive are opened only while the set is short of a
part, at `UNC_SEG_HEAD` decoded bytes each, and decoded whole only once
the fingerprint places them. Mixed envelopes, uninformative wrapper names
and parts in three directories join byte-exact; `unar` given part 1
unwraps that part alone and exits 0. Not for the two name-placed schemes:
a wrapped part's filename is the wrapper's (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-13).

### Compact Pro segments

Derived rather than transcribed: measured on sets cut by Compact Pro 1.51
(`tools/vmac/cptsegment.py`, DECISIONS.md 2026-08-14). Every segment carries the ordinary 8-byte
archive header — signature, volume, a 16-bit set identity, and an index
offset that is 0 in all but the LAST segment. `src/fmt_cptseg.c` has the
layout and what would falsify it. **Identity is content** (the manual:
"the names of the files are not important"), and **any** segment opens the
set (the application itself requires the final one). Entries are
addressed as (volume, offset), as in a spanned zip.

Part opens (backward), beside the floor `run_segments.py` derives
independently from each archive's directory:

| case | segments | measured = floor | without the index buffer |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cpt4` (9 files, folders) | 4 | 5 opens, 1 backward | 19 opens, 8 backward |
| `cpt5` (one entry) | 5 | 6, 1 | 6, 1 |

The last column is the fallback when the index allocation is refused.
Peak heap ~20 KB (the reader's 16 KB of buffers plus the index). `unar`
given the final segment writes garbage with exit 0 (measured, asserted by
the suite).

### Spanned ("split") zip — `NAME.z01` … `NAME.zip`

The parts concatenate with nothing to strip; the archive addresses itself
as (part, offset): "disk number start" at offset 34 of each
central-directory entry and the two disk numbers in the EOCD record.
`src/fmt_zipseg.c` has the layout and its sources. Only the FIRST part (a
local header at offset 4) and the LAST (the EOCD, the only place the
COUNT appears) identify themselves, so the host orders parts by filename
and the engine adjudicates structurally — part count, directory position,
every local header, every CRC-32. A lax reader (CRC and signature checks
removed) fails exactly the suite's *wrong order* and *foreign part* cases.
`zip -s` writes its `50 4B 07 08` marker even unsplit; such a file says
"disk 0" and reads as an ordinary zip.

The join costs exactly one backward crossing, the format's floor;
`run_segments.py` requires measured = floor (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-09):

| case | parts | measured = floor |
|---|---|---|
| `zstored` | 5 | 6 opens, 1 backward |
| `zdeflate` | 4 | 5, 1 |
| `znested` | 3 | 4, 1 |
| `zmany` | 12 | 14, 2 |

`zmany` needs two because its central directory crosses from part 11 into
12. Peak heap **12.4–13.0 KB** (the 12,288 B decode buffer plus the
directory); when the directory does not fit the budget the reader fetches
one record at a time (16 opens instead of 6). Both paths run in `ci.sh`.

### `message/partial` — a message split across several FILES

In scope since 2026-08-29: RFC 2046 section 5.2.2 — each part a message
whose body is a slice, `Content-Type: message/partial` carrying the set's
`id`, this part's `number` and the set's `total`. `src/fmt_mimeseg.c`
reads it; the source is `reference/mpack/decode.c` `handlePartial()`, and
mpack writes the sets `tools/run_mime.py` grades against munpack.

- **Each part's header is a different length** (its own mail headers), so
  the joiner asks the part (`unc_mimeseg_head`, the `hdr` hook), which
  re-proves number and id.
- **The set states a part COUNT and no byte total**; the length is learnt
  at the last part.
- **Identity is content**: parts match on `id`; two sets in one directory
