  do not mix (asserted). Parts are identified by `unc_segment_describe`,
  since mail headers exceed the 100 bytes `unc_segment_match` gets;
  `total` is required only on the LAST part.
- **Only the part bodies are joined**: part 1's body begins with the
  enclosed message's headers, so the MIME walk reads the stream directly
  and a split `multipart/appledouble` comes out with both forks
  (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-29, *a message in several files*).
- **A missing part is refused before anything is written** (unlike a
  uuencoded posting; OPEN_ITEMS.md, part 1).

**An anchor that is not a part comes out wrong, with exit 0** (measured
2026-10-11, mpack's three-part set of `MacROT13.cpt`, 3,309 B, `x -nr`):

| anchor | this engine | munpack |
|---|---|---|
| any real part | 3,309 B, exit 0 | the same |
| the three parts concatenated into one file | 929 B, exit 0 | the byte-identical 929 B, and *"corrupted in transit"* |
| part 1 with a line of text appended | 3,328 B, exit 0 | 3,327 B, *"Premature EOF"* and *"corrupted in transit"* |

The joiner takes the anchor's whole tail as part 1's body, as munpack
does; munpack's warning is the enclosed `Content-MD5`. Checking it was
built and caught both, at **+1,446 B** of `ovl_text`, +0 resident
shipped, +784 B in the control — and not taken (OPEN_ITEMS.md, *Out by
the brief*). The one-byte gap is an unpadded final group, which munpack
drops and `unc_b64_flush` keeps.

**Joined on a Mac Plus, 2026-08-29**, at 4 MB and 1 MB: four parts named
nothing alike, carrying a `multipart/appledouble`, become one file with
both forks — 773 B data, 406 B resource, read back off the Out volume
byte for byte (`tools/vmac/segcheck.py --case mimeseg`).

### Segmented sets on a real emulated Mac Plus (System 6.0.8)

**All five floppy schemes join on the Mac** (M11's four since 2026-08-14,
`SPLT` since 2026-08-21), and so does a StuffIt set whose parts are inside
envelopes. The StuffIt table below is from 2026-08-08; the others:

| scheme | how it was driven | result |
|---|---|---|
| Compact Pro, 4 MB and **1 MB** | `run.py`, opened at a MIDDLE segment, the others named `One`, `Three`, `Last` | 9 files byte-exact against the Linux oracle, peak heap 19,915 B |
| Compact Pro, last segment withheld | same | 0 files, refused — the negative control |
| Spanned zip, 4 MB and **1 MB** | `interactive.py --named zip`, opened at part 1 of 5 through Standard File | joined file **byte-exact**, 278,294 B |
| `.NNN` split, 4 MB and **1 MB** | `interactive.py --named split`, opened at part 1 of 3 | joined file **byte-exact** |
| `message/partial`, 4 MB and **1 MB** (2026-08-29) | `segcheck.py --case mimeseg`, four parts named nothing alike | one file, **both forks byte-exact** — 773 B data, 406 B rsrc |
| Parts inside envelopes, 4 MB and **1 MB** | `interactive.py --wrapped`: part 1 as `Apart.sit.1.hqx`, part 2 as `zzz-download.bin`, part 3 as `Other.9.hqx` | same contents as the Linux CLI on the same three files, and no decoded parts left on the volume |
| A chained file (`.sit.hqx`), 4 MB and **1 MB** | `interactive.py --chain` | every layer comes off and the intermediate is removed (since 2026-08-14, BLOCKERS.md) |
| A chained file, three layers deep, 4 MB | `interactive.py --chain` on `chain.dd.ascii` and `chain.dd.uu` — btoa and uuencode around a MacBinary around a DiskDoubler archive | both come all the way down to `Doc`, the same one file the Linux CLI produces, with both intermediates deleted (2026-08-27) |
| The end-of-run report, 4 MB | `interactive.py --report` on a generated DiskDoubler archive: names too long for HFS, one that fits only without its extension, a duplicate, two folders that shorten alike, eighteen photographs alike for 31 characters, and one damaged entry | every rename and the failure listed, the outcome under the list, the box scrolled and saved as a document — and that document read back off the volume and checked line by line (2026-08-29) |
| DiskDoubler `SPLT`, 4 MB | `segcheck.py --case ddseg-real`: two parts **cut by DiskDoubler Pro 4.1.1 itself**, part 2 on the volume as `zzz-piece2` | the joined file off the Out volume equals the file the corpus publishes — 15,918 B data + 342 B resource fork, the latter with the OS-stamped header bytes masked — and the grid agrees, peak heap 8,208 B |

The two name-placed schemes go through the real UI (the autotest opens a
file called `archive.sit`; DECISIONS.md 2026-08-14). All of it replays
with `tools/vmac/segcheck.py`. The three interactive rows were re-run on
2026-09-03 and pass unchanged (BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*).

`derived-Cal.sit` (50,205 B) cut into three parts on one floppy, named
`archive.sit`, `part-two` and `zzz`:

| tier | result |
|---|---|
| Mini vMac, 4 MB | joined and written as `Cal.sit`, **byte-identical** to the original, type/creator `SIT!/SIT!` off the header |
| Mini vMac, **1 MB** (floor target) | same, byte-identical |
| 4 MB, part 2 removed | `ret = -11` (`UNC_ERR_SEGMENTED`), 1 entry error, the 20,000 B that part 1 held left on the volume; peak heap **8,208 B** |

Read back off the Out volume with hfsutils (TESTING.md, layer 9), not the
on-screen grid, which cannot see a filename. The application reads every
file in the folder and asks the engine, so the names are free. Re-run
with:

```
python3 tools/vmac/run.py PART1 --extra-file HOST:MACNAME ... --keep-disks
```

### The "locate part N" dialog, driven on the same machine (2026-08-09)

The interactive application, part 1 alone on the boot volume and parts 2
and 3 on a second floppy so the folder scan fails:

```
python3 tools/vmac/interactive.py fixtures/derived-Cal.sit --segments -o DIR
```

| step | what the Mac showed |
|---|---|
| open part 1 | *Part 2 of "derived-Cal.sit" has not been found. Locate it, skip it, or cancel.* — Cancel / Skip / **Find...** |
| Find..., switch to the other floppy, offer part **3** | *That is part 3 of the same archive. Part 2 is the one needed.* |
| Find... again, offer part 2 | extraction resumes with no further prompt: locating one part rescans its folder, which picks up part 3 beside it |
| finish | *Extraction complete.* |

**It ends on a byte comparison**: the joined file read back off the
volume, 50,205 B, byte-exact (two harness bugs here each produced a
plausible wrong screenshot; DECISIONS.md). A disk inserted mid-run is
`interactive.py --diskswap`; what no emulator can settle (Mini vMac gives
every insertion a new drive) is MANUAL_TESTS.md, "A disk swap in ONE
drive".

### Plain `.NNN` splits

No header, marker or part count: the scheme is the naming convention
(`reference/XADMaster/XADSplitFileParser.m`). The host builds the
descriptor, the engine joins `.001` … `.NNN` flat, and the joined file
comes out named without the extension for the caller's next layer. The
length exists only as the parts' sum, so every part is located before
extraction. One open per part, 0 backward (`--stats`, required in
`ci.sh`); peak heap the 8 KB streaming buffer.

**Only the joined file adjudicates such a set** (measured both ways): a
swap of two parts of a split zip is refused by the payload's own offsets
and CRCs unless it involves part 1, after which the join no longer sniffs
as an archive; a missing tail is undetectable (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-10).

### DiskDoubler `SPLT`

DiskDoubler's Split command, in scope since 2026-08-21. `src/fmt_ddseg.c`
has the layout, derived from the six specimens in
`ssokolow/diskdoubler-test-files`; why each field reads as it does, and
why a `SPLT` header beats a `.NNN` filename: DECISIONS.md 2026-08-21.

A 94-byte header per part: the set's 26-byte identity, part INDEX, part
COUNT, both fork lengths of the original and the part's payload length —
the only scheme matched by content AND with a known size (a missing tail
is detectable), and the only one where bytes appended to a part never
reach the join. No NAME is stored; the joined file takes the opened
part's, minus the `.N`. The payload is DATA then resource fork, the
opposite of StuffIt's.

**No reference reads it** (measured 2026-08-21: `unar` 1.10.8 recognises
neither a part nor the parts concatenated). All three specimen sets
reassemble byte-exact against the files they were cut from, including the
14,128-byte resource fork of the self-extractor; and DiskDoubler Pro 4.1
splitting a 1,024,000-byte file under Mini vMac (`tools/vmac/ddpro.py
--experiments`) gives a set this engine joins back to the input. One open
per part, 0 backward; peak heap 8 KB. The CRC-16/XMODEM at offset 48 is
exact on all six specimen parts and is checked while a part is read
straight through.

**The 4 bytes at offset 4 are the Mac's `TickCount` at the split**
(measured 2026-08-21, `ddpro.py --experiments`): three files split 72 s
apart got 7,581, 11,905 and 16,235 — 60.06 and 60.14 per second against
the Mac's 60.15 Hz tick; two byte-identical files with different names
differed, so it is not a checksum or name hash. Written once per SET; the
joiner matches on all 26 identity bytes anyway.

## Hardening

- Differential: 980 fixtures byte-exact against `unar` manifests (both
  forks + type/creator + Finder flags), `./ci.sh`; the DiskDoubler corpus's
  manifests are built from the files it was made from rather than from a
  decoder (TESTING.md). `run_diff.py` prints the number it ran, plus two
  figures to read with it: **9 forks across 4 fixtures are
  oracle-unadjudicated** (2026-10-07) — both implementations decline them,
  so the engine's CRC-16 is the only byte-exact check, except the 3 newest,
  which `tools/run_sitopt.py` grades three other ways; 8 more agree with
  DiskDoubler's own expander on an emulated Mac (resource header's
  OS-stamped bytes masked), reported on a line of their own — and **7
  entries use a method the ORACLE names as outside v1 scope**, graded on
  names and entry counts alone. No fixture is skipped for anything our own
  engine says about it (TESTING.md, *When our engine declines an entry*).
- Robustness: 10 truncation/bit-flip variants per fixture — no crashes,
  hangs, or sanitizer trips under ASan+UBSan+`-fsanitize=alignment`
  (`-fno-sanitize-recover=all`).
- Fuzz: seeded mutation/splice/garbage fuzzing (`tools/fuzz.py`); 4,000
  cases clean at M9, 150-case smoke in every CI run.
- 32-bit `-m32` build runs the full suite (68000 discipline: no unaligned
  access, no recursion, 32-bit arithmetic in hot paths).

## Size limits

**Measured 2026-08-25** on Linux x86-64 (`-O2`) with sparse subjects; a
corrupted copy of the 3 GiB gzip fails, so the passes test something.

| what | limit | past it |
|---|---|---|
| container, any format | **the width of `unc_off_t`**: 4 GiB on the Macintosh, unbounded in the CLI since 2026-08-28 | `UncIO`'s `seek`/`tell`/`size` are `unc_off_t` (`macextract.h`), 32-bit unless a host defines `UNC_OFF64`, which the Linux build does and the Macintosh build never will — its File Manager takes a signed 32-bit `LONGINT`. A 6 GiB tar of four 1.5 GiB members lists three and exits 1 at 32 bits, four and exits 0 at 64. At 32 bits the CLI says *"larger than 4 GB; this engine addresses the first 4 GB of it"* and hands the engine `UNC_OFF_MAX` rather than a wrapped size. **Only tar reaches past 4 GiB**: its member positions are a running sum, while zip's directory fields are 32-bit and its Zip64 escape is read as halves |
| declared entry size, tar | **2 GiB** | an explicit guard in `fmt_exo.c`; a per-ENTRY refusal (`UNC_ERR_TOOLARGE`, listed with `?` for a size) since 2026-08-27, the walk resuming at the next member |
| declared entry size, zip | 4 GiB − 1 | the format's own field. A 3 GiB stored entry lists and CRC-verifies. A Zip64 extra field can state more; past 4 GiB the entry lists `?` and is refused alone (`UNC_ERR_TOOLARGE`) |
| entry POSITION, zip | 4 GiB | past 2 GB the directory holds `0xFFFFFFFF` and the real offset is in extra field 0x0001, read since 2026-08-27 (below); an offset whose high half is set is refused per entry |
| decoded output, streamed (gzip) | **none found** | 3 GiB and 5 GiB both verify, exit 0. The output size is never held, so nothing bounds it |
| zip entry count | 4,294,967,295 | the Zip64 record's count is read as a `uint32`; a 70,000-entry archive extracts all 70,000 (below) |
| `JPEG Lossy` image (StuffIt 2.0 Optimizer) | **8,192 pixels wide**; any height a TIFF states | `src/sit_ccube.c` refuses a wider image per entry (`UNC_ERR_TOOLARGE`): the cap keeps `w * h * channels` inside a `uint32`. Height uncapped since 2026-10-15; 16 x 8,208 matches 2.0.1's own restore. A 8,208-pixel width crashed 2.0.1 under emulation, once (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-15, *the height nothing needed*) |
| Mac binary | 2 GiB, **not measured** | `SetFPos` takes a signed `long` (`mac/macextract_mac.c`), and HFS caps a fork at 2 GB anyway; exercising it needs real hardware (MANUAL_TESTS.md §3) |

The tar boundary is exact: a 2 GiB − 1024 member lists its size, a 2 GiB
one (0x80000000) lists `?` and fails as an entry. `tools/run_sizelimits.py`
holds both sides and the 4 GB one, in `ci.sh`.

**Only tar and a zip with Zip64 extra fields can express a size the engine
cannot address**; every other format's sizes are 32-bit, and the Macintosh
formats inherit HFS's 2 GB. The other `0x7FFFFFFF` guards in `src/` are
probe rejections (SpaceSaver, SuperDisk!, MacBinary) and segmented-set
descriptors (`fmt_sitseg.c`, `fmt_ddseg.c`). Measured 2026-08-27: a StuffIt
fixture whose second entry declares 0xFFFFFFFF (header CRC recomputed)
lists it as `?`, fails it alone, and extracts the other eight.

**A zip's entry count is 16 bits; past it was silent until 2026-08-25**
(70,000 entries: 65,535 extracted, exit 0). The count now comes from the
Zip64 end-of-central-directory record, read as `uint32` halves (no 64-bit
arithmetic in the engine); a set high half returns `UNC_ERR_UNSUPPORTED`.
Zip64 proper — files over 4 GB — is out of scope (OPEN_ITEMS.md, part 1).
`tools/run_ziplarge.py` holds the boundary from both sides in `ci.sh`;
DECISIONS.md 2026-08-25 has why a count is worse than a length. The corpus
could not have caught it: **the largest zip in 937 fixtures has 29
entries** (2026-08-25).

**A zip's per-entry Zip64 escape arrives at 2 GB, not 4** (fixed
2026-08-27): past CPython's `ZIP64_LIMIT` (2³¹ − 1) the directory holds
`0xFFFFFFFF` with the real offset in extra field 0x0001, which `fmt_exo.c`
did not read. On a 3.4 GB archive of 23,400 entries written by CPython's
`zipfile`, **21,352** failed, every one at or past 0x80000000
(`big/02047.bin` at 0x7FF157D5 passing, `big/02048.bin` at 0x80015800
failing); it now verifies whole. A set high half refuses that entry alone
(`UNC_ERR_TOOLARGE`). BLOCKERS.md has the cost; DECISIONS.md 2026-08-27
(*the escape that arrives at 2 GB*) why the two Zip64 refusals differ.

## Name limits

**Measured 2026-08-27.** A name meets two caps on its way to disk. The
ENGINE carries it in `UncEntryInfo.name`, `UNC_NAME_MAX` (255) bytes plus
a terminator, in a struct that is
**346 bytes on the 68000 target** since 2026-10-17 (DECISIONS.md
2026-10-17, *the metadata, carried to the disk*); one byte of struct costs
two of size, because 292..300 are packed and `name_full_len` then needs a
pad. It travels on a 4 KB call stack. The FILESYSTEM takes what `pathconf`
says (255 bytes per component, 4,096 per path on ext4). Before this date
the first cut names silently (a 457-byte zip path became 255 bytes, exit
0).

| cap | value | what meets it |
|---|---|---|
| `UNC_NAME_MAX` | 255 | every format; the engine's own |
| ustar `prefix` + `/` + `name` | 256 | tar states one byte more than the engine carries |
| uuencode line buffer | 96 | a `begin` line's name is cut here first, before the cap above |
| btoa line buffer | 256 | `xbtoa5 N name Begin` — the name is inside the line |
| one MIME header | 512 | a `Content-Type` `name=` or `Content-Disposition` `filename=` |
| AppleSingle real name | 255 | was 64 until 2026-08-27 |
| `pathconf(_PC_NAME_MAX)` | 255 on ext4 | one component: 255 MacRoman bytes are up to 765 of UTF-8 |
| `pathconf(_PC_PATH_MAX)` | 4,096 on ext4 | `outdir/name`, and the only one of these that refuses |

### How deep anything actually nests

**Measured 2026-10-07** with `tools/measure_depth.py`, over every archive
in `fixtures/` and every cached specimen, envelopes peeled (depth =
enclosing folders). There is no depth limit: `UNC_PATH_MAXDEPTH` (24) is
**deleted**; nesting is bounded by the path STRING at `UNC_NAME_MAX`,
which eight 31-character folders fill (TESTING.md, *Folder nesting, and
the limit that was deleted*).

| format | walks with `unc_path_push` | archives | entries | deepest | where |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AppleLink package | yes | 14 | 72 | **4** | `Sys 7 for MA 2.0.1.pkg` (specimen) |
| DiskDoubler | yes, both containers | 152 | 540 | **2** | `derived-Terrainman_2.0__.sea` |
| Alysis SuperDisk! | yes | 23 | 35 | **2** | `derived-SD__Utilities.x` |
| Now Compress | yes | 73 | 391 | **1** | `SyntheticReferences2.0.sea.bin` |
| StuffIt 5 | no — `unc_entry_path_append` | 63 | 1,055 | 6 | `800saywhat-301-jp.hqx` |
| StuffIt classic | no — the same | 528 | 3,572 | 4 | `derived-OTCFM68KGlue.sit` |

Every subject reaching 24 is generated (`tools/synth_deep.py`), and three
walks were misfiling entries there (BLOCKERS.md, *what ignoring a refused
push cost*). **Removing the limit gave back 64 B** on a 1 MB Mac Plus
(−68 B `CODE`, +4 A5): the walker now writes the uncut path straight into
the host's `long_name` buffer, ONE path with ONE length. Per function,
against the build before: `path_full_add` 152 → **0**, `unc_path_push` 194
→ 160, `unc_path_pop` 62 → 78, `unc_path_init` 30 → 78, `unc_path_leaf`
246 → 318 — **net −50 in the walker**; all six ceilings came down.

#### What the DESTINATION actually allows — measured 2026-10-07

The 24 was three transcriptions of one XADMaster constant.
`tools/vmac/depthtest/` asks System 6.0.8 on an emulated Mac Plus by both
routes a file is reached through:

| route | deepest | the error that ended it |
|---|---|---|
| **created by dirID** — `PBDirCreateSync` with a parent id per level, which is what `EnsureDir` does | **250** | **noErr**: it reached the probe's own loop cap, not a limit |
| read back by dirID | **250** | noErr |
| read by full **pathname** — `PBGetCatInfo` given `Volume:a:b:...` | **49**, at 254 bytes | noErr: a `Str255` cannot say more |

**Classic System software has no depth limit**; the only limit is the
255-byte path string, which the Macintosh shell never builds (it walks by
parent dirID). The HFS volume format agrees: 89 levels created, unmounted
and walked back with hfsutils on Linux (which crashed on its own
double-free at 97).

**Graded on a Macintosh since 2026-10-07**: a 30-level `deep-sqp.pkg` on
an emulated Mac Plus at 4 MB, System 6.0.8, comes off the Out volume as
**61 files, 61 forks byte-exact, names included** (`checkdisk.py
--fixture`), in **30 folders**, deepest `D01/../D30/f30.txt`, all where
the archive put them (the 2026-10-06 application had misplaced five
folders; TESTING.md, *Folder nesting, and the limit that was deleted*).

Stack, `-Os -fstack-usage`, against the build before: `unc_dd_extract`
1,028 → 952 B, `unc_now_extract` 1,064 → 892, `unc_superdisk_extract`
1,428 → 1,344, `unc_sqp_extract` 516 → 516; `dd_fork` unchanged at 1,828;
the deepest chain, through `unc_dd_extract`, **3,386 → 3,310 B** against
the 4 KB budget (*Stack*).

### A name in the wrong encoding is not a name

**A StuffIt 5 name is UTF-8**; every other Mac format here states
MacRoman (`UncEntryInfo.name_utf8`, set by `fmt_sit5.c` alone). The engine
converts nothing:

| | |
|---|---|
| Linux CLI | passes the bytes through, as it already did for zip and tar. `Cool Tools ƒ` instead of `Cool Tools ∆í`, and 35 names across 4 of the corpus's 31 StuffIt 5 fixtures change with it |
| Macintosh shell | folds UTF-8 into MacRoman, once, on the whole path, before anything else reads the name (`Utf8ToMacRoman`), because an HFS name is MacRoman bytes. A code point MacRoman has no byte for becomes `_` and the end-of-run report says so per component; the 31-character cut then acts on the FOLDED name, which is how 16 `é` — 32 bytes of UTF-8, 16 of MacRoman — stop being cut at all |

**Graded on a Macintosh since 2026-09-22**: the sample carries
`derived-Cool_Tools__.sit` (a character MacRoman has) and
`derived-800saywhat301j.sit` (three it has not), and `checkdisk.py` reads
the folded names back off the Out volume (TESTING.md, *The Macintosh
shell's names*); `interactive.py --report-utf8` asserts the report line.
**The archive's names are DECOMPOSED**: `ユーザーライセンスの購入.rtf` is
thirteen code points and the Mac writes thirteen `_`; the oracle manifests
hold the composed spelling, so `run_diff.py` normalizes both sides.

**The fold costs a 1 MB Mac Plus 896 B**: 580 B of `CODE` (inlined into
`sink_begin`, `.text` 1,114 → 1,596 B, plus one report line's function)
and 316 B of A5 (MacRoman's 128-entry high half, `.rodata` 11 → 268 B, and
the string). Stack `sink_begin` 400 → 412 B; peak heap and ticks 0
(DECISIONS.md 2026-09-22, *a name an HFS volume can hold*).

**The notice carries the archive's own name IN FULL** wherever the format
still holds the bytes at the cut: the host passes `UncOptions.long_name`
(the CLI: 64 KB; the Mac shell: NULL, losing only the text), and
`name_truncated` reads 2 rather than 1 when it was filled:

| where the cut happens | the notice gives |
|---|---|
| zip, directory in memory | the whole name |
| tar (`prefix` + `/` + `name`) | the whole name |
| AppleSingle real name | the whole name, re-read from the file |
| the filesystem, not the engine | the whole name — the engine carried it |
| uuencode `begin` line | the whole name: the line is kept in the host's buffer as it streams past, rather than dropped at 96 bytes |
| a Macintosh folder path that SUMS past the cap (DiskDoubler, Now Compress, SuperDisk!) | the whole path: `unc_path_leaf` writes it into the host's buffer as it descends, since 2026-08-27 |
| LZH, btoa's name field, MIME's applefile name | the length, from the field |
| zip with the directory streamed (budget too small for it), btoa's or MIME's cut LINE | the length where the field states it, and *"the rest was cut before it reached us"* — never the shortened name presented as the archive's |

**The CLI shortens and carries on**: the file is extracted under the
shortened name (cut on a UTF-8 boundary), a collision the cut creates is
numbered `~1`, `~2` …, and every shortened name is listed at the end of
the run with the stated length where known; exit 0. Only a path past
`_PC_PATH_MAX` is refused per entry (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-27, *the name
cap*). The CLI's name buffer is sized from `UNC_NAME_MAX` (765 bytes for
an all-MacRoman name), so it cannot be what cuts a name.

**The Macintosh application** (since 2026-08-29) fits every component to
31 bytes, **keeping the extension while the stem is cut**
(`LongDocumentName.txt` → `LongDocumen.txt`); a collision is numbered
`~1`..`~99` off `dupFNErr`, the number **before** the extension (`dup.txt`
→ `dup~1.txt`); a name that cannot be made unique fails its own entry. It
reads `name_truncated` too. Why the suffix survives: DECISIONS.md
2026-08-29.

**A FOLDER is numbered the same way** since 2026-09-03 (two folders
agreeing for 31 characters: `Correspondence With The Publish` and
`Correspondence With The Publ~1`). The folder memo — 200 rows of directory
id and a 32-bit name hash, 1,600 B — authorises the rename, so a directory
this run did not make is still descended into below the top level; at the
top it is numbered since 2026-10-11 (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-11, *the folder
that is already there*). Past `~99` or the 200th folder, the merge is
reported as *combined with* (DECISIONS.md 2026-09-03, *a folder is a name
too*). A comment that could not be written is listed and counted (since
2026-08-29).

| the report holds | measured |
|---|---|
| lines kept | 64, of which the last **16 are reserved for failures** and for a folder that could not be numbered — 100 renames must not push out the one line to act on |
| text | 6,144 B packed end to end, 1 KB of it likewise reserved. **Names go in whole**: eighteen photographs whose names differ at character 34 are one line repeated eighteen times if the name is cut to a row, and the list exists to say which file became which |
| rows a line takes | as many as the box needs, wrapped at a measured width and **broken after the name** where it has to wrap, so one row is what the archive called it and the next is what became of it |
| rows visible at once | 20, Geneva 9 on 11-point rows, in a 460×300 box at (30,26) — the whole of it on a 512×342 Mac Plus screen. A list longer than that scrolls under the last two, which hold a blank line and the outcome |
| static cost | 6,144 B of lines + 1,600 B of folder memo (200 folders, id and name hash), against the ~64 KB the 1 MB floor target had spare on 2026-08-25 (*the PAK methods*) |
| the application, 2026-08-29 | 184,576 B of MacBinary, from 178,944 B before it |

The outcome — *"Extraction complete."* or any other — is the list's LAST
LINE, after a blank one, in bold, pinned to the bottom row while the list
scrolls. A run with failed entries reads **"Extraction finished."** where
the list is shown (*"Some entries could not be extracted."* where it is
not); a run whose archive stopped being readable reads **"Extraction
stopped part way through this archive."** (photographed on System 6.0.8
over a `.sit` cut to 60 %; BLOCKERS.md, *9,320 robustness variants
clean*). **Save as Text** writes the same lines as a `TEXT`/`ttxt`
document. A run with nothing per-entry to say ends in the one-line alert
(DECISIONS.md 2026-08-29, *the end-of-run report*).

**The corpus could not have caught any of it: the longest name in 932
fixtures is 120 bytes** (2026-08-27). `tools/run_names.py` holds both caps
from both sides in zip, tar, uuencode, AppleSingle and a nine-deep Now
Compress tree, in `ci.sh`.

## Zip's Macintosh extra fields

**Read since 2026-10-01**, cited to specifications alone: APPNOTE.TXT
4.6.4–4.6.6 for ZipIt's three fields, and Info-ZIP's
`proginfo/extrafld.txt` (`reference/notes/zip_extrafld_infozip.txt`,
fetched by `tools/fetch_references.sh`) for `0x334d`, which APPNOTE.TXT
names without a layout. `src/fmt_exo.c` stays in `check_provenance.py`'s
HISTORICAL set; NOTICE did not change.

| field | what is taken | cost in `ovl_exo` |
|---|---|---|
| Info-ZIP `0x334d`, central | type, creator | +200 B |
| ZipIt `0x2705` / `0x2805` | type, creator, Finder flags | +160 B |
| Info-ZIP `0x334d`, local | Finder flags, created and modified | +668 B |
| ZipIt `0x2605` | both forks + all of the above, off the MacBinary header | +1,000 B |
| | **total** | **+2,000 B** |
| Info-ZIP `0x334d`, local, since 2026-10-17 | the whole FInfo/DInfo and FXInfo/DXInfo, the backup date, the Finder comment | with the next row, **+664 B** in `unc_zip_extract` and `zip_attr_emit` |
| the central record, since 2026-10-17 | the lock from the external attributes by host, the per-file comment, the MS-DOS date | (above), and the 250 B date conversion |

Resident `CODE` +0, A5 +0, peak RAM +0 on the eight corpus zips there
were when measured, list and extract. The two ZipIt fixtures sit inside
the matrix row's range: 13,645 B listing and 21,317 B extracting
`MrTs_Paint_Folder.zip`, 53,297 B either way for `NPWritersKit.zip`.
**One non-zero RAM figure**: `0x334d`'s attribute record is deflated, and
inflating it takes **7,672 B** transiently (zlib's state and a 512-byte
window, freed before the entry's decode), so a MacZip of stored entries
goes 14,056 → 21,728 B.

**The dates are taken UNADJUSTED, deliberately**: `FlCrDat` is, per
Info-ZIP, an "original Mac FileTime value (local time!)", and the
consumer `ioFlCrDat` is local too. **The two references disagree on
`CrGMTOffs`'s sign** (XADMaster adds, deark subtracts, both calling it
UTC), so neither adjudicates; every entry of `rexnebular_1_0_0.zip`
carries `CrGMTOffs = -18000`, so these dates stand 5 h from `lsar`'s on
all 22 (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-01, *the field that was published all
along*). `fixtures/manifests/rexnebular_1_0_0.zip.json` declares
`"ungraded": {"dates": ...}`; `run_diff.py` refuses any other field under
the key and prints the declaration every run (TESTING.md, *The Finder
metadata, and the one field no oracle settles*).

### What Aladdin's own software does with the same bytes

**No StuffIt reads them; every one guesses from the filename** — three
generations, all run: 3.5.2 on 2026-10-02 (`tools/vmac/zipmeta.py`), 5.5
and 7.0.3 on 2026-10-04 (`tools/vmac/sitzip.py`). 3.5's `UnZip Translator`
carries an `E2Ty` resource — nineteen rows of extension → type/creator
(`.TXT` → `TEXT/ttxt`, `.DOC` → `TEXT/MSWD`, `.GIF` → `GIFf/GCon` …) — and
that is the whole of how an unzipped file gets Finder info.

Four entries cut from `rexnebular_1_0_0.zip`, `0x334d` fields intact,
through each box's own StuffIt Expander:

| entry | the archive | 3.5.2 | 5.5 | 7.0.3 | us |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `checksum.md5` | `TEXT/ttxt` | `TEXT/ttxt` | `TEXT/ttxt` | **nothing** | `TEXT/ttxt` |
| `docs/rex_codes.txt` | `TEXT/MSWD` | `TEXT/ttxt` | `TEXT/ttxt` | `TEXT/ttxt` | `TEXT/MSWD` |
| `docs/rex_codes_dos.txt` | `TEXT/MSWD` | `TEXT/ttxt` | `TEXT/ttxt` | `TEXT/ttxt` | `TEXT/MSWD` |
| `docs/rex_bonus_notes.txt` | `TEXT/MSWD` | `TEXT/ttxt` | `TEXT/ttxt` | `TEXT/ttxt` | `TEXT/MSWD` |

`TEXT/ttxt` is the table's fallback, so `checksum.md5` agreeing is not
evidence; **`MSWD` is the discriminator**, lost three times out of three
by all three. 7.0.3 gives that entry type and creator both zero. **3.5.2
and 5.5 rewrite the data**: `docs/rex_codes.txt` comes off at 720 B
against 739, nineteen CRLF pairs made bare CR and nothing else (our
extraction with `\r\n` → `\r` is byte-identical on all three `.txt`
entries); **7.0.3 converts nothing**.

**The binaries say the same** (5.5's and 7.0.3's payloads extract whole
since 2026-10-03, through method 14, without an emulator; 7.0.3's from a
`.smi` whose data fork is a plain HFS volume):

| version | the binary that reads zip | bytes | `0x334d` | `ZPIT` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3.5 (1994) | `UnZip Translator 3.5`, its own `E2Ty` of 19 rows | 55,523 | 0 | 0 |
| 5.5 (1999) | `Zip Translator 5.5`, no `E2Ty` of its own | 57,923 | 0 | 0 |
| 5.5 (1999) | `StuffIt Engine 5.5`, carrying `E2Ty` of **234 rows** | 2,118,391 | 7 | 1 |
| 7.0.3 (2003) | `StuffIt Plug-Ins/zip`, PowerPC | 435,334 | 0 | 0 |
| 7.0.3 (2003) | `StuffItEngineShell`, PowerPC | 1,427,888 | 1 | 0 |

A reader testing the field would carry `33 4d` as a compared immediate
(68k `CMPI.W #$334D`, PowerPC `cmpwi rX,0x334d`); none of the eight
occurrences is preceded by a compare or move-immediate, and two of the
engine's seven are the ASCII of its `E2Ty` row `.S3M` → `S3M `. The
totals are below chance for the size, so the instruction-encoding
argument carries this, not the count. Both runs used an installation
ASSEMBLED into a plain-HFS Mac OS 9 System Folder from Linux
(`tools/vmac/sitzip.py`; `tools/vmac/README.md` for what it cannot see);
7.0.3's Expander needs CarbonLib 1.6.0 (`hfsboot.img` carries 1.4), so
Apple's update is installed by the guest (`sitzip.py --carbonlib`).
**6.0.1 has not been run** (an uninstalled installer here). Expander
4.0.2 cannot open a zip at all without DropStuff with Expander Enhancer
(System 7 only, not free, not on this machine).

### What grades it

| what | against |
|---|---|
| `0x334d` type, creator, Finder flags | `lsar`, **22/22** on `rexnebular_1_0_0.zip` — a real MacZip archive, 29.7 MB, Zip 2.3, host 0x07, a corpus fixture since 2026-10-04, so `ci.sh` compares all three on every run |
| `0x334d` data forks | Info-ZIP's `unzip`, 21/21 byte-identical, and the ten disk images against the MD5s the archive ships in its own `checksum.md5`; `unar`'s manifest grades the same 21 in `ci.sh` |
| both attribute-record shapes | the deflated one on that archive; the `0x0004` stored one on a generated archive |
| all three ZipIt fields | **nine archives ZipIt itself wrote** (*ZipIt's own archives*, below): 119 entries, type, creator, Finder flags and fork lengths agreeing with `lsar`, 113 forks byte-identical to `unar`; two are fixtures (`MrTs_Paint_Folder.zip`, `NPWritersKit.zip`) graded by `run_diff.py`, seven specimens by `tools/run_zipit.py` |
| `0x2605` both forks | `NPWritersKit.zip`'s entry, 150,016 B of data fork and 323 B of resource fork, byte-identical to `unar` every `ci.sh` run; and extracting the same MacBinary FIXTURE standalone — byte-identical — with `unar` agreeing on the wrapper (`cdev/altw`, 31,714 B) |
| `0x334d`'s three dates | **nothing, and nothing here can**: the two reference implementations disagree on `CrGMTOffs`'s sign (above), so a second implementation is not an oracle for this field. Declared ungraded in the fixture's manifest and printed by `run_diff.py` every run |

### ZipIt's own archives

**Nine, found 2026-10-04 by searching for the BYTES**: five
self-extractors Discmaster's identifier names (`format=zipIt`), and four
from a `Range`-scan of the first local header of all 3,029 zips on the 40
CD-ROMs and FTP mirrors `tools/fetch_specimens.py` cites (512 bytes each,
**seven hits**; DECISIONS.md 2026-10-04, *the archive that carried its own
extractor*). `tools/specimens/zipit.tsv` re-fetches them.

| | |
|---|---|
| writers | **three are ZipIt's own distribution** — 1.4, a Japanese-market 1.x and 2.2.2 — and **five are other people's work**: a White Knight script set (1994), a Java applet's zip living in a resource fork, an Atari emulator a Mac user re-zipped, an EV Nova plug-in and its installer (2002), and a MacTech source drop (2007) |
| entries | **119** |
| `0x2605` | 22 — 16 with a reachable payload, **all 16 a MacBinary stream**; 3 on DIRECTORIES (ZipIt 1.x; see below); 3 refused by method |
| `0x2705` | 86, and **not one of them is MacBinary** |
| `0x2805` | 11, all 8 bytes |
| against `lsar` | 119/119 on name, type, creator, Finder flags and both fork lengths, bar 4 names (below) |
| against `unar` | **113 forks byte-identical** |
| sniffed unaided | **9 of 9 since 2026-10-05** — the five self-extractors needed `-f Zip` until the probe learnt to read past the stub (*What the zip pair claims, and what it costs*, below). Four of them arrived MacBinary-wrapped and extract whole through that envelope |

**`0x2605`'s premise holds both ways**: its `FileType` and `Creator` equal
the MacBinary header's in all 16. **Field lengths a real writer uses**:
`0x2705` at **12** bytes (type and creator alone — ZipIt 1.x, a form no
generated archive here produced), **14** (plus `fdFlags`) and **16**
(plus the trailing word); `0x2805` always 8, `frFlags` 0x0000, 0x03a0 and
0x2310 — both length guards do something. **ZipIt 1.x writes the long
field on directories** (three entries, 1.4 and the Japanese build), whose
zero bytes are not MacBinary; `zip_mb_peek` refuses anything under 128
bytes and probes the header, so the claim is verified, not trusted.

**One name differs from `unar`'s since 2026-10-07**, and ZipIt's field
adjudicates it: the engine extracts under `0x2605`'s "full Macintosh
filename"; over 20 real `0x2605` entries the zip's name, the field's and
the MacBinary header's agree 16 times. The one that differs is ZipIt
2.2.2's `Drop files here to zip (OS X)` (XADMaster renames to the
header's name, which lost the `(OS X)`); the other three, an archive
whose names were made MS-DOS form, now agree with `unar`.

**The name coupling** (re-measured 2026-10-05, re-run 2026-10-06), every
`0x2605` field on this disk — the nine specimens' 22 and
`NPWritersKit.zip`'s, **23**:

| | |
|---|---|
| fields | 23 = 3 on directories + 3 Implode + 17 MacBinary payloads |
| FileName differs from the zip's leaf | **3**, every one Zip method 6 in `zipit-Psycho_1.0b1.zip` |
| …of those, on an entry this build decodes | **0** |
| longest FileName, and the field's end within the extra record | 29 B, 46 B — every one of the 23 is the FIRST field, so the streamed reader's 64-byte `exbuf` reaches all of them |

Since 2026-10-07 `tools/run_zipit.py` asserts of all 23 fields that the
leaf this engine LISTS is the field's FileName; its negative control (a
stub answering with the zip's own leaf) fails in 3 of 5 built cases
(TESTING.md, *ZipIt's own archives*). **The conversion is a PREFERENCE,
not a version** (measured): ZipIt 1.4 carries a `DOSN` boolean, "dosify
filenames" ("Convert filenames to MS-DOS compatible format (8.3,
uppercase)") (BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*). **A leaf is not a path**:
`Psycho!v1.0ß1.zip`'s entries sit under `PSYCHO!/`, for which the archive
holds no entry and no field, so the substitution recovers `Psycho!`,
`READ ME` and `Sample` and leaves the folder upper-cased — 3 of 4 wrong
components.

**`Psycho!v1.0ß1.zip` is Macintosh Implode, extracted since 2026-10-07**
(`zipit-Psycho_1.0b1.zip` on disk; February 1994; three MacBinary entries
with resource forks, every one method 6): all six forks byte-identical to
`unar`'s — 5,801 + 29,628, 4,331 + 670 and 2,289 + 670 B — so the suite's
tally is **119 of 119** and `run_zipit.py`'s `UNGRADED` is empty
(*Implode*, below).

**The five self-extractors ran on all seven Mini vMac tiers on
2026-10-10** (data fork read by the application, MacBinary taken off on
the host; `run.py --expect`), matching the Linux output at 21,551–62,051
B of peak heap. **On System 3.0 the 1440 KB Out floppy is too small for
`zipit-1.4.sea`**: the Finder's `DeskTop` file leaves 152,064 B for a
154,165 B last fork, a per-entry `UNC_ERR_SINK`; at 1600 KB it passes
(BLOCKERS.md, *Status*).


## Implode: zip method 6, PKZIP 1.x

**In scope since 2026-10-07** (M63), by the maintainer's decision
(DECISIONS.md 2026-10-07, *the method that was out for a premise with no
halves left*). `src/fmt_exo.c` decodes it; APPNOTE.TXT 5.3 is cited
section by section, and `reference/deark/src/fmtutil-lzh.c` (MIT) is the
reference each reading was checked against (NOTICE).

### What it costs

Measured 2026-10-07. Implode moved from `ovl_exo` to `ovl_zipold` on
2026-10-17 so that `ovl_exo` links (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-17, *dates in
the Macintosh's own zone*).

| | |
|---|---|
| resident `CODE` and A5, SHIPPED | **+0 and +0** — every byte is in an image |
| the `ovl_exo` image | 29,252 → **31,176 B**, +1,924 |
| resident `CODE` in the resident CONTROL | 164,408 → **166,264 B**, +1,856; A5 unmoved |
| the download, `MacExtract.bin` | 225,408 → 227,328 B |
| peak heap, per Implode ENTRY | **5,096 B at a 4 K dictionary and 9,192 B at 8 K** — 1,000 B of `ImpState` (the three decode tables, their symbol runs and the length scratch) plus the dictionary the flag word names, in two allocations so a refusal is one failure |
| zip's peak-RAM row | **unchanged at 12–54 KB**: deflate's `unc_inflate_raw` (11,256 B, 2,064 B more than Implode's worst) still sets it. `4acalc.zip` 13,477 → 22,669 B against `synth-exo-zip-deflate.zip`'s 24,863; the 54 KB end is `NPWritersKit.zip`'s central directory, 53,297 B |
| stack | `unc_zip_extract` 1,432 → 1,460 B, `zip_decode` 56 → 132 B, `namebuf` unchanged at 256; the deepest zip chain 1,836 → **1,940 B**, then the DEFLATE path; Implode's own 1,684. *Stack* has today's |
| tier tallies | `run_tiers.py --check` moves none: 71/71, 67/71, 64/71 resident and 71/71, 69/71, 66/71 shipped, the same fixtures by name |

### What grades it

| what | against |
|---|---|
| the 7 entries of `4acalc.zip` and `4alnch11.zip` | **`unar` for 5 and deark for 2** (the two `unar` fails, *"Attempted to read more data than was available"*), every `ci.sh` run; `"Implode"` joined `make_manifests.py`'s `DEARK_METHODS` before any decoder existed. `ORACLE_OUT_OF_SCOPE` in `run_diff.py` is empty |
| the 3 entries of `Psycho!v1.0ß1.zip` | **`unar`**, all six forks byte-identical, in `tools/run_zipit.py` (*ZipIt's own archives*). A specimen, so not in `ci.sh`'s fixture set |
| the 13 entries of `PKZ102.EXE` and the 15 of `PKZ110.EXE` | the stored CRC-32, through `tools/specimens/implode.tsv`. They are the NEGATIVE controls and stay specimens: 1.02 writes no bug-capable entry at all and 1.10 is where *version made by* turns from spec 1.0 into 1.1 |
| all 10 wild entries | **the CRC-32 PKZIP and ZipIt themselves stored**, which is the check no decoder here can influence |
| four shapes nothing wild holds | **`unar`, deark, the writer's own plaintext and the stored CRC-32**, in `tools/run_implode.py` every run. `tools/synth_implode.py` is an ENCODER (nothing here writes the method); one of its entries is the fixture `synth-exo-zip-implode.zip` |
| the 13 entries of `pkz101.exe` | **`unar` for 7 and deark for 6**, every `ci.sh` run (*The fixture that carries the bug*, below) |
| two more shapes nothing wild holds, since 2026-10-08 | **deark, the writer's own plaintext and the stored CRC-32**, with `unar` as a NEGATIVE control required to refuse both — the two combinations PKZIP 1.01/1.02's reading is observable in, written that way, one archive each (*The minimum match length PKZIP 1.01 and 1.02 used*, below) |
| on a Macintosh | **`4alnch11.zip`**, in `tools/vmac/sample.txt` since 2026-10-10: byte-exact on all seven Mini vMac tiers at **22,740 B** of peak heap (22,730 on Linux), and on Mac OS 9.2 with all four files read back off the volume (`os9.py --launch`) |
| bent streams | **240 mutants on a fixed seed** in `run_implode.py` (edits inside the declared compressed bytes, clean exit, no sanitizer report), plus `run_robustness.py`'s 10 variants and `run_truncation.py`'s three cuts; `tools/fuzz.py` has it as its third stream seed, and `fuzz_cover.py` reads the decoder's five functions at **0 never entered**, 88.9–100 % of lines (TESTING.md, *Fuzzing*) |

**The suite can fail**: bending the minimum-match-length rule, the
zero-filled window, the length-63 escape, the distance bias or the
direction codes each fails `run_implode.py`.

### What the corpus exercises, and what is UNEXERCISED

**Measured 2026-10-07** over the 10 wild entries then in reach — 27,238
literals and 20,247 matches. The counts were not re-taken over
`pkz101.exe`'s 12 Implode entries (2026-10-08), which change only the
first row:

| parameter | combinations | where |
|---|---|---|
| dictionary × tree count | **all four** | 4 K/2 trees: `4ACALC.EXE`, `CALC.TXT`, `4ALAUNCH.EXE`, `THREED.VBX`, and `pkz101.exe`'s six. 8 K/3 trees: `REGCALC.TXT`, `4ALAUNCH.HLP`, `LAUNCH.TXT`, and `pkz101.exe`'s three. **4 K/3 trees: `PSYCHO!/READ ME` and `PSYCHO!/Sample`, and `pkz101.exe`'s `README.DOC`, `ORDER.DOC` and `OMBUDSMN.ASP` — the only COMMITTED fixture reaching it.** **8 K/2 trees: `PSYCHO!/Psycho!` alone, and it is a specimen** — no fixture holds a wild one, and that is why the generator writes it (*Which half is graded by a real file*, below) |
| the literal tree present / absent | both | coupled to the tree count by the format (APPNOTE 5.3.3), so the four rows above cover it; 8 raw bits per literal in the 2-tree half |
| minimum match length 2 and 3 | both | the same coupling (5.3.4) |
| the length-63 escape, 8 more bits | **249 matches** | every archive but `CALC.TXT`, `LAUNCH.TXT` and `PSYCHO!/READ ME` |
| a match reaching back past the start of output — 5.3.9's zero-filled window | **30 matches** | 7 of the 10 entries |
| a match overlapping its own output | **347 matches** | all 10 |
| the window wrapping | **24 times** | 7 of the 10 |
| code lengths stated | **1..16** in a length tree, **1..8** in a distance tree, **4..16** in a literal tree | a length-**1** code in three length trees (`4ACALC.EXE`, `4ALAUNCH.EXE`, `THREED.VBX`) and `PSYCHO!/Psycho!`'s distance tree; a length-**16** code in **six** length trees and **one** literal tree (`PSYCHO!/READ ME`), so the generator's trees carry one |

**Nothing on the format's own parameter axes is unexercised**, two of the
four combinations supplied by the ZipIt specimen. The one thing that WAS —
PKZIP 1.01 and 1.02's minimum match length — is implemented and graded
since 2026-10-08 (below), caught by the generator's `8k-2trees.txt` and by
the fixture `pkz101.exe`, each catching something the other does not.

### The fixture that carries the bug

**`pkz101.exe`, a committed fixture since 2026-10-08**: PKZIP 1.01's own
July 1989 distribution self-extractor, 131,517 B, sha256
`f36d9551c1917a7990db2b4582191656d748179ea2bd15499294388e9c4fa458`,
re-fetchable from six Discmaster items, two pinned in
`tools/specimens/implode.tsv`, byte-identical (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-08,
*the stub is the writer's signature*).

| | |
|---|---|
| entries | **13**: 12 Implode and 1 stored |
| bug-capable entries (flags 0x0004 — 4 K dictionary, three trees) | **3**: `README.DOC` 873 B, `ORDER.DOC` 4,607 B, `OMBUDSMN.ASP` 595 B |
| …decoding to the stored CRC-32 under 5.3.4 | **0** |
| …under the dictionary-size rule | **3**, and the bytes are identical to deark's output for all three |
| what adjudicates it | **deark** (`MML bug detection: 1`, all 13 clean; it needs `-m zip`, `make_manifests.py`'s `DEARK_MODULE`). `unar` fails the 3 *"Wrong checksum"* and 3 more *"Attempted to read more data than was available"*; Info-ZIP `unzip` fails the 3 *"bad CRC"* |
| the other 9 Implode entries | flags 0x0000, 0x0006, 0x8000 and 0x8006 — combinations where the two rules coincide; this engine extracts **13 of 13** since 2026-10-08 |
| the manifest | **7 forks by `unar`, 6 by deark**, every entry adjudicated and nothing `unadjudicated` — so `run_diff.py` grades all 13 on every `ci.sh` run. The three bug entries' hashes are deark's, and the engine's output is byte-identical to them |
| what it grades that nothing else here can | a probe that ignores the stored CRC-32: `README.DOC` under 5.3.4 decodes to the right LENGTH and wrong bytes, while the generated `8k-2trees-mmlbug` runs out of stream. Measured: that bend passes `run_implode.py` and fails this fixture |

**Two negative controls**: `PKZ102.EXE`, the other buggy version, writes
**no bug-capable entry** (all 13 at 0x8000 or 0x8006); `PKZ110.EXE`, the
fixed version, is where *version made by* turns from ZIP spec **1.0**
into **1.1**.

### The minimum match length PKZIP 1.01 and 1.02 used

**Implemented 2026-10-08** (M64). Those two versions took the minimum
match length from the DICTIONARY SIZE — 3 at 8 K, 2 at 4 K — where
APPNOTE.TXT 5.3.4 takes it from the literal tree's presence:

| flags | dictionary × trees | 5.3.4 | PKZIP 1.01/1.02 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0x0000 | 4 K, two trees | 2 | 2 |
| **0x0002** | **8 K, two trees** | **2** | **3** |
| **0x0004** | **4 K, three trees** | **3** | **2** |
| 0x0006 | 8 K, three trees | 3 | 3 |

No specification states the second column; the source is
`reference/deark/src/fmtutil-lzh.c`'s `params->mml_bug` branch, with the
predicate from `reference/deark/modules/zip.c`'s `could_have_mml_bug`
(NOTICE).

**Measured on the programs, 2026-10-08**, under DOSBox
(`tools/pkzip_writers.py`): PKUNZIP 1.01 and 1.02 read by the second
column and 1.10 by the first (on `PKZ101.EXE`'s three bug entries and a
1989 conforming `CPUTEST.ZIP`, each fails exactly the other reading's
entries). **The shipped WRITERS never use the two bold rows**: in PKZIP
1.01, 1.02 and 1.10 the dictionary size follows the tree count — 0 of 138
Implode entries over 50 inputs, 0 of about 170 more by hand — so
`PKZ101.EXE`'s three entries come from a writer that is none of them
(DECISIONS.md 2026-10-08, *the key that survives and has nothing to
separate*).

#### Where the second attempt goes, which was the design problem

deark decodes twice and keeps the pass matching the stored CRC-32; this
engine streams each fork to the sink, so it cannot keep a pass:

| shape | what it costs | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **1. decode with no output, compare the stored CRC-32, then decode again for real** | one extra decode of ONE entry per archive (below) | **taken** |
| 2. emit, fail the entry, retry it | a new sink contract: `begin_entry` is *"called once per entry before any writes"* (`include/macextract.h`), the CLI refuses to overwrite, the Mac shell creates the file in `begin_entry` (`MyHCreate`, `sink_begin_inner`) | **out** |
| 3. buffer the fork and emit only on success | `usize` is a 32-bit field, so the bound is the format's and not a corpus figure; the largest Implode fork in reach is `THREED.VBX` at **64,544 B**, against a zip whose whole peak-RAM row is **12–54 KB** and a 1 MB floor. One buffer would exceed the format's entire budget | **out** |

**The verdict is per ARCHIVE** (deark's `d->mml_bug_policy`): 5.3.4 is
probed first and a match ends it, so a conforming archive pays one probe
pass of its first bug-capable entry and an unambiguous entry nothing.
Per-entry detection would be **19.4× the passes** over 29,799
bug-capable entries in 1,537 archives. **Measured narrower than the
assumption**: none of those 1,537 mixes the two readings (each entry
classified, 2026-10-08), but the format allows it, so since 2026-10-08 an
entry that inherited the verdict and failed gets one probe under the
other reading; if that matches, it is reported as a checksum failure
(its bytes have reached the sink) and the archive goes back to unsettled.
A verdict per *version made by* is not built (1.10 keeps the 0x000a
stamps of records it did not write, measured 2026-10-08; the one real
1.0x-plus-1.10 archive, `PKZ101.EXE` after `PKZIP 1.10 -a`, extracts in
full). Graded on a three-entry mixed archive: the assumption costs one
entry (TESTING.md, *Implode*, lane 4; DECISIONS.md 2026-10-08, *the
writer a zip is allowed to have twice*).

#### What it costs

| | |
|---|---|
| resident `CODE` and A5, SHIPPED | **+0 and +0**, in both the eager and the lazy configuration — every byte is in `ovl_exo` (then) |
| the `ovl_exo` image | 31,176 → **31,784 B**, +608 |
| resident `CODE` in the resident CONTROL | 166,264 → **166,884 B**, +620; A5 37,928 → **37,916 B**, −12 |
| the download, `MacExtract.bin` | 227,328 → **227,840 B**, and → **228,096 B** with the recovery |
| what a zip or a tar pays on a tier | **nothing, to the byte.** `OVL_SAVED` rose 608 B and the `ovl_exo` image rose 608 B, so the three rows of *What each format is granted* that hold that image do not move |
| peak heap | **unchanged to the byte** on six zips — `pkz101.exe` 23,200, `4acalc.zip` 22,669, `4alnch11.zip` 22,730, `synth-exo-zip-implode.zip` 22,700, `synth-exo-zip-deflate.zip` 24,863, `NPWritersKit.zip` 53,297; the probe frees its `ImpState` and window before the real decode |
| stack | `-Os -fstack-usage` (*Stack*): `unc_zip_extract` 1,460 → **1,468 B**, `zip_decode` 132 → **56 B** (`zip_implode` now its own 112 B frame, beside `zip_imp_dry`'s 172). **The deepest zip chain fell, 1,940 → 1,900 B**, now the PROBE path; deflate's is 1,872 B, an ordinary Implode entry's 1,684 → 1,728 B |
| I/O, `read` + `lseek` counted with `strace -c` | `4acalc.zip` 8 + 15 → **8 + 15**, `4alnch11.zip` 17 + 17 → **17 + 17** — **an unambiguous combination pays nothing**; `synth-exo-zip-implode.zip` 13 + 17 → 15 + 19, `pkz101.exe` 36 + 36 → 36 + **40** |
| instructions, callgrind `Ir` | `pkz101.exe` 21,154,978 → **21,457,500, +1.43 %**, extracting three more entries; `synth-exo-zip-implode.zip` 5,364,267 → 6,709,029, **+25.1 %**, the worst case in miniature (one probe decodes a quarter of the output); `4acalc.zip` −0.39 % and `4alnch11.zip` −0.42 %, code layout |
| tier tallies | `run_tiers.py --check` moves none: 71/71, 67/71, 64/71 resident and 71/71, 69/71, 66/71 shipped, the same fixtures by name |

#### Which half is graded by a real file, and which by this project's own encoder

**Not both, and a green run must not be read as saying so.**

| combination | subject | graded by |
|---|---|---|
| **0x0004 — 4 K, three trees** | **authentic**: three entries of `pkz101.exe`, PKZIP 1.01's own distribution — `README.DOC` 873 B, `ORDER.DOC` 4,607 B, `OMBUDSMN.ASP` 595 B | the CRC-32 PKZIP stored, and deark, through the fixture's own manifest in `run_diff.py` on every run |
| **0x0002 — 8 K, two trees** | **generator only**: the one real 8 K/2-tree entry, `PSYCHO!/Psycho!`, is ZipIt's and follows 5.3.4; searched for by the writer's fingerprint on 2026-10-09 and not found — 151,924 Implode entries in 19,278 MS-DOS/1.0 zips, 0 at 0x0002 or 0x0004 (TESTING.md, *Searching somebody else's index by content*) | `tools/synth_implode.py`'s `8k-2trees-mmlbug`, with **deark as the second reader** and `unar` required to refuse it (`tools/run_implode.py`); **and PKUNZIP 1.01 and 1.02**, by hand under DOSBox: both read it, 1.10 refuses it (`tools/pkzip_writers.py`, 2026-10-09; DECISIONS.md, *the run PKZIP never codes at distance one*) |

deark is not optional on that lane: it finds the rule by its own
double-decode and must agree byte for byte; `unar` has only 5.3.4's
reading, so a generated stream it accepts was never the bug's — the
control, failing both generated shapes as it fails the three real ones.

#### What the suites cannot see, measured by bending the decoder six ways

| bend | caught by |
|---|---|
| the 1.01/1.02 rule keyed on the tree count instead of the dictionary size | `run_implode.py` (both shapes) **and** `pkz101.exe` |
| the probe ignores the stored CRC-32 and asks only whether the decode finished | `run_implode.py` (`4k-3trees-mmlbug`) **and** `pkz101.exe` |
| the probe adopts the 1.01/1.02 reading where 5.3.4 MATCHED | `run_implode.py`, on the four conforming shapes |
| the two-combination predicate narrowed to nothing — the rule removed | `run_implode.py` (both shapes) **and** `pkz101.exe` |
| the two-combination predicate WIDENED to every Implode entry | `run_implode.py`'s pass count, since 2026-10-09: **+1 where 0 is right** on the two unambiguous generated shapes, `4acalc.zip` and `4alnch11.zip`. No output changes — a conforming entry's probe matches under 5.3.4 either way |
| the probe ORDER inverted — 1.01/1.02 asked first | `run_implode.py`'s pass count, since 2026-10-09: **+2 where 1 is right** on the four conforming shapes, **+1 where 2 is right** on `pkz101.exe`. No output changes; the mixed archive's total is +4 either way, so it alone would not catch this one |

The last two change only how many times an entry is decoded; every
byte-comparing lane passed them, and the exact pass count fails them
(TESTING.md, *Implode*, lane 5). The count cannot see WHICH reading a
pass used (lanes 3 and 4 hold that) and does not price a pass (the `Ir`
and `strace` rows do).

### Searching for it: three keys, and the one that worked

**Measured 2026-10-07 and 2026-10-08.** The bug is a decode outcome, not
a byte, so each key names candidates and the stored CRC-32 answers
(`tools/scan_discmaster.py --probe implode`):

| key | what it names | archives probed | bug-capable entries | by 5.3.4 | by the bug |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **the PKSFX 1.01/1.02 stub**, `detection=4d5a98001f000b002000c10affff0a0e*` — the first 16 bytes of a self-extractor those two versions built, identical in 1.01 and 1.02 and different in 1.10 | **5,576** files, 1,323 deduplicated | **1,323**, all of them | **12** | 0 | **12**, in 4 archives, every one a copy of `PKZ101.EXE` |
| `detection=504b0304????02000600*` and `...04000600*` — a zip whose FIRST local header is Implode at one of the two disagreeing combinations | 8,943 files (4,074 + 4,869), 3,821 deduplicated; against 1,125,083 zips whose first entry is method 6 at all and 6,805,790 zips | 800 on 2026-10-07, then **1,537 of 3,821** on 2026-10-08 under the version filter below, after reading all 3,821 directories | 19,107, then **29,799** | 19,105, then **29,795** | **0** |
| the same two tokens with `tsMin=1989&tsMax=1990` — PKZIP 1.1's era and everything before it | 975 | **975** | **3,543** | 3,543 | **0** |

The 4 decoding under neither rule are two copies of one truncated
`synchro.zip` and two more with their central directory gone; `unar` and
deark refuse those too.

**The central directory's *version made by* is a writer key**, with the
host byte (APPNOTE.TXT 4.4.2: spec version low, host high;
`central_vermade()` reads it over two HTTP `Range`s). PKZIP **1.01 and
1.02 write 1.0**, **1.10 writes 1.1** (measured off the distributions).
Over the 3,821 deduplicated plain-zip candidates:

| version made by | archives |
|---|---|
| spec 1.0, host 3 (Unix) | **1,467** |
| spec 1.0, host 0 (MS-DOS) — **the PKZIP 1.01/1.02 fingerprint** | **10** |
| spec 1.0, other hosts (2, 6, 7) | 61 |
| spec 1.1, host 0 | 919 |
| spec 1.1, host 3 | 25 |
| spec 2.0, host 0 | 1,111 |
| spec 2.0 / 3.2, host 7 | 161 |
| spec 0.0–0.9 | 57 |
| directory unreadable | 10 |

All 25 bug-capable entries in those 10 follow 5.3.4: the plain-zip
population at these flags is overwhelmingly PKZIP 1.10 and 2.x, and the
buggy versions are reached through their self-extractors (TESTING.md,
*Searching somebody else's index by content*).

**Also UNEXERCISED**: a `0x2605` FileName containing a `/`. The engine
applies its `/`→`:` swap to the field's leaf (`unc_entry_path_append`),
so a field cannot invent a folder; none of the 23 fields holds one.

## Shrink: zip method 1, PKZIP 0.9x and 1.x

**In scope since 2026-10-08** (M65). `src/unc_shrink.c` decodes it
(`src/unc_lzw.c` until 2026-10-11), from
`reference/deark/src/fmtutil-lzw.c`'s `DE_LZWFMT_ZIPSHRINK` paths
(NOTICE); APPNOTE.TXT names the method and says nothing more (DECISIONS.md
2026-10-08, *the method a Macintosh wrote once*). Reduce: *Reduce*, below.

### Who wrote it: the search, by content

**Measured 2026-10-08** with Discmaster's `detection=` over the first 16
bytes, `504b0304????????MM00*` (a zip whose FIRST local header names
method `MM`). Control: method 6 gives **1,125,083**, as *Searching for it:
three keys* found, and 75 of 75 hits sampled across methods 1–5 carry
that method byte.

| method | files | deduplicated | in a Macintosh context | written ON a Macintosh |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1, Shrink | **96,193** | 48,126 | **141 distinct** | **1** |
| 2–4, Reduce 1–3 | 117 / 143 / 43 | 105 / 92 / 13 | 0 | 0 |
| 5, Reduce 4 | **12,711** | 7,753 | **5 distinct** | **0** |

*In a Macintosh context*: a Mac type/creator other than `TEXT/hscd` (an
ISO 9660 file read on a Mac), the HFS side of a disc, a path inside a
`.sit`, `.sea`, `.cpt`, `.hqx`, `.toast` or `.dmg`, an
Info-Mac/Apprentice/`pub/mac` path, or a Mac CD-ROM collection by name.
All 146 fetched and their directories read: **144 are MS-DOS- or
Unix-written archives (137 + 2 Shrink, 5 Reduce) of DOS and Windows
programs** on Mac media, and one is truncated before its directory.
**The one written on a Macintosh** is `BBA.zip`, an EndNote style from
`pub/mac/` (1993): *version made by* host 7 at spec 3.2, the signature of
`Psycho!v1.0ß1.zip`, so ZipIt by that signature — a HYPOTHESIS. Its
410-byte text entry is shrunk and its style file imploded.

**The search fails on a known case as it should**: at
`Psycho!v1.0ß1.zip`'s size the method-6 key lists all 3 copies and the
method-1 key none, and the context test flags all 3. **What it cannot
see**: a zip whose first entry is another method; a zip behind a
self-extractor; a MacBinary-wrapped zip (**13** in the whole index,
`format=macBinary` with extension `.zip`, the one real zip among them
Implode).

### What grades it

| what | against |
|---|---|
| the 42 entries of the three fixtures | **`unar` for 29 and deark for 13**, fork for fork on every `ci.sh` run. The 13 are every entry with a PARTIAL clear: XADMaster's `XADZipShrinkHandle.m` performs it as a full clear, and `unar` fails each with *"Error on decrunching"*. `"Shrink"` joined `make_manifests.py`'s `DEARK_METHODS`; `ORACLE_OUT_OF_SCOPE` stays empty |
| **2,246** wild entries in **345** archives, 2026-10-08 — the 146 above and 200 drawn at random from the 42,604 distinct method-1 zips under 400 KB (`gtrbites.zip` in both) | **the stored CRC-32**: a Python transcription of the deark paths **2,246 of 2,246**, the C decoder **2,246 of 2,246** (re-run 2026-10-09; set under `~/.cache/macextract/discmaster/shrink/2026-10-08/`). The directories hold 2,252 Shrink entries outside `Thief35.zip`, six encrypted and not graded; `Thief35.zip`'s directory points 1,024 B past ten of its twelve entries and the engine and `unar` refuse it (counted in the next row, by local header) |
| **1,965** wild entries in **346** archives, 2026-10-09 — the same 146 (`scan_discmaster.py --mac-context`) and 200 drawn with `--seed 20261009` from the 42,579 other distinct method-1 zips under 400 KiB (`~/.cache/macextract/discmaster/shrink/`, `manifest.tsv`, TESTING.md) | **the stored CRC-32, both decoders**: the transcription **1,954**, the engine **1,952 of the 1,953 it reaches** (the 12 it does not are `Thief35.zip`'s, of which the transcription fails 10). The 1 both fail is `DEASE.EXE` in `DEASE4-2.ZIP`, which deark and `unar` fail too, all stopping at output byte 26,864 of 330,296; no single-bit flip in the 21,206 compressed bytes before it restores the CRC and there is no second copy: **damaged, a HYPOTHESIS** |
| two bends of the decoder | the free-slot walk made an error fails `shrink-wild-dream.zip` and nothing else; the partial clear made a full one fails `dream` and `titanic` — so each rule has a fixture that needs it |

| fixture | what it pins |
|---|---|
| `shrink-wild-BBA.zip` | the Macintosh-written one; Shrink beside Implode |
| `shrink-wild-dream.zip` | **the one entry in 2,246 that needs the free-slot walk** (`MM.COM`): a partial clear freed the old code, and the next string was defined on it |
| `shrink-wild-titanic.zip` | 13-bit codes, a table filled to slot 8,191, partial clears at every width |

**On a Macintosh since 2026-10-09**: `shrink-wild-titanic.zip` is in
`tools/vmac/sample.txt`, byte-exact on System 3.0 and 6.0.8 at 1 MB and
on both 512Ke tiers, at **38,807 B** of peak heap against 38,777 on
Linux. `dream`, 317 B heavier, is not sampled.

Over the 2,246: 1,798 entries take the KwKwK case, 467 widen, 270 have a
partial clear (PKZIP clears at 9 bits as well as 13), 35 fill the table;
the longest string is 356 B. **UNEXERCISED**: a code naming a free slot
other than the next one (deark accepts it and this follows; 0 in 2,246).
No fuzzer has a Shrink seed.

### What it costs

| | |
|---|---|
| resident `CODE` and A5, SHIPPED | **+0 and +0** |
| the images | `ovl_codecs` 6,404 → 7,520 → 7,628 B, and since 2026-10-11 `ovl_zipold`, with Reduce (*Reduce*); `ovl_exo` 31,936 → 32,036 → 32,056 → **32,084 B** for the method guard and adapter, the tick per partial clear (+76 B) and the peek's Cancel (+20), and the dry passes' progress (+28) (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-08, *the writer a zip is allowed to have twice*) |
| resident CONTROL | `CODE` 167,036 → 168,200 B, A5 −12; **+96 on 2026-10-09** for the tick and the Cancel, 169,784 → 169,880 B, A5 +0; **+28 and +4** the same day when zip's dry passes stopped counting progress, → **169,908 B** (*the bar that counted the peek*, DECISIONS.md 2026-10-09) |
| the download | 228,096 → 229,248 B; 231,424 → 231,552 B on 2026-10-09 (the download today is FORMATS' *Three configurations, one tree*) |
| peak heap per Shrink ENTRY | **3 B per table slot**, sized from the compressed length — 257 + bits/9, at most 8,192 slots, **24,576 B**. `shrink-wild-BBA.zip` peaks at 18,474 B, `dream` at **39,094 B**, `titanic` at 38,777: above deflate's 24,863, inside zip's 12–54 KB row. A separate kind byte made it 4 B a slot and 47,286 B |
| stack | `unc_shrink_decode` is a **120 B** frame since 2026-10-09's tick (116 before) and the Shrink chain **1,672 B**, under the Implode probe's 1,896 (*Stack*). Written in `src/fmt_exo.c` first, it was inlined into `zip_decode` and took that frame 56 → 140 B, which every deflate and Implode chain runs through |
| tier tallies | `run_tiers.py --check` moves none: 71/71, 67/71, 64/71 resident and 71/71, 69/71, 66/71 shipped. **Nor on 2026-10-09** for the tick: 72/72, 68/72, 63/72 resident and 72/72, 70/72, 66/72 shipped, the same fixtures by name; every grant falls 12 B and the three holding `ovl_exo` 32 |
| heap | **+0** for the tick: it allocates nothing |
| speed | the tick costs ~33 68000 instructions a clear: **+0.007 %** on `titanic`, +0.017 % on `dream` |
| speed, 2026-10-11 | **`titanic` 3,573 → 3,099 engine ticks (−13.3 %) and `dream` 1,970 → 1,709 (−13.2 %)** on the tick probe, for +82 B of `unc_shrink_decode`, which the Reduce round's −92 B in the same image paid for (*Reduce*, *Speed*): a code is taken inline when the shared bit reader's window holds its bits. A fully local reader was −268 ticks for +112 B. **Not tried, an ESTIMATE**: a code's string is walked twice (`shr_len`, `shr_put`), the first walk ~8 % of the instructions |

**`ovl_exo` cannot take the decoder**: an image is `-mpcrel` 68000 code
with 16-bit PC-relative references, and with the decoder in
`src/fmt_exo.c` the image link failed (`relocation truncated to fit:
R_68K_PC16 against g_ovl_fns`) at about 33 KB. `ovl_exo` is **30,200 B**
now (`tools/run_tiers.py`), Implode having moved to `ovl_zipold` on
2026-10-17 for the same reason (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-17, *dates in the
Macintosh's own zone*); the exact reach depends on where the linker puts
`g_ovl_fns` (HYPOTHESIS beyond the one failure).

### What a crafted stream costs

**Measured 2026-10-09** in 68000 instructions under `qemu-m68k`
(`tools/m68kprof.py`), priced at **~0.5 M instructions a second**
(`derived-EmpireBuilderDemo.sit`'s 27.1 M for 56 s on a Mac Plus,
`derived-FirstClass_Demo.cpt`'s 42.2 M for 80 s; *Timed on the same Mac
Plus*) — an ESTIMATE good to the order of magnitude. Each crafted stream
is a one-entry zip with a correct stored CRC-32 that deark accepts.

| stream | 68000 instructions | Mac Plus | between ticks, before 2026-10-09 |
|---|---|---|---|
| `shrink-wild-titanic.zip`, 173 KB, for scale | 33.2 M | ~66 s | 8 KB of input |
| 100 KB: the table's top pushed to 8,191 with one-byte codes, then nothing but code 256, 2 at 9 bits — 41,542 partial clears | **~143,500 a clear**, ~6.0 G | **~3.3 hours** | **~17 minutes** |
| 100 KB: 79 chains built round-robin so the shallow nodes fill the low slots, then a clear and one byte code, alternately — 18,357 clears | ~160,000 a clear and its add, ~2.9 G | ~1.6 hours | ~9 minutes |
| either, in an entry with ZipIt's `0x2605` field | **twice** the above | twice | **none at all** in the first pass |

A partial clear walks the table from 257 to the highest slot ever used,
twice, and that slot never comes down, so 18 bits of input buy ~15,900
slot visits (deark's `delzw_partial_clear` does the same; the cost is the
format's). Progress ticked once per 8 KB of INPUT, which is what
`run_ticks.py` grades (TESTING.md, *The progress callback*: it cannot see
how long a gap takes). **Since 2026-10-09 `unc_shrink_decode` ticks after
every partial clear**, so Cancel answers within one clear, **~0.3 s** of a
Mac Plus; the total is unchanged. `test_zip_shrink_ticks` asserts it on
100 clears in 373 bytes and fails without either half. Not done: lowering
the walk's top to the highest slot still in use — a factor of ~3 (~2.8 K
instructions a bit against ~8 K), not a bound.

**The peek decodes up to 8 KB**: `zip_mb_peek` stops at the first EMIT
completing 128 bytes, and Shrink emits per 8 KB (a stream that emits
nothing, whole): +0.12 M instructions on a 961 B entry, +0.62 M on 6 KB,
+0.92 M on 75 KB — **at most ~2 s a ZipIt Shrink entry, and there are 0
in reach** (the 23 `0x2605` fields: 16 deflate, 3 stored, 3 Implode and
`NPWritersKit.zip`'s; `BBA.zip` carries none). The peek ticks through
`c->ps` since 2026-10-09, as `zip_imp_dry` does, and a Cancel ends the
archive before the entry is begun.

## Reduce: zip methods 2–5, PKZIP 0.9x

**In scope since 2026-10-11.** `src/unc_reduce.c` decodes all four
compression factors, from APPNOTE.TXT 5.2 read beside
`reference/deark/foreign/ozunreduce.h` (NOTICE). Reasoning: DECISIONS.md
2026-10-11, *Reduce comes in*. Who wrote it is *Shrink*'s census, above:
13,014 files, none written on a Macintosh. On a Macintosh it is in
`ovl_zipold` with Shrink, the image only zip keeps (*What it costs*).

### What grades it

| what | against |
|---|---|
| the 9 entries of the five fixtures | **deark**, fork for fork on every `ci.sh` run: `unar` refuses every Reduce entry (*"File is not fully supported"*); `"Reduce 1"`–`"Reduce 4"` are in `make_manifests.py`'s `DEARK_METHODS` |
| **2,379** wild entries in **385** archives, 2026-10-11, out of 706 files (the Shrink sweep's 346, every distinct method 2–4 zip in the index (105 + 92 + 13), 150 method-5 zips drawn with `--seed 20261011`); URLs and SHA-256s in `~/.cache/macextract/discmaster/reduce/manifest.tsv` (TESTING.md, *Searching somebody else's index by content*) | **the stored CRC-32**: a Python transcription **2,371 of 2,379** (by factor 1–4: 628/629, 654/658, 113/114, 976/978), the C decoder the same entries file by file. **The 8 are deark's failures too**: 3 PHP `compress_unsup*.zip` test files, 2 archives cut before their central directory, 3 CRC-bad in deark — **damaged, a HYPOTHESIS**; those 3 are the only entries with a copy longer than its distance, decoded as APPNOTE.TXT says, CRC failing either way |
| four bends of the decoder | window filled with 0xAA, or `144 0` emitted as 0: all five fixtures fail; a copy longer than its distance copied backwards, or the index-past-count refusal removed: `test_zip_reduce_rules` fails. The last two are in no CRC-good entry, so only the unit test can fail on them |
| **3,000 mutants**, `fuzz.py --stream-only --stream-shape reduce --seed 11`, ASan and UBSan | **0 failures** (TESTING.md, *Fuzzing*) |

| fixture | factor | what it pins |
|---|---|---|
| `reduce-wild-checksum.zip` | 1 (method 2) | 91 literal 144s, 5 long copies (V's low bits all set, so a length byte follows) |
| `reduce-wild-2048acad.zip` | 2 (method 3) | each rule at least 3 times in 1,893 B |
| `reduce-wild-america.zip` | 3 (method 4) | 50 literal 144s, 29 long copies |
| `reduce-wild-fractlan.zip` | 4 (method 5) | 14 copies reaching before the first output byte, 75 long ones |
| `synth-exo-zip-reduce.zip` | all four | `tools/synth_reduce.py`, the only Reduce WRITER here: one 5,785 B body per factor, each with a copy into the zeros before the start, 9 literal 144s, long copies and follower-set misses. deark reads all four byte-exact |

**1,480 of the 2,371 good entries reach before the first output byte**,
into the zeros APPNOTE.TXT 5.2.5 says are there. An index past a follower
set's count is refused (5.2.4 defines none; `ozur` gives 0); **no wild
entry has one**.

**On a Macintosh, 2026-10-11** (sixteen-image build): the five fixtures,
the three Shrink ones and `4alnch11.zip` byte-exact on System 3.0 and
System 6.0.8, each at 1 MB and on a 512Ke (`run.py`, `sys30.py`, Mini
vMac); `tools/vmac/sample.txt` **74/74** on System 6.0.8 at 1 MB; a 549 KB
wild specimen at 4 MB. Peak heap 21,805–21,978 B against 21,775–21,948 on
Linux. None of the five is in the sample, so the tier tallies do not
count them.

### Speed

**Tick probe** (`run.py --emu mv-4M-1x -D UNC_AT_TIMING`, 7 MHz Mac Plus),
a 156 KB wild archive holding 549 KB of factor-2 and factor-4 entries:
**4,533 ticks (75.6 s)** with the first decoder, **2,136 ticks (35.6 s)**
with the rewrite, −53 % (File Manager 113 and 98 ticks of those).
`tools/m68kprof.py`: 40.8 M → 22.6 M instructions; **43 % of the first
decoder's instructions were the shared bit reader**, now two locals topped
up from the reader's window, and B(N) a 33-byte table. CRC-32 is a quarter
of what is left.

**A second round, kept only where nothing else grew** (engine ticks,
excluding the File Manager):

| change | image | Reduce, 549 KB out | taken |
|---|---|---|---|
| A — the window top-up adds ONE byte when fewer than 9 bits remain, not up to three: the register-count shift then never exceeds 8 | −4 B | 2,037 → 2,023 | yes |
| B — one `left` countdown for the position and the size | −8 B | 2,023 → 2,027, **slower**, twice | no |
| C — the copy loop on two cursors and an end pointer, `(An)+` where it was double-indexed, as `fmt_cpt.c` does | +4 B | 2,023 → 1,961 | yes |
| D — the follower sets, read once per entry, through the reader proper alone, without two inlined top-ups | −92 B | 1,961 → 1,960 | yes |
| E — the index mask from a table rather than a register-count shift | +37 B | 1,960 → 1,963 | no |
| a shared `UNC_NOINLINE` flush for the three call sites | +40 B | not run | no |

**Reduce 2,037 → 1,957 engine ticks, −3.9 %; a second archive (579 KB,
factor 2) 3,009 → 2,947, −2.1 %**, against HEAD built in its own worktree.
The three taken are −92 B together; the frame 84 → 88 B. What is left is
register pressure: at `-Os` GCC moves the copy destination from a data to
an address register every byte; a rewrite with fewer live values (+34 B)
bought 63 ticks, not counted as free.

### What it costs

Measured 2026-10-11, at the commit; later commits moved every absolute
figure (current ones: *Three configurations, one tree*).

| | |
|---|---|
| resident `CODE` and A5, SHIPPED | **+80 and +16 B** (54,720 and 19,104), all for the sixteenth image, not for Reduce: the release's fourth keep, the zip shim patching `ovl_zipold` into the vector, a fourth keep slot plus `_sdata` padding (`tools/build_mac.sh`). The lazy pair +56 and +8 |
| the images | **`ovl_zipold`, 2,658 B** (Shrink, moved unchanged from `src/unc_lzw.c` to `src/unc_shrink.c`, and Reduce; 10,832 B now that Implode and the Mac OS X sidecars share it, `tools/run_tiers.py` `IMAGES`). `ovl_codecs` 7,628 → **6,404 B**; `ovl_exo` 32,084 → **32,160 B**. Bundle 162,064 → **163,588 B**; download 231,808 → **233,472 B** |
| the heap a format is granted, ON THE MACHINE | **+1,128 B** for every format that keeps the codecs image and not `ovl_exo`, **+1,052 B** for tar, **−1,614 B** for zip, **−1,620 B** for an envelope. No tally moved (`run_tiers.py --check`: 74/74, 72/74, 68/74 shipped, the same fixtures by name) |
| resident CONTROL | `CODE` 170,056 → **171,224 B**, A5 37,960 → **38,008 B** (alignment); `OVL_SAVED` → **135,408 B**; `TIERS` lowered 1,216 B by derivation |
| peak heap per Reduce ENTRY | **8,448 B** of follower sets (256 sets of up to 32 bytes plus a count each), a constant kept on purpose: the window is the zip reader's own output buffer, and the entry peaks at **21.8–21.9 KB**, under deflate's and Shrink's. The sets use a median of 359 of their 8,192 value slots over the 2,379 entries, at most 7,936 (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-11, *Reduce comes in*) |
| stack | `unc_reduce_decode` an **88 B** frame; the chain `unc_zip_extract` (1,464) → `zip_decode` (56) → `unc_reduce_decode` → `zip_put` (0) → `exo_emit` (32) **1,640 B**, under Shrink's 1,672 and the Implode probe's 1,896 (*Stack*) |

## What the zip pair claims, and what it costs

**Measured 2026-10-05.** A zip behind a self-extracting stub is recognised
because the backwards scan it needs was **already run on every file the
sniffer rejects** (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-05, *the scan that was already
being paid for*).

### What the backwards scan already cost, before any of this

`tools/sniff_io_probe.c` counts `UncIO.read` calls and bytes for
`unc_sniff` alone (on a Macintosh each call is a `SetFPos` + `FSRead`
pair). Over the **955 corpus files**, the scan in `unc_zipseg_probe`
disabled as the control:

| | reads | bytes |
|---|---|---|
| a whole sniff of the corpus | 19,025 | 13,456,817 |
| of which the end-of-central-directory scan | **9,519 (50.0 %)** | **9,605,702 (71.4 %)** |

Per file reaching it: **44.8 reads and 45,271 bytes for a BinHex fixture**
(204 of them), 50.0 and 50,678 for a tar, 36.2 and 36,459 for an ARC. A
file claimed by an earlier magic number pays nothing.

### What recognising the archive behind a stub adds to that

| population | verdict changes | reads | bytes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 955 corpus fixtures | **0** | +10 | +40 |
| 8,364 files that are not Macintosh archives¹ | 6 (below) | +125 | +30,740 |
| …of those, the **7,601 the sniffer rejects** | 0 | **+0** | **+0** |
| …of those, the 757 it claims | 0 | +4 | +16 |

¹ `reference/`, this repository's trees and the emulator's specimen cache,
swept with both binaries over identical bytes.

New is one four-byte read of the central directory, paid only by a file
whose record corroborates. The six changed verdicts: **one gained**,
`zipit-MrTs_Paint.sea` (the ZipIt self-extractor without a MacBinary
wrapper), and **five withdrawn** — four `.pyc` files and one deark `.o`
that the segment probe had called spanned-zip parts on four chance bytes
(BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*); those five walking the rest of the
probe list are the whole of the +125.

### 68000 instructions

`qemu-m68k` through `tools/m68kprof.py`, the `-Os -mcpu=68000` build
(TESTING.md, *The 68000 profile*). Instructions, not cycles:

| sniffing | before | after | |
|---|---|---|---|
| the 10 zip fixtures | 145,879 | 151,243 | **+5,364, +536 per zip, +3.7 %** |
| 12 BinHex fixtures | 8,414,926 | 8,414,855 | **−71, nothing** |

Run-to-run spread ~83 instructions, so +5,364 is real and −71 is not. No
tick probe: one `t` of a 440 KB zip is 17,518,142 instructions, so +536 is
0.003 % of an extraction.

### Resident memory

The probe is resident in **every** configuration (the sniffer calls it for
every file); 2026-10-05, before the sniffer had an image of its own:

| build | `CODE` | A5 |
|---|---|---|
| eager — SHIPPED | 54,012 → **54,328** (+316) | 19,060 → 19,064 (+4) |
| lazy | 52,588 → 52,904 (+316) | 19,148 → 19,144 (−4) |
| resident — the control | 163,744 → **164,060** (+316) | 37,920 → 37,924 (+4) |

**320 B off every extraction's budget on a 1 MB Mac Plus** then.
`MacExtract.code.bin` 65,664 → 66,048 B; `src/fmt_zipseg.c` 628 → 916 B
of text. `run_tiers.py --check` moved no tally. Since 2026-10-21 the probe
is in `ovl_sniff` (*Overlays*).

### What the claim rests on, and what each test rejects

Four bytes of `PK\5\6` are not evidence. `zs_cd_is_there` requires five
things of the record, four arithmetic on fields already read and one a
four-byte read:

| the test | APPNOTE.TXT | what it rejected here |
|---|---|---|
| at least one entry | 4.4.22 | 2 of the 5 chance signatures |
| no more entries on this part than in the whole directory | 4.4.21 vs 4.4.22 | 2 of the 5 |
| the directory does not start on a LATER part than this one | 4.4.20 vs 4.4.19 | 1 of the 5 |
| ≥ 46 bytes of directory per entry, and ≥ 46 per entry that is on THIS part in front of the record | 4.3.12, 4.3.6 | a junk record in `tests/unit_formats.c` |
| and, where the directory is on this part, `PK\1\2` at the offset the record gives | 4.3.12 | the three MacBinary-WRAPPED ZipIt specimens, whose offsets are 128 B short of the file, and a stub glued in front of an archive `zip -A` was never run on |

**The entry COUNT is not walked**: 4.3.13 lets a digital-signature record
follow the headers, and on a 74-entry ZipIt specimen it would cost ~74
reads where the whole sniff costs 4. The ≥ 46-bytes row is the same claim
as a lower bound, at zero reads.

**Two tests are conditional because Info-ZIP `zip -s` splits the central
directory**: measured 2026-10-05 at 1,200 entries and 64 KB per part —
disk 7 of 8, the directory starting on disk 6, 132,048 bytes declared, 238
of the 1,201 entries in the 26,312-byte file holding the record.
`tools/synth_zipsplit.py`'s `zspancd` case is that shape, in `ci.sh`.

### What is graded

| what | against |
|---|---|
| all five ZipIt self-extractors | `tools/run_zipit.py`, **9 of 9 sniffed unaided** where it was 4 of 9, every fork still byte-identical to `unar`; and the **four that arrived MacBinary-wrapped extracted WHOLE**, the route a user takes, against their data fork's extraction |
