| each of the five tests, bent one at a time | `test_zip_behind_a_stub` in `tests/unit_formats.c`, on a synthetic archive behind a `Joy!peff` stub. 5 of its checks fail on the engine of the day before |
| a chance `PK\5\6` in something that is not an archive | the same test, four disk numbers; three of the four were claimed as a segment before |
| a spanned set whose directory crosses a part | `tools/synth_zipsplit.py`'s `zspancd`, in `tools/run_segments.py` — graded against `unar`, against the source tree and against the same content zipped unsplit |


## Overlays: what the application ships, and what it costs

**The decoders and ciphers (since 2026-09-25) and the sniffer with its
probes (since 2026-10-21) are not resident.** They are seventeen flat,
position-independent images in `'OVLY'` resources; the shipped build reads
all seventeen as ONE bundle at launch, sniffs from it, and releases
everything the sniffed format cannot want — the sniffer's image first —
before the engine takes its budget. Mechanism `mac/ovl_load_eager.c`,
images built by `tools/overlay/`; why it is the build rather than a branch:
DECISIONS.md 2026-09-25, *two branches that were one file apart*; the
seventeenth image: DECISIONS.md 2026-10-21, *the probes that left with the
bundle*.

### Three configurations, one tree

| build | switch | resident `CODE` | A5 | `MacExtract.code.bin` | `MacExtract.bin` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **eager — SHIPPED** | (default) | **38,588 B** | 16,484 B | **46,336 B** | **237,568 B** |
| lazy | `UNC_OVL_LAZY=1` | 37,824 B | 16,544 B | 45,568 B | 236,800 B |
| resident — the control | `UNC_OVL_RESIDENT=1` | **177,732 B** | **36,476 B** | 208,000 B | 214,912 B |

Read off the builds 2026-10-25 (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-25, *the review
before the release*; the `MacExtract.bin` column includes the archive
document icon, *the archives that drew as the application*). `CODE`
excludes the MacsBug names, stripped since 2026-10-22 (*the names in the
code*); A5 is data + bss in the heap since 2026-10-22's runtime trim (*the
runtime the application did not need*). The ceilings, and what each
change cost them entry by entry, are `tools/build_mac.sh`'s ratchet
records; this table has gone stale behind them several times, so read the
builds, not this table, before quoting it.

Re-takes that measured something beyond the ratchet deltas:

- **2026-10-22, the runtime the application did not need**: eager −2,552 B
  `CODE` and −1,304 A5. The machine gave more, **+4,980 B to every shipped
  budget and +4,204 B to the control's** (+4,186 on System 3.0), read off
  the budget probe on both builds on all three tiers: the 816-byte jump
  table, six 80-byte stub segments and 1,368 B of relocations were resident
  and never in this table. `OVL_RESIDUE` −666 → −322.
- **2026-10-22, the names in the code**: eager −4,416 B `CODE`, the
  control −10,180; the seventeen images sum 192,066 → 179,276 B. A
  format's layer gained between **+5,076 B** (AppleLink package) and
  **+7,552 B** (an encrypted zip), **+17,176 B** for an envelope. Shipped
  74/74, 72/74, 68/74, the same by name on the machine.
- **2026-10-21, the probes that left with the bundle**: eager −9,284 B
  `CODE`, −364 A5; `ovl_sniff` **13,384 B**. A format's layer **+9,616 B**
  where the release keeps images, **−3,780 B** where it keeps the bundle
  whole (an envelope, a split set's first layer). On the machine 74/74,
  72/74, 68/74, the same fixtures by name. `synth-dd-m5.dd` read "98 B
  short" at System 6.0.8 / 512Ke; **CORRECTED 2026-10-22: a lower bound,
  not a distance** — the lane reports the FIRST refused allocation; the
  machine's peak for it is 401,408 B (*512 KB machines*) against a 235,142
  B grant.
- **2026-10-05, AppleLink packages**: the first format since the overlays
  whose shipped column moved by more than eight bytes, **+788 `CODE` and
  +60 A5**, for its 666-byte resident PROBE (the decoder, 1,474 B, is in
  `ovl_sqp`) (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-05, *the probe that had to be a file
  test*). The probe moved to `ovl_sniff` on 2026-10-21.
- **2026-10-06**: a 4 B text change cost the lazy build **+28 B of A5**,
  `*fill*` under `. = ALIGN (0x20)` at the head of `.data` — the
  rodata-alignment step recorded in `tools/build_mac.sh`, stepping the
  wrong way in one configuration (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-06, *the directory
  of empty records*).

**What a FORMAT's own code costs the shipped build: measured 2026-09-29,
and it is nothing.** A 5,166-byte blob added to `src/fmt_sit.c` moved the
eager build's `CODE` and A5 by **0 and 0**; it landed in `ovl_stuffit.bin`
(18,582 → 23,754 B) and the bundle; only the control grew (152,068 →
157,252), and `run_tiers.py --check` held every tally by name. **Decoder
code inside one format's overlay is free to every archive not of that
format**; to price the next one, compile a blob of its size into the
format's file and read the six numbers off. Zip's Macintosh extra fields
(+2,024 B control, +0 shipped, 2026-10-01), Implode (+1,856 / +0,
2026-10-07, DECISIONS.md 2026-10-07, *the method that was out for a premise
with no halves left*) and StuffIt method 14 (+2,624 / +0, 2026-10-03) are
the same measurement on real formats.

`ci.sh` compiles all three configurations since 2026-09-26 (TESTING.md,
*The three Macintosh configurations*).

**What the harness builds is none of those three, and what the probe
builds is a fourth** — every budget figure in this file is read off a
`UNC_MAC_AUTOTEST` build with `-D UNC_AT_BUDGET`, the only way a budget
reaches the screen (`tools/vmac/README.md`, *Why an entry is refused*).
Measured 2026-09-27:

| build | `CODE` | A5 | over the shipped column above |
|---|---|---|---|
| autotest, eager | 47,564 B | 21,940 B | −2,320 B |
| autotest, eager, `-D UNC_AT_BUDGET` | 47,840 B | 22,064 B | −1,920 B |
| autotest, resident | 145,560 B | 40,760 B | −2,544 B against the control |
| autotest, resident, `-D UNC_AT_BUDGET` | 145,776 B | 40,848 B | −2,240 B against the control |

**The probe cost the eager configuration 400 B and the control 304 B**
(2026-09-27), and every budget here is that much lower than the build
whose tallies it is quoted beside. It cancels in a difference taken from
one probe pair (*What it buys, against the control*): **117,040 B saved
between the shipped pair against 116,720 B between the probe pair**, both
2026-09-27. The shipped pair is **159,136 B** (`OVL_SAVED`,
`tools/run_tiers.py`) since 2026-10-25 (*the review before the release*); the
probe pair has not been re-measured, so the 320 B is that day's
difference, not today's.

The bundle is **184,160 B** — the seventeen images sum to 183,942 B, so its
index costs **218 B** (`tools/run_tiers.py` `BUNDLE`, `IMAGES`): a
4-byte header, 12 bytes of directory per image and padding of each image to
a multiple of 4 — the padding is why the index is not a constant. The
bundle is not `ovl_bundle.r.rsrc.bin`, the Rez'd resource fork around it
(DECISIONS.md 2026-09-26, *the fork that was mistaken for its contents*).
It is not resident while an archive is extracted, so
`tools/check_app_size.py` watches `MacExtract.code.bin` (`EXPECT`
**46,336 B**) and only warns on the bundle.

**The launch READ is not a new cost** (DECISIONS.md 2026-09-26, *the fork
that was mistaken for its contents*). Figures of 2026-10-06, not
re-derived from the resource map since: a resident build's whole `CODE`,
read at launch by the Segment Loader, was 196,608 B; the shipped build read
66,400 B of `CODE` (2026-10-06) plus the **167,208 B** bundle,
233,608 B; the terms are quoted and the subtraction left to the reader,
because the difference went stale twice when only one term was updated.

### What it buys, against the control

The number is what the engine is GRANTED — `mal_take_budget` at its first
allocation, every image already loaded. **Measured 2026-09-27 at 1 MB on
System 6.0.8, one tree, one probe pair** (`run.py --mem 1M -D UNC_AT_BUDGET`,
with and without `-D UNC_OVL_RESIDENT`):

| the archive | sniffed as | eager (shipped) | over the control |
|---|---|---|---|
| `derived-Export.sit`, `derived-KeyGraphics.sit` | StuffIt, StuffIt 5 | 735,676 B | **+82,144 B** |
| `lhasa-unix114i-h2_lh7.lzh`, `aminet-lha_68k.lha` | LZH | 753,918 B | **+100,386 B** |
| `derived-keyAhead.cpt` | Compact Pro, nothing encrypted | 747,702 B | **+94,170 B** |
| `synth-dd-m3.dd` | DiskDoubler | 747,702 B | +94,170 B |
| `synth-exo-zip-store.zip` | Zip, nothing encrypted | 736,234 B | **+82,702 B** |
| `synth-exo-tar.tar` | Tar | 736,234 B | +82,702 B |
| `synth-arc-mixed.arc` | ARC | 745,558 B | **+92,026 B** |
| `synth-packit-1.pit` | PackIt | 751,030 B | **+97,498 B** |
| `derived-Expand_Now_1.0.1.sea` | Now Compress | 758,548 B | **+105,016 B** |
| `synth-exo-gz.gz`, `export-hc.hqx` | gzip, BinHex — an envelope | 626,826 B | **−26,706 B** |

**The control is 653,532 B for every row** (three LZH archives at this tier,
two formats at System 6.0.8 / 512 KB): with every decoder linked the heap does
not depend on the archive, so the gain belongs to the mechanism — which is why
the resident build is kept, as an instrument. The arithmetic of *What each
format is granted* accounts for all ten rows to the byte with one additive
constant; re-taken after the AppleLink probe's removal, every gain was
unchanged while each grant rose the same 536 B as the control (DECISIONS.md
2026-09-27, *the residue that was two numbers*).

**Eager against lazy is 894 B–10.7 KB** of budget for a format that keeps the
ciphers, against no disk access after the archive is chosen (measured
2026-09-24; the lazy lifetime is not shipped and not re-measured). Eager ships
because on a one-drive Mac Plus the application's floppy may be out by then.

### What each format is granted, against the control

A tier's budget for a format is the resident build's budget for that tier
(`TIERS`; *512 KB machines*, *Floor target verification*), plus
the 159,136 B of `CODE` and A5 the shipped build does not carry (`OVL_SAVED`;
*Three configurations, one tree*), less the images the format keeps and 8 B of
block header each, less a **322 B residue** (`OVL_RESIDUE`, re-taken
2026-10-22 from the shipped and resident budget probes on all three tiers;
DECISIONS.md 2026-10-22, *the runtime the application did not need*).
`run_tiers.gain()` computes it and `tools/run_tiers.py --shipped` applies it
(TESTING.md, *The tiers a budget predicts*; DECISIONS.md 2026-09-26, *a budget
per format, and one per layer*).

The model's `.Z` budget lands within 40 B of the machine's own — ONE data
point, `a2ps.V4.3.tar.Z` on System 6.0.8 / 512Ke, itself derived: 81,938 B
(a2ps's 86,080 B peak less the 4,142 B shortfall it was refused by) against the
model's 81,978. **"It decides nothing at a narrowest margin of 16,490 B" —
WITHDRAWN 2026-10-09**: the bases were the CONTROL build's, 16,888 B stale, and
a fixture was refused (*Budget*; DECISIONS.md 2026-10-09, *the base nobody
re-took*).

The table equals `run_tiers.gain()` to the byte (checked 2026-10-25). Rows
marked *(measured)* are formats whose TALLY the machine confirmed at that
budget (a fixture is in the Mac sample); the rest are the same sum with no
fixture. Every row has since moved by arithmetic only — each application or
image change since 2026-10-09 shifts `OVL_SAVED`, the images and `TIERS` by
amounts recorded in its DECISIONS.md entry and in `tools/run_tiers.py`'s
comments, last on 2026-10-24 (*the format before BinHex*), with
`run_tiers.py --check` moving no tally.

| sniffed format | images kept | granted, against the control |
|---|---|---|
| **AppleLink package** | **sqp, codecs** | **+151,046 B** (arithmetic: no `.pkg` is in the Mac sample) — the largest grant, because `ovl_sqp` is the smallest of the seventeen images at 1,736 B |
| Now Compress | now, codecs | **+148,014 B** (measured) |
| SuperDisk! | superdisk, codecs | +146,148 B (arithmetic: no SuperDisk! fixture is in the Mac sample) |
| LZH | lzh, codecs | **+142,324 B** (measured) |
| PackIt | packit, ciphers, codecs | **+140,990 B** (measured) |
| DiskDoubler | cptdd, codecs | **+137,638 B** (measured) |
| StuffIt SpaceSaver | spacesaver, codecs | +136,614 B (arithmetic: no SpaceSaver fixture is in the Mac sample) |
| ARC | arc, ciphers, codecs | **+135,474 B** (measured) |
| Compact Pro, Compact Pro segment | cptdd, ciphers, codecs | +128,026 B (arithmetic), and **+137,638 B** where the directory says no entry is encrypted (measured) |
| Tar | zipold, codecs | **+141,950 B** (measured) |
| StuffIt, StuffIt 5 | stuffit, ciphers, codecs | **+114,976 B** (measured) |
| Zip, spanned zip | exo, zipold, ciphers, codecs | +102,130 B (arithmetic), **+111,742 B** unencrypted (measured) |
| every envelope, and every segment scheme that joins to a file | the whole bundle, kept | **−25,354 B** (measured) |

A change to resident code moves every row by the control's growth less the
shipped build's; a change to an image moves only the rows that keep it — so a
format whose own image absorbs a feature can fall while the rest rise
(zip method 6, 2026-10-07: the `ovl_exo` rows −68 B against +1,856 elsewhere;
DECISIONS.md 2026-10-07, *the method that was out for a premise with no halves
left*).

**The last row is the one no tally had shown**: a `.hqx`, `.gz`, `.Z` or
MacBinary wrapper does not know what is under it, so the bundle stays and that
layer runs with **less** memory than a resident build would give it —
184,160 B held against 159,136 B saved. It costs nothing on the tiers because
the layer under the envelope gets its own budget a moment later (*What an
archive keeps*), and the envelope's own decode is small: the heaviest in the
Mac sample is `popper-1.7.tar.Z`'s LZW at 147,764 B, which clears the
System 3.0 / 512 KB envelope budget of 173,572 B by 25,808 B (arithmetic from
`TIERS` and `gain()`, CORRECTED 2026-10-25 from 194,686 and 46,922 B).

### Where the traps are written down

Each cost a day or a silent failure, and each now lives in the code that
embodies it:

| trap | where |
|---|---|
| `-mpcrel` does NOT make an image position-independent — arrays of pointers in constant data hold link-time addresses, and a blob loaded elsewhere dereferences them into nowhere | `tools/overlay/mkreloctrailer.py`, and `ovl_fixup` in `mac/ovl_reloc.c` |
| gcc emits calls to `memcpy`/`memset` that appear in no source file, and an image links no libc | `tools/overlay/ovl_rt.c`, dropped by `--gc-sections` when unused; `mkoverlay.sh` prints `runtime: memcpy` beside an image that took one |
| an image's resource id written in three places, two of which can disagree with no build error | `tools/overlay/ovlid.py` — every id comes from `overlay.h` BY NAME, and a NUMBER is refused |
| an image rule naming its own sources and none of the headers they include, so a header edit rebuilds the application and leaves the images stale | `OVL_DEPS` plus a `CONFIGURE_DEPENDS` header glob in `mac/CMakeLists.txt` |
| a kept image locked in the middle of the heap the released bundle just vacated | `MoveHHi` before the `HLock` in `ovl_keep` (BLOCKERS.md, *Corrected figures*) |
| executable bytes written as data and then executed | `ovl_flush_code` in `mac/ovl_reloc.c` (DECISIONS.md 2026-09-25, *the flush nobody wrote*) |

### What an archive keeps

An envelope keeps everything: a `.sit.hqx` sniffs as BinHex and what is under
it is unknown until unwrapped; the second layer gets the memory back when the
unwrapped file is opened as a new layer. Everything else keeps its own decoder,
the codecs image, and the ciphers only where an entry might be encrypted — for
Compact Pro and zip decided by walking the directory first
(`unc_archive_encrypted`), for everything else YES by default, because the
release happens before any entry is read.

## Resident memory, stack and launch (Mac target)

Measured on the Retro68 target's own flags (`-mcpu=68000 -Os
-ffunction-sections`, zlib with the `Z_TESTW=0` and `NO_GZIP` its CMake
sets), summed per object, **re-measured 2026-09-23**:

| | code | rodata |
|---|---|---|
| engine (`src/*.c`) | 116,432 | 20,977 |
| zlib | 8,882 | 4,301 |
| Mac shell | 17,066 | 2,183 |
| **total** | **142,380** | **27,461** |

Summing per object overstates what ships, because `--gc-sections` drops what
nothing calls; the linked figure is `tools/code_map.py`'s, below. (The rodata
half was wrong 2026-09-19 to 09-23: it still counted `unc_crypt.c`'s three
2,048-byte tables after they moved to key setup — *Where the code is*.)

The application ships as **237,568 B** of MacBinary (`dist/MacExtract.bin`,
`ls -l` 2026-10-25; it read 232,320 B on 2026-10-22). **46,336 B** is
`MacExtract.code.bin`, read at launch and never purged, and **184,160 B** is
the `'OVLY'` bundle, read at launch and mostly given back before the engine
takes its budget (*Overlays*). What stays costs the heap one for one: the
link produces a single `MacExtract.code.bin`, and `Elf2Mac/LdScript.cc`
emits `*(.rodata*)` inside `.data`, so it is never purgeable.

**A month of growth (+10,112 B, 2026-08-28 → 09-23, all still resident)
cost no tally** — measured by moving the budget in `tools/run_tiers.py`
rather than the binary: 10,112 B larger gives the same **71, 67 and 64**;
10,112 B smaller drops System 6.0.8 / 512 KB to 63. The narrowest passing
fixture then, `derived-LHA_Expander_1.0.3.sea`, needed **116,784 B**
(bisected 2026-09-27) against that tier's 129,244 — **12,460 B of
headroom**, which is why `tools/check_app_size.py` fails CI on a 2 KB move.
At 2026-10-09's re-taken 110,332 the resident lane refuses it, 6,452 B short
(*Budget*). Formats added that month peak at 5 KB (base64) and 8 KB (MIME)
(DECISIONS.md 2026-09-23, *the ten kilobytes nothing watched*).

The five largest objects (2026-09-23): `macextract_mac.c` (17,066 + 2,183),
`fmt_spacesaver.c` (6,292 + 9,712, its rodata almost all `ss_dict.h`),
`fmt_sit.c` (5,804 + 3,473), `fmt_dd.c` (8,672 + 12) and `unc_crypt.c`
(7,158 + 1,432).

As the LINKER places it (`tools/code_map.py`, 2026-09-23): 153,180 B of
CODE — decoders 91,730 (59.9 %) across 24 files, shell 17,956, shared codec
machinery 13,118, engine core and util 9,846, ciphers 7,666, deflate 7,442,
Retro68 runtime 5,422 — plus 25,962 B of read-only tables in the A5 world.

### Size flags, priced in both currencies (2026-08-30)

`gTkRun` from `-D UNC_AT_TIMING` on `derived-EmpireBuilderDemo.sit` at
authentic 7 MHz; three runs per build gave identical ticks, so one run
prices a configuration.

| flag | code | ticks | |
|---|---|---|---|
| baseline | 148,048 B | 3,158 (52.63 s) | |
| `-fno-tree-loop-distribute-patterns` | −440 B | +1 | kept |
| `-freorder-blocks-algorithm=simple` | −336 B | **+26** | kept; the whole cost |
| `-fipa-icf` | 0 B | 0 | kept: ~12 B in combination only |
| `-fno-early-inlining` | **+8 B** | **+2** | rejected, worse both ways |
| the three kept | 147,268 B | 3,184 (53.07 s) | **−780 B for +0.82 %** |

The 780 B carried the budget to 566,164 B and brought the corpus's three
heaviest archives (566,009 B) inside a 1 MB Mac Plus by 155 B; without
`-freorder-blocks-algorithm=simple` they did not fit (DECISIONS.md
2026-08-30, *the flag that was never measured on these files*). An 8-byte
code change (147,260 vs 147,268 B) measured the same 566,164 B budget: heap
allocation is granular, so do not read single-digit byte differences as
budget.

### Where the bytes are, and why the flags are finished

**MEASURED 2026-08-31.** **147,276 B** of code sections against **32,568 B**
of A5 world. Summed function symbols are **138,686 B**; the remaining
~8.6 KB is Retro68's startup section and padding.

| function | bytes |
|---|---|
| `ExtractOne.constprop.0` (shell) | 5,884 |
| `dd_fork` | 5,136 |
| `unc_spacesaver_extract` | 4,154 |
| `unc_zip_extract` | 3,492 |
| `unc_sit_decode_fork` | 2,478 |
| `unc_arsenic_decode` | 2,344 |

None is a switch worth flattening: `dd_fork`'s 5,136 B is eleven method
bodies inlined because each is called once, and un-inlining them
(`-fno-inline-functions-called-once`) measured **+2,444 B**. The top 25
functions are ~55 KB of 138 KB, so there is no single structural win. The
`UNC_HOT` set (`sit_lzw`, `sit_huffman`, `unc_huff_decode`,
`unc_canon_decode`, `lzw_code`, `lzw_chain`, `unc_lzw_read`,
`unc_crc16_arc`) appears nowhere in the table: the bytes and the time are in
disjoint functions.

### Launch

**A kilobyte of code is not a kilobyte of launch time; a RELOCATION is.**
Ballast compiled in behind a temporary define and referenced from the
startup path; three runs each on `mv-4M-1x` (authentic 7 MHz), System 6.0.8,
booting straight into the application:

| build | app | relocations | boot |
|---|---|---|---|
| shipping | 150,144 B | 2,376 | **8.9 s** |
| + 64 KB of `const unsigned char[]` | 215,680 B | 2,376 | **8.9 s** |
| + 64 KB as 16,384 pointers | 232,064 B | 18,682 | **10.0 s** |

64 KB without relocations is below the harness's 0.1 s resolution; 16,306
more relocations cost 1.1 s, about **67 µs each**. The application's 2,376
cost ≈ **0.16 s of an 8.9 s boot**. Hence the CODE resource is not segmented
(BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*), and moving data tables out of the A5 world
would buy nothing at launch.

### What the reserve is for, measured at last

**The reserve's job is CONTIGUITY, not volume** — `GetNewDialog` needs one
block. `-D UNC_AT_CONTIG` samples `MaxBlock()` as each dialog and alert is
created (2026-08-31):

| run | smallest contiguous block at any dialog | dialogs |
|---|---|---|
| password path, **512 KB** machine (122,808 B free heap) | **118,508 B** | 3 |
| report path, 1 MB | **652,112 B** | 1 |
| segmented set, 1 MB — the mid-run "locate part N" alert | **637,918 B** | 4 |

Essentially the whole free heap every time: no dialog is created while the
engine holds a big allocation (progress goes up before extraction; password
and "locate part" open between entries). **So the reserve is 20 KB**, 1.5x
the **13,242 B** the shell and Toolbox have ever been measured using; the
12,288 B released went to the engine (*Budget*). On a heavy 1 MB extraction
(`derived-Troi-ClipSave-Plug-in1.1.sit`, engine peak 472,137 B) the shell's
share at the tightest moment measured **−33 B**, i.e. nothing beyond the
engine within sampling skew. Verified byte-exact at that size on all four
tiers; current tallies are BLOCKERS.md's tier table.

**Not proven:** every figure comes from an emulator with a stripped System
Folder, no INITs and a RAM-backed disk. A real Plus with a Chooser
extension, floppy File Manager buffers or AppleShare costs more volume;
MANUAL_TESTS.md carries it.

### The Linux budget check reads the Mac to 40 bytes

**`--budget N` on Linux predicts the machine to within 40 B.** MEASURED
2026-09-18, each fixture capped on a 1 MB Mac Plus at **562,000 B** — 559 B
above its bisected Linux minimum of 561,441 B:

| fixture | Linux peak at 562,000 | Mac peak at 562,000 | verdict | extract, all-out |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `derived-XMenu.dmg.sit` | 561,882 B | 561,922 B | byte-exact | 704 s |
| `derived-EasyFind.dmg.sit` | 561,879 B | 561,919 B | byte-exact | 1,427 s |

The 40 B is the Mac allocator's 16 B per live block less what 32-bit
pointers save in the engine's structs — a constant, the same one *Floor
target verification* reports. At the 648,100 B budget of the day XMenu read
631,644 against 631,684 B. The earlier claim of a 16–62 KB, non-constant gap
is withdrawn: it read the run's first (expected) refusal and a full 1,440 KB
Out floppy (BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*; DECISIONS.md 2026-09-18, *the
floppy that was full*).

**The proxy cannot predict TIME.** A fixture capped near its minimum sits on
a low rung of the sampled index: EasyFind at 562,000 B is 17 s on Linux and
1,427 s on the emulator at all-out speed. Raise `--timeout`, and give the
Out volume room (`--out-kb`).

### What the application costs the engine

**Code and the A5 world both cost the engine, one for one; only RESOURCES are
free.** MEASURED 2026-09-02:

| ballast | budget |
|---|---|
| none | 647,802 B |
| **+16,384 B of `.data`** | 631,384 B |

16,384 B of data cost 16,418 B of budget: the A5 world sat above
`ApplLimit`, lowering the heap's ceiling by its own size. Since 2026-10-22
`--mac-single` puts data inside `CODE` 1 and bss in a heap block, which
costs the same bytes by another road (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-22, *the runtime
the application did not need*). What is free is a resource that is not
loaded — a `DITL`, an `ICN#`, an `OVLY` blob nobody has asked for. The
2026-08-30 measurement that said data was free is withdrawn (BLOCKERS.md):
`--gc-sections` had dropped its ballast. **`nm` the binary that actually
ran.**

    budget ≈ 708,100 - code - A5      (autotest build, 1 MB, System 6.0.8)

**So put material in RESOURCES**: a table in C costs its size on every
extraction, `const` or not. The `tools/build_mac.sh` ratchet watches code
AND data (data only since 2026-09-02).

### Where the code is

**147,568 B of `CODE`, and there is no hotspot in it.** MEASURED 2026-09-01
from the linker map with `tools/code_map.py` (which checks itself against
`size`; 74 B of link fill) (DECISIONS.md 2026-09-01, *no hotspot,
twenty-three decoders*):

| | bytes | share |
|---|---|---|
| Macintosh shell — UI, File Manager, memory | 17,358 | 11.8 % |
| **format decoders, 23 files** | **87,508** | **59.3 %** |
| shared codec machinery — Huffman, LZW, LZ, BWT | 12,564 | 8.5 % |
| deflate (zip + gzip) | 7,442 | 5.0 % |
| engine core, util, segment joining | 9,720 | 6.6 % |
| encryption: ciphers and key schedules | 7,480 | 5.1 % |
| Retro68 runtime and libc glue | 5,422 | 3.7 % |

The largest decoder is `fmt_dd` at 8,860 B; the largest two functions are
5,136 and 5,114 B, 3.5 % each; eleven decoders together make 16,342 B. The
size is the format count, not any one format.

**Read-only tables outside `CODE`: 32,077 B, 26,489 B since 2026-09-19** —
`ss_dict0` (9,701 B), the cipher S-boxes, the fixed inflate tables. The DES
SP tables and Compact Pro's CRC-64 table (6,144 B) are now built at key
setup into a 2 KB heap block from 544 B: A5 −5,588 B against +756 B of code,
net +4,832 B of budget, paid by 2 KB of heap during an encrypted entry
(DECISIONS.md 2026-09-19, *the transient tables*). No large table remains
that could be moved out to buy budget.

Two wrong ways to count: summing `size` over objects gives 178,696 B
(`--gc-sections` drops 17 %), and counting every `.text`/`.rodata` input
section 179,561 B (`.rodata` goes to the A5 world). Only the map's `.code*`
output sections answer what the budget asks.

### Budget

**What a 1 MB Mac Plus leaves the engine in the resident control build:
630,496 B, measured 2026-10-22** (`-D UNC_OVL_RESIDENT -D UNC_AT_BUDGET`,
`derived-PCCTS.tar`; `FreeMem()` less a flat 20 KB reserve), with 201,590
and 106,208 B on the 512Ke tiers (*512 KB machines*). Lowered since by
derivation to **627,832 B** (2026-10-25), one for one with the control's
growth; `tools/run_tiers.py` (`TIERS`, `TIERS_CONTROL`, `TIERS_MACHINE`)
holds the current figures and each step. Earlier machine figures: 634,364
(2026-10-09), 653,532 (2026-09-27), 648,250 (2026-09-22), 648,100
(2026-08-31). Every re-take has moved all three tiers by the identical
amount — the control build's `CODE` + A5 change. `-D UNC_AT_BUDGET` itself
costs the control ~300 B (*Three configurations, one tree*). The shipped
build's per-format budget is this plus what the overlay release gives back
(*Overlays*).

**Withdrawn on this figure** (BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*): `TIERS`
16,888 B above the machine 2026-10-02 → 10-09, so the shipped lane
over-granted every format (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-09, *the base nobody
re-took*); "647,204 B, arithmetic", a correct subtraction from a three-week
stale base — the machine had gone UP 182 B. A constant derived from a
measurement is only as current as the measurement. The 2026-09-22 change
was predicted at −64 B and measured −32 (DECISIONS.md 2026-09-22, *the
header that was a length*): the measurement is the number.

**Re-pricing unmeasured constants bought 81,936 B in two days**, at no cost
in speed: 566,164 → 615,348 B when the reserve went flat (49,184 B), →
635,812 B when the stack was priced (20,464 B, *Stack*), → 648,100 B when
the reserve was re-priced (12,288 B, *What the reserve is for*). Against that,
a day of size flags bought 780 B for 0.82 % of every extraction (*Size flags,
priced in both currencies*). The stack cut's revert and re-make is in
BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*.

**The reserve** (`kMemReserveMax` = `kMemReserveMin` = 20 KB): a flat 80 KB
until 2026-08-24, a quarter of free clamped to [32 KB, 80 KB], a flat 32 KB
2026-08-30, 20 KB since 2026-08-31 (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-24, *the reserve*).
Each step went entirely to the engine; 80 KB → the quarter rule took Now
Compress from none of its fixtures to all three in the minimum partition
(*Budget sweep*). `-D UNC_AT_RESERVE` measured the shell and Toolbox at
**13,242 B** (559,136 B low-water against 647,096 free, engine peak 74,718)
and **22,508 B** of total heap on the password path, the most dialog-heavy
moment. Pricing it needs a fixture that fits and is heavy: an archive too
big has its allocation refused and never stresses the heap (596,362 B
low-water on one such run). The 69-fixture 1 MB tier was **69/69** on it with
unchanged peaks, so no decoder spends what it is offered. A probe over all
67 fixtures of the 1 MB sample found the tightest moment left **121,124 B**
free, with Toolbox and File Manager under 1 KB while the engine sat at its
peak.

**`kMemReserve` cannot be traded for speed** (derived 2026-08-30): the next
`target` rung for the slowest fixture needs a 650,222 B peak, and a 1 MB
Mac Plus had **648,084 B** free before any reserve.

**The three heaviest archives**, each needing 566,009 B, stopped fitting when
the budget fell from 597,036 B (2026-08-24) as the application grew 31,860 B;
they were refused per entry and none is in the on-Mac sample. Replayed on
the machine at 1 MB on 2026-08-31 (a Linux-proxy claim that they fit was
withdrawn first — BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*):

| fixture | on a 1 MB Mac Plus | peak |
|---|---|---|
| `derived-Domino_v1.4.sit` | **extracts**, 2 files byte-exact | 614,018 B |
| `derived-XMenu.dmg.sit` | **extracts**, 1 file byte-exact | 600,692 B |
| `derived-EasyFind.dmg.sit` | **extracts**, 1 file byte-exact — since 2026-09-18, capped at 562,000 B with a 2,400 KB destination; the 2026-08-31 "refusal" was the harness's 1,440 KB Out floppy | 561,919 B |

"What EasyFind would take is 2,031,101 B more" is withdrawn: it was the
transform vector's expected refusal (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-31, *the message
that failed a successful extraction*). The whole corpus is inside a 1 MB Mac
Plus since 2026-09-18; Domino cleared a 627,620 B budget by
13,602 B. Run at 615,348 and 627,620 B, all three peaks were identical to
the byte (614,018, 600,500, 600,692).

**The budget was 678,392 B until 2026-08-24, 91 KB too generous**: it went
stale silently because it is `FreeMem()` when extraction begins and falls
whenever the application grows, and nothing re-measured it for seventeen
days. The index's halving `target` hid it — a budget error smaller than the
gap between two rungs changes nothing observable. A cross-validation that
agrees is not evidence when the thing it drives is quantised.

**Two budgets, three and a half times apart.** Under MultiFinder and System 7
the application gets a PARTITION; `macextract.r` declares a 400 KB minimum.

| | free | reserve | budget |
|---|---|---|---|
| System 6.0.8, 1 MB, no MultiFinder (whole machine), resident control | 650,976 B | 20 KB | **630,496 B** (measured 2026-10-22; 634,364 on 2026-10-09) |
| System 7 / MultiFinder at the declared 400 KB minimum, **shipped overlay build** | 285,812 B | 20 KB | **265,332 B** |
| the same partition, resident control build | 202,126 B | 20 KB | **181,646 B** |

The partition rows are measurements of 2026-09-25 (System 7.0.1, preferred
size built down to 400 KB, `-D UNC_AT_BUDGET`, one run each on
`derived-Export.sit`). The eager build holds a 146,176 B bundle from launch
there and still gains **+83,686 B**, because the bundle is released before
the engine takes its budget — in line with the 82–105 KB of the
whole-machine tiers (*Overlays*).

### How low it goes

Two floors, binary-searched with `tools/run_budget.py` over the whole corpus
(2026-08-24; a sweep is two seconds):

| | budget | partition |
|---|---|---|
| every archive at least OPENS — entries named, folders made, each failure per entry | **27,596 B** | ~223 KB |
| every fixture EXTRACTS FULLY — nothing budget-limited at all | **566,009 B** | **~798 KB** |

Partition is `budget + reserve + overhead`; the overhead (code, stack, A5)
was measured at **197,640 B** with a 32 KB stack (409,600 − 211,960 at
400 KB, 870,400 − 672,760 at 850 KB) and is **177,176 B** with the 12 KB
stack — derived, not re-measured; both partition figures fall 20,464 B. So
about 800 KB in Get Info extracts the whole corpus; at the 400 KB minimum
every archive opens and lists, and 16 of 31 StuffIt 5 fixtures and two
others fail per entry with a message.

The opens-floor was 137,264 B until 2026-08-24: Now Compress's block buffers,
the engine's only per-ARCHIVE allocation, refused the whole archive. It
fails per entry now.

### Budget sweep: the corpus at both budgets

`tools/run_budget.py` at the MultiFinder figure of the day (*Budget*), the
one a user can choose:

| format | fixtures | limited at 158,970 B | peak |
|---|---|---|---|
| BinHex | 204 | 0 | 8,192 B |
| Compact Pro | 108 | 0 | 31,111 B |
| StuffIt | 227 | 0 | 81,212 B |
| LZH | 8 | 0 | 44,624 B |
| Gzip | 3 | 0 | 52,216 B |
| **Now Compress** | 3 | **0** | 137,264 B |
| DiskDoubler | 91 | 1 | 158,720 B |
| Unix compress | 4 | 1 | 120,880 B |
| StuffIt 5 | 31 | **16** | 158,165 B |

Before 2026-08-24 the same sweep at 130,136 B had Now Compress at 2 of 3
limited with a peak of zero (refused whole) and StuffIt 5 at 21. At the
roomy 597,036 B budget only StuffIt 5 refuses anything (8 of 31), hence its
memory-for-time path. Windows sized from the entry (2026-08-24):

- **Now Compress** allocated 131,072 B per ARCHIVE before an entry was read;
  sized from the largest fork the small fixture is 84,248 B, and listing
  skips the allocation, so `l` works in any partition.
- **SuperDisk's window**: flat 98,304 B → bounded by the fork, 117,294 →
  85,943 B; `Read Me` in `alysis-disk-expander-12` extracts under 50,000 B.
- **zip's inflate window**: `synth-exo-zip-deflate.zip` 53,515 → 24,843 B.
  gzip's cannot be sized (ISIZE is in the trailer; `fmt_exo.c` passes
  `UNC_SIZE_UNKNOWN`).

ARC (33,732 B) and SpaceSaver (36 KB measured, 68 KB ceiling) fit at
130,136 B and stay on their ceilings — a fact about today's corpus. ARC's
tables are indexed by a 12-bit code from the stream, so shrinking them
changes what a damaged file does; it would need `unc_lzw.c`'s two-number
treatment. No generic adaptive mechanism is warranted until a
megabyte-class window arrives; the Arsenic answer will not simply port
(DECISIONS.md 2026-08-03, the LZW table). LZH is not that format: 64 KB
worst case, and it halves.

### Per-file `-O2`

**Tried and rejected, 2026-08-24**, on `sit_arsenic.c`, 4 KB of code that
runs for twenty minutes:

| | app | ISBN at 1 MB, 1x |
|---|---|---|
| `-Os` | 154,240 B | **1,284 s** |
| `-O2` on `sit_arsenic.c` | 155,776 B | **1,304 s** |

1,536 B for "≤ 20 s, sign unknown": the runs are one poll interval apart at
`--poll 20`, the finest these runs tolerate. Do not re-try without a harness
that resolves better on a 22-minute run.

### Stack

**Measure it; do not assume it.** One command gives every function's frame:

    m68k-apple-macos-gcc -mcpu=68000 -Os -std=c99 -Iinclude -Isrc \
        -fstack-usage -c src/FILE.c -o /dev/null

and the call graph comes from the relocations in the linked objects
(`m68k-apple-macos-objdump -dr`), so the worst path is a sum over the two.

| | before 2026-08-24 | now |
|---|---|---|
| deepest chain in the engine | 6,664 B (`unc_dd_extract` → `dd_fork` → `dd_ddn`) | **3,350 B**, derived 2026-10-17: 3,310 B since 2026-10-07, +44 B for `UncEntryInfo` (`unc_dd_extract` 952 → 996), −4 for DiskDoubler's FInfo (996 → 992) |
| Now Compress chain | 5,632 B | ~1,400 B; −616 B since 2026-09-21 (a shape-3 block's table staging moved to the per-archive allocation; OPEN_ITEMS.md, *What is still XADMaster's*) |
| largest single frame | 3,740 B (`dd_fork`) | **2,352 B** (`ars_index_build`) |
| largest frame in the SHELL | — | **1,544 B** (`ExtractOne`, whose `MacSink` holds an entry), measured 2026-10-17; `StampCat` 272 → **320 B** 2026-10-21 (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-21, *the Desktop Manager System 6 can have*) |

Zip's chains, measured 2026-10-08/09 (*Implode*, *Shrink*): deflate
`unc_zip_extract` → `zip_decode` (56) → `unc_inflate_raw` (108) → `inflate`
(88) → `inflate_table` (152) = **1,868 B**; the minimum-match-length probe
`unc_zip_extract` → `zip_imp_dry` (172) → `zip_decode` → `zip_implode` →
`imp_flush` (60) → `exo_emit` (32) = **1,896 B**, zip's deepest; Shrink
→ `unc_shrink_decode` (120) → `zip_put` (0) → `exo_emit` (32) = **1,672 B**,
all with `unc_zip_extract` at 1,464 B. Since 2026-10-17 `unc_zip_extract`
is **1,540 B** and LZH's `unc_lzh_extract` 1,560 / `lzh_ext` 64 (DECISIONS.md
2026-10-17, *the archives' own Finder info*); `unc_ad_apply` 80 B since
2026-10-18, off any decode chain (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-18, *the comment
Spotlight kept*). None is near the engine's deepest.

#### What the stack actually uses, measured two ways

The static bound is not what the machine needs: the Toolbox and File Manager
run on this stack too. `-D UNC_AT_STACK` reports two instruments, because the
first alone was wrong:

- **SAMPLE** — the stack POINTER, taken in the progress pump, the sink write
  path, the allocator, and inside the Dialog Manager's own filter and
  user-item procs, where the Toolbox's frames are below us. Exact where it is
  taken, blind between.
- **PAINT** — the region between ApplLimit and the stack base, painted at
  launch and read back. Sees everywhere, including inside a Toolbox call, but
  cannot tell a stack write from anything else.

Swept across stack sizes on a 1 MB Mac Plus, 2026-08-31 (`UNC_STACK_BUMP_KB`
rebuilds at any size), on `derived-Library_ISBN_Barcoder.sit`, the heaviest
fixture:

| stack | SAMPLE | PAINT | floor left untouched |
|---|---|---|---|
| 10,172 B | **5,200** | 5,604 | 4,568 |
| 12,220 B | **5,200** | 6,828 | 5,392 |
| 16,316 B | **5,200** | 9,288 | 7,028 |
| 20,412 B | **5,200** | 15,568 | 4,844 |
| 24,508 B | **5,200** | 18,840 | 5,668 |
| 32,700 B | **5,200** | 25,392 | 7,308 |

SAMPLE never moves and PAINT tracks the region (peak heap 559,805 B at every
size). Sweeping DOWN, PAINT converges on SAMPLE — 9,288 → 6,828 → 5,604 — so
the real requirement is **about 5,600 B**. Through the real UI (Standard
File via `SFPGetFile`'s dialog hook, progress and password dialogs) SAMPLE
is **3,134 B** at 10 KB and at 32 KB, and the password dialog drove
correctly down to 10 KB.

**So `kStackBump` is 4 KB, a 12 KB stack** — 2.1x the deepest write observed
— and **20,464 B** went to the engine (*Budget*). Verified byte-exact at that
size on **69/69 at 1 MB**, 512 KB and System 7, and on Mac OS 9.2 on
2026-09-04 (21/21 plus six DiskDoubler archives); that tier had been named as
verified four days before it was run (BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*). The
Mac OS 9 bomb of the time was a field nobody assigned, not the stack size
(DECISIONS.md 2026-09-04).

**PAINT is not a stack measurement.** Its lowest dirty word sits a
near-constant 5–7 KB above ApplLimit at every stack size, but the stack is
anchored at the TOP of the region; the heap's `bkLim` equals `ApplLimit`,
measured, so the heap is not the writer either. *Hypothesis, not measured:* a
ROM routine sizing a buffer from the space available, safe under a smaller
stack by construction. The word holds `0x0040C602`, in the Mac Plus ROM's
range.

**What neither instrument can see:** a path no fixture took. SAMPLE is blind
between its points — Standard File's own modal loop, the File Manager's
frames during `FSWrite` below `sink_write`'s sample. Read it WITH the static
table, never instead of it; the margin covers the gap.

Three earlier metrics were wrong: scanning for the first dirty long up from
ApplLimit, or the first clean one down from the base, finds a hole rather
than a boundary (a frame holding an uninitialised buffer leaves clean longs
inside it); the lowest dirty long in the top quarter saturated at the
quarter boundary. A metric with a threshold baked in reports the threshold.

The sixth join scheme's deepest chain is **2,796 B** (2026-08-29):
`unc_extract_segments` → `unc_mime_extract` → `mime_emit` → `mime_dec_run`
→ `mime_dec_next` → `seg_read` → `seg_open` → `unc_mimeseg_head` →
`mseg_scan` (1,256 B: a 512-byte read window and a 512-byte unfolded header)
→ `unc_read_at` — the only chain through the engine twice.

What fixed the 6,664 B chain: DiskDoubler's three `UncCanon` and Now
Compress's two moved to the heap (~3.9 KB each), and the zip end-of-central-
directory scan window stopped at 1 KB rather than 4 KB. `UncPath`'s per-level
uncut length adds 56 B in `unc_dd_extract` and 52 in each other walk frame
(DECISIONS.md, *the path builder's own cap*).

## Decode cost, before and after the 2026-08-24 pass (Linux, callgrind `Ir`)

Instruction counts, not cycles: ratios between builds of the same engine,
never predicted runtime. The Mac Plus figures two sections down are what a
user waits through.

| Fixture | What it exercises | before | after | |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ACInfo_Comm.sit.bin` | opening a MacBinary envelope | 757,559 | 163,776 | **−78.4 %** |
| `alpha401.cpt` | the same, Compact Pro inside | 761,290 | 167,655 | **−78.0 %** |
| `xmasbeeps.sit` | the same, StuffIt inside | 789,219 | 195,059 | **−75.3 %** |
| `derived-Library_ISBN_Barcoder.sit` (`--budget 597036`) | StuffIt 5, sampled index | 2,346,708,561 | 697,304,583 | **−70.3 %** |
| `derived-SyntheticReferences2.0.sea` | Now Compress | 791,938,983 | 419,972,116 | **−47.0 %** |
| `derived-Terrainman_2.0__.sea` | DiskDoubler DDn | 52,194,820 | 32,424,007 | **−37.9 %** |
| `derived-EmpireBuilderDemo.sit` | StuffIt, serialized-tree Huffman | 48,971,940 | 31,422,881 | **−35.8 %** |
| `derived-a2basic.dmg.sit` (`--budget 597036`) | StuffIt 5, sampled index | 487,403,395 | 315,159,205 | **−35.3 %** |
| `derived-XferIt_1.5b4.sea` | Compact Pro | 114,726,855 | 77,335,095 | **−32.6 %** |
| `PCCTS.tar.gz` | gzip (zlib inflate + our CRC-32) | 6,342,819 | 5,411,475 | **−14.7 %** |
| `derived-MUSFMT10.ZIP` | zip (deflate) | 13,728,964 | 12,068,337 | **−12.1 %** |
| `derived-xmasbeeps.sit` | StuffIt classic method 2 (LZW) | 34,270,500 | 30,635,054 | **−10.6 %** |
| `a2ps.V4.3.tar.Z` | Unix compress (LZW) | 4,648,124 | 4,212,836 | **−9.4 %** |
| `macburger-helper.hqx` | BinHex | 26,385,143 | 24,054,217 | **−8.8 %** |
| `4acalc.zip` | a real zip, 8 KB | 151,835 | 151,784 | −0.0 % |

- **The two StuffIt 5 rows** are capped at 597,036 B, but the older build fell
  to `target` 256 there where the newer holds 512, so part of the −70 % is a
  rung of the memory-for-time dial, not cheaper instructions (*Budget*).
- **The three envelope rows are one change, not a decoder**:
  `unc_macbinary_probe` moved ahead of `unc_zipseg_probe`'s 64 KB backwards
  scan, which became a pointer walk. `4acalc.zip` is the control.
- **CRC functions**: four bytes per loop test, walked with a pointer;
  `unc_crc32` was **40.7 %** of a gzip extraction. Not slicing-by-4 (aligned
  32-bit reads, 4x table). 384 B of code.
- **Both Huffman decoders peek and look up**: `unc_canon_decode` (Compact Pro,
  DDn, Now Compress, StuffIt 13, LZH) and `unc_huff_decode` (StuffIt 3,
  DiskDoubler 4, PackIt; was 48 % of EmpireBuilder). `unc_bitin_bits` takes
  n bits in one masked shift: another 6–10 points on DiskDoubler and Now
  Compress.
- **Both tables are TEN bits wide, measured**: 8 → 10 is a further **17.0 %**
  of a Now Compress run, 8.0 % DiskDoubler, 7.8 % Compact Pro, 8.7 % the tree
  decoder's StuffIt fixture; 11 adds 2.7 %, 12 a further 0.7 %. Cost 1 KB
  (canonical) or 2 KB (tree) per table; `derived-keyAhead.cpt` got faster.

**Changes since, each measured on its own** (48 fixtures = the two largest
of every format):

| change | date | measured | code / A5 / heap | DECISIONS.md |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| consolidation: one fork writer, one RLE90 filter | 2026-08-27 | **+0.44 %** over 48; BinHex +2.3 to +3.6 %, ARC +0.7 to +4.4 %, LZH +0.7 to +3.1 %, DiskDoubler **−5.3 %** | — | *the fork writer and the filter* |

**Linux counts mis-rank 68000 changes**: a clearer Now Compress draft cost
**790 → 811 ticks, +2.7 %** while executing FEWER x86 instructions
(5,871,817 → 5,859,843) — the register allocation across a call boundary in
a 32,768-iteration loop (DECISIONS.md 2026-09-21, *asking the program that
wrote it*).

### What is still hot, measured on the same runs

`ars_next_symbol`'s linear symbol search averages **10.4 iterations per
decoded symbol** (471,500 decodes on the ISBN fixture; its two lines 4.52 % +
5.37 % of the run against 0.72 % for both divisions), so a multiply per
iteration would cost a 68000 more than one `__udivsi3`. The divisions are
unsigned.

**BinHex** `bh_get_bits` was 38 % of a large `.hqx`; an inline fast path
measured **worse** (−5.5 % against −6.6 %). Decoding four groups to three
bytes changes what a stream truncated mid-triple does, and that is graded by
9,320 robustness variants rather than argued.

`-flto` crashes Retro68's gcc 16.1.0 (ICE `write_symbol, at
lto-streamer-out.cc:3097`, IPA pass `modref`) on `mac/macextract_mac.c`.
Tried 2026-08-24; do not re-try without a newer toolchain.

## Measured on a 7 MHz Mac Plus (Mini vMac 1x, System 6.0.8, 4 MB)

`run.py --emu mv-4M-1x`; the extract column is the archive **plus the
autotest build's read-back** (every fork read back and CRC-32'd before the
grid is painted). Set: `tools/vmac/timing.txt`, one large fixture per format,
so each figure is an upper bound. **All fifteen rows re-measured 2026-10-10**,
byte-exact, after the read-back CRC went table-driven; "was" is the
bit-at-a-time harness, which spent 2,860 ticks of `kermitut9test.bin`'s ~52 s
in the read-back against 90 for the extraction (corrected 2026-10-10:
this column had been called "the archive alone"; DECISIONS.md 2026-10-10,
*the clock that was timing its own check*). Older dated figures in this
file are on the old harness and compare only with each other.

Every figure is accurate to ±one poll. **A read-back of ~5 s per 300 KB of
output is still in every row** (measured: 341 ticks on `kermitut9test.bin`, 325 on
`derived-PCCTS.tar`).

| Fixture | Format | In | Extract | was | Poll |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `derived-Export.sit` | StuffIt | 1.6 KB | 3 s | 3 s | 3 s |
| `kermitut9test.bin` | MacBinary | 304 KB | **9 s** | 52 s | 3 s |
| `derived-PackIt.pit` | PackIt | 62 KB | 9 s | 18 s | 3 s |
| `derived-KeyGraphics.sit` | StuffIt 5 | 4.6 KB | 15 s | 24 s | 3 s |
| `popper-1.7.tar.Z` | compress | 59 KB | 15 s | 40 s | 3 s |
| `derived-PCCTS.tar` | Tar | 287 KB | **15 s** | 46 s | 3 s |
| `derived-Expand_Now_1.0.1.sea` | Now Compress | 62 KB | 18 s | 31 s | 3 s |
| `PCCTS.tar.gz` | gzip | 64 KB | 21 s | 58 s | 3 s |
| `derived-MUSFMT10.ZIP` | Zip | 172 KB | 43 s | 110 s | 3 s |
| `macburger-helper.hqx` | BinHex | 306 KB | 46 s | 83 s | 3 s |
| `derived-EmpireBuilderDemo.sit` | StuffIt | 278 KB | **61 s** | 119 s | 3 s |
| `derived-MacPass390.sea` | DiskDoubler | 156 KB | 61 s | 110 s | 3 s |
| `derived-XferIt_1.5b4.sea` | Compact Pro | 285 KB | 122 s | 251 s | 3 s |
| `derived-a2basic.dmg.sit` | StuffIt 5 (Arsenic) | 153 KB | 281 s | 321 s | 20 s |
| `derived-Library_ISBN_Barcoder.sit` | StuffIt 5 (Arsenic) | 202 KB | 461 s | 542 s | 20 s |

**There is no boot column: this harness cannot measure boot.** It reports the
first poll at which the grid is visible — 9.2 s at `--poll 3`, 20.1 s at
`--poll 20` for every fixture. A stripped System 6.0.8 reaches the application
in about 10 s.

**StuffIt 5 / Arsenic at 1x — and it is in a class of its own.** Extract time,
both paths, on the purpose-built 1 MB 1x emulator (`build_mv mv-1M-1x 1M z`),
one emulator at a time:

| Fixture | Output | 4 MB — transform table | 1 MB — sampled index | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `derived-a2basic.dmg.sit` | 205,019 B | **281 s** (peak 829,041 B) | **341 s** (peak 703,565 B) | 1.21x |
| `derived-Library_ISBN_Barcoder.sit` | 641,115 B | **461 s** (peak 1,353,789 B) | **742 s** (peak 644,858 B) | 1.61x |

**All four cells re-measured 2026-10-10**, byte-exact. The fallback costs
1.21x to 1.61x; the ratio widens with block fill, and neither the x86 ratio
nor output size predicts it (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-07, *the inverse BWT falls
back to a sampled occurrence index*). The sampled index sizes itself to the
budget it is given (*Budget*), so a 1 MB peak that rises with the budget is
the dial, not a regression. Measured component costs on the ISBN fixture: the
unsigned divisions **20 s of 1,404** (1.4 %), a 512-byte coarse table over
`cum[]` **100 s of 1,384** (7.2 %); the sentinel and three-byte samples are
DECISIONS.md 2026-08-24 (*the performance pass*).

**Poll no faster than 20 s on these runs.** `--poll 5` and below report a
bogus FAIL after about five seconds — four times out of four, against five
clean runs at `--poll 20`. The alive-grid-read-mid-paint explanation is a
**hypothesis**; the pattern is measured. `--poll 3` is fine for the short
runs. DECISIONS.md 2026-08-07 records ~40 s between two earlier 4 MB sets as
unexplained.

**`macburger-helper.hqx`'s rate is very non-uniform** (16 % of the input in
the first seven minutes on the old harness, the last 205 KB in a hundred
seconds), which misled the timeouts chosen for it three times (BLOCKERS.md).

## How long a click waits (Mac Plus, 1x)

The widest gap between two progress callbacks is the longest the window,
Cancel, Cmd-. and the menu bar go unanswered (OPEN_ITEMS.md, *A click during
an extraction*). `-D UNC_AT_TICKGAP` at `--emu mv-4M-1x --poll 20`,
2026-10-12, `timing.txt`, all byte-exact; File Manager time is included, the
shipped 10 Hz gate (up to 0.1 s) is not. **Before the first callback** holds
the decoders' load and the archive's directory.

| fixture | format | widest gap | before the first | callbacks |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `derived-a2basic.dmg.sit` | StuffIt 5 (Arsenic) | **0.8 s** (254 s before 2026-10-12) | 1.2 s | 1,472 |
| `derived-Library_ISBN_Barcoder.sit` | StuffIt 5 (Arsenic) | **1.1 s** (140 s) | 1.4 s | 4,623 |
| `derived-KeyGraphics.sit` | StuffIt 5 (Arsenic) | **0.3 s** (9.4 s) | 0.2 s | 235 |
| `derived-Expand_Now_1.0.1.sea` | Now Compress | **6.1 s** | **5.5 s** | 3 |
| `derived-XferIt_1.5b4.sea` | Compact Pro | **4.8 s** | 3.0 s | 34 |
| `derived-MacPass390.sea` | DiskDoubler | **4.2 s** | 2.4 s | 18 |
| `derived-EmpireBuilderDemo.sit` | StuffIt | 2.5 s | 2.1 s | 33 |
| `derived-MUSFMT10.ZIP` | Zip | 2.2 s | 2.0 s | 18 |

| `popper-1.7.tar.Z` | compress | 1.5 s | 1.8 s | 7 |

**At 1 MB, under the sampled index**: a2basic **0.6 s** (18.3 s with only the
block decode ticking), the Barcoder **1.9 s** (24.1 s), inside the walk.

Every row but Arsenic's reports every 8 KB of INPUT (`UNC_PROGRESS_STEP`, two
`UNC_IOBUF` refills); Now Compress reads a 32 KB block whole. A 68LC040 at
25 MHz is several times faster (*hypothesis*: no machine here has timed one).

### What a 4 KB step buys, measured 2026-10-13

`UNC_PROGRESS_STEP` 8,192 → 4,096, stored-fork copy buffers left at 8 KB, NOT
shipped (OPEN_ITEMS.md, *A click during an extraction*), both builds with
`-D UNC_AT_TICKGAP` (its callback runs the shipped `prog_tick`, not the bar's
redraw). 1x, `timing.txt` less the two large Arsenic fixtures, byte-exact.

| fixture | gap, 8 → 4 KB | extraction, System 6.0.8 | System 7.0.1 |
|---|---|---|---|
| `derived-XferIt_1.5b4.sea`, Compact Pro | 4.8 → **3.1 s** | 102.80 → 102.85 s, +3 ticks | +14 ticks, +0.22 % |
| `derived-MacPass390.sea`, DiskDoubler | 4.2 → **2.8 s** | 53.75 → 53.78 s, +2 | +7, +0.21 % |
| `derived-EmpireBuilderDemo.sit`, StuffIt | 2.5 → **1.6 s** | 52.18 → 52.25 s, +4 | +13, +0.40 % |
| `derived-MUSFMT10.ZIP`, Zip | 2.2 → **1.5 s** | 32.05 → 32.07 s, +1 | +7, +0.35 % |
| `PCCTS.tar.gz`, gzip | 1.9 → **1.2 s** | +1 | — |
| `popper-1.7.tar.Z`, compress | 1.5 → **0.8 s** | +1 | — |
| `macburger-helper.hqx`, BinHex | 1.1 → **0.6 s** | +4 | — |
| `derived-Expand_Now_1.0.1.sea`, Now Compress | 6.1 → 6.1 s | +0 | — |

The other five rows move by at most one tick. **Price: 0 B** in every image,
resident `CODE` and A5, no heap; at most **+0.3 % on System 6 and +0.4 % on
System 7**, about 1.5 and 7 ms per extra pump. Gaps fall by a third to a half
(*hypothesis*: the widest gap holds File Manager writes, which do not halve).
**What this cannot see:** System 7's autotest boots the application as the
shell, so no other process was given time; with the Finder running each pump
can switch to it (*hypothesis*: more than 7 ms).

## What keeping a folder's date costs (Mac Plus, 1x)

**Measured 2026-10-17**, `run.py -D UNC_AT_TICKGAP --emu mv-4M-1x`, System
6.0.8, default floppy. `run=` is `RunExtract`'s duration in 60 Hz ticks, every
cell repeated to the tick. A folder's date is held in a one-entry cache
(DECISIONS.md 2026-10-17, *the date held once per folder*).

| subject | `main` (8f11eaf) | branch | branch, preservation disabled | preservation's own cost |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `derived-CursorAnimator.sit`, 86 files, 84 in folders | 2,058 t | **2,063 t** | 2,004 t | **+59 t, 1.0 s, +2.9 %** |
| 100 files of 100 B in one folder (synthetic, `synth_sqp.py`'s writer) | 779 t | **761 t** | 760 t | **+1 t** |

Against `main` the branch is +0.2 % and −2.3 % (`EnsureDir` looks a folder up
before creating it). A per-ITEM first version measured 2,278 t and 889 t
(+10.6 % / +13.5 %). **What it cannot see:** a hard disk; an archive whose
entries alternate between folders, where the cache pays three calls per change
of folder.

## What -O3 is worth, and where

2026-08-30, `derived-EmpireBuilderDemo.sit` at Mac Plus speed (`run.py --emu
mv-4M-1x -D UNC_AT_TIMING`, ticks).

**Where an extraction's seconds go:**

| phase | ticks | seconds | share |
|---|---|---|---|
| File Manager reads | 34 | 0.6 s | 0.8 % |
| File Manager writes | 16 | 0.3 s | 0.4 % |
| create, `SetEOF`, close, `FlushVol`, catalog | 12 | 0.2 s | 0.3 % |
| **the engine** | 4,164 | **69.4 s** | **98.5 %** |

**What the optimiser is worth on the four files that matter:**

| build | extract | application |
|---|---|---|
| `-Os` everywhere | 70.4 s | 182,144 B |
| `-O2`, `unc_lzw.c` + `unc_huffman.c` | 58.8 s | +1,536 B |
| `-O2`, those plus `unc_util.c` + `fmt_sit.c` | 56.2 s | +5,504 B |
| `-O2`, the whole engine bar `sit_arsenic.c` | 56.0 s | +40,192 B |
| `-O3`, the same four files | 52.5 s | +8,320 B |
| **`UNC_HOT` — six functions, `-O3`** | **52.6 s** | **+1,024 B** |

`-O3` on whole files spent 8,850 B of text, +2,428 B of it in
`unc_huff_read_tree` (0.44 % of the run). `UNC_HOT` (`src/unc_internal.h`)
gets the same time for an eighth of the size; the set it marks today is
wherever `UNC_HOT` appears in `src/`. GCC will not inline across differing
optimisation attributes, so `unc_sit_decode_fork` came out 226 B SMALLER.
Byte-exact with `UNC_HOT` in: **69/69 at 4 MB and 69/69 at the 1 MB floor**
(2026-08-30).

**Compact Pro, 2026-09-19**, `derived-XferIt_1.5b4.sea`:

| build | whole run | engine | File Manager |
|---|---|---|---|
| shipped | 10,409 ticks | 10,256 ticks (170.9 s) | 153 ticks |
| `UNC_HOT` on `unc_cpt_lzh_fork` and `cpt_rle_push` | 8,884 ticks | 8,735 ticks (145.6 s) | 149 ticks |
| the LZH and RLE stages fused, same day | **6,107 ticks** | **5,970 ticks (99.5 s)** | 137 ticks |

**−14.8 % for +1,028 B**, then **−31.7 % more for +76 B** (the 8,735-tick row
reproduced exactly on the later tree); DiskDoubler method 8 runs the same
functions, −47.0 % of Linux `Ir` on `ddtf-dd377ddb.dd` (DECISIONS.md
2026-09-19, *the seventh that was measured* and *the byte that went to two
places at once*).

**LZH, same day**, `aminet-lha_68k.lha` (`-lh5-`, 5 entries, 331,234 B, the
largest LZH output in the corpus):

| build | whole run | engine | File Manager |
|---|---|---|---|
| shipped | 3,650 ticks | 3,527 ticks (58.8 s) | 123 ticks |
| the static and LArc decoders rebuilt on lhasa, the match fused | **3,182 ticks** | **3,046 ticks (50.8 s)** | 136 ticks |

**−13.6 % of the engine for +140 B**; −17.8 % of 68000 instructions here and
−46.4 % on LArc's `long.lzs`. `UNC_HOT` on the new copy loop: **+416 B for
3.8 % MORE instructions**, refused (DECISIONS.md 2026-09-19, *the match that
was walked twice*). The run-length half: `derived-Guy_s_Mac_Consultants.cpt`
engine **3,536 → 2,318 ticks**, −34.4 %.

## Relative decode cost per format (Linux, same engine)

The shape, not the clock: relative cost transfers to any CPU as a ratio.
`macextract t`, best of three, release build:

| Fixture | Format | In KB | Out KB | ms | in KB/s |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| kermitut9test.bin | MacBinary | 297 | 296 | 0.6 | 485,802 |
| derived-PCCTS.tar | Tar | 280 | 231 | 0.6 | 439,603 |
| derived-PackIt.pit | PackIt (stored) | 60 | 60 | 0.7 | 85,110 |
| macburger-helper.hqx | BinHex | 299 | 253 | 2.9 | 102,708 |
| derived-MUSFMT10.ZIP | Zip (deflate) | 168 | 498 | 3.9 | 42,704 |
| popper-1.7.tar.Z | Compress | 58 | — | 2.0 | 29,526 |
| PCCTS.tar.gz | Gzip | 62 | — | 2.0 | 31,162 |
| derived-EmpireBuilderDemo.sit | StuffIt (2 LZW, 3 Huffman) | 272 | 407 | 9.0 | 30,204 |
| derived-XferIt_1.5b4.sea | Compact Pro | 279 | 926 | 18.2 | 15,351 |
| derived-KeyGraphics.sit | StuffIt 5 (Arsenic) | 5 | 33 | 1.6 | 2,859 |

Envelopes are essentially a copy; BinHex a few times that; StuffIt's LZW and
Compact Pro's LZH 15–30x a copy per input byte; Arsenic two orders of
magnitude off the top.

## Hand-written 68000 assembly: the candidates, estimated

**ESTIMATES, from the MC68000 timing table; none has been timed on a machine.**
The item and what would close it are OPEN_ITEMS.md's (*Hand-written 68000
assembly*), including "the shared prerequisites"; why it is filed rather than
written is DECISIONS.md 2026-10-09 (*what GCC will not do with an index*).

| candidate | C today | assembly sketch | what would close it |
|---|---|---|---|
| **RC4's keystream loop** (`unc_crypt_apply`, `UNC_CRYPT_RC4`; Private File and StuffIt 5's encrypted forks) | 19 instructions, ~178 cycles a byte | 10 instructions, ~104 cycles: index registers cleared once, so `add.b` wraps at 256 and `(d8,An,Dn.w)` addresses the table with no `andi.l #255`; `eor.b Dn,(An)+`; `dbf` | the shared prerequisites, then the 68000 path beside the C one under `#ifdef __mc68000__`, graded and timed |
| **CRC-32** (`unc_crc32`; zip, gzip, StuffIt 5, Compact Pro, Private File — a third of Private File's instructions, FORMATS.md *What it costs*) | 8 instructions, ~111 cycles a byte | 7 instructions, ~77: the cleared-index trick, `add.w` twice to scale, `(d8,An,Dn.w)` | as RC4's row |
| **CRC-16/XMODEM** (`unc_crc16_xmodem`; BinHex, MacBinary, PackIt) | 9 instructions, ~115 cycles a byte | 8 instructions, ~67: `rol.w #8` replaces two 22-cycle shifts; `clr.b`, then the cleared-index table read | as RC4's row |
| **CRC-16/ARC** (`unc_crc16_arc`, `UNC_HOT`; StuffIt 1.5–4 forks) | 9 instructions, ~92 cycles a byte | 7 instructions, ~63: the cleared-index table read, one `lsr.w #8` | as RC4's row |
| **Arsenic's sampled-index scan** (`ars_index_next`'s `do { i++; } while (block[i] != c)`; every StuffIt 5 entry on a 1 MB machine whose block outgrows the transform table — 742 s for the ISBN fixture, FORMATS.md *StuffIt 5 / Arsenic at 1x*) | 3 instructions, ~32 cycles a byte | ~16 cycles a byte: `cmp.b (An)+,Dn` with the sentinel ending the run, unrolled four times; `i` recovered once per scan | as RC4's row, plus the `UNC_ARS_FORCE_SAMPLED` build in the `qemu-m68k` lane; its share of a run is not measured (77 % before the sentinel) |
| **LZW's string walk** (`lzw_emit`, `UNC_HOT`; compress, StuffIt method 2, DiskDoubler method 1, ARC) | 7 instructions, ~76 cycles a byte: `andi.l #65535` after every parent read, the `chr` base re-copied each byte | 4 instructions, ~50: upper word cleared once; both bases in address registers; `dbf` on `k` | as RC4's row |
| **Compact Pro's match copy** (`cpt_rle_match`, `UNC_HOT`; DiskDoubler method 8 runs the same loop) | 8 instructions, ~64 cycles a byte | 6 instructions, ~46: `0x81` in a register; cursor post-incremented and corrected on the escape exit; `dbf` on `k` (≤ 8,192) | as RC4's row |
| **The fork writer's run copy** (`unc_fork_write`; every output byte of StuffIt methods 5, 13 and 14, LZH's `-lh1-`/`-lh2-`/`-lh3-` and DART) | 12 instructions, ~154 cycles a byte: `o->buf`/`o->len` reloaded and stored every byte (aliasing), then `len == cap` tested | 2 instructions, ~22: `n` clipped once per piece, `move.b (An)+,(Am)+` / `dbf` (~8 a byte with `move.l` at equal parity). **A C loop over the clipped piece would get most of this too** (100 B; its time not recorded) | as RC4's row |
| **Arsenic's renormalisation** (`ars_next_symbol`'s `while (range <= HALF)`, once per compressed bit) | 22 instructions, ~280 cycles a bit: bit reader, `range` and `code` loaded and stored each bit | ~6 instructions, ~60: all in registers; `lsr.b #1` into X, `addx.l` into `code`. **Inference, not measured:** ~1.6 M compressed bits in the ISBN fixture puts the saving near 8 % of its 4 MB run | as RC4's row |
| **The masked match copies** (`window[pos++ & mask] = window[from++ & mask]`: method 13's `sit13_fork`, counted; `sit14.c` and `unc_lzah.c` same line, not counted) | 12 instructions, ~138 cycles a byte on method 13 | 2 instructions, ~22: the match split where it wraps or meets `outlen`, then `move.b (An)+,(Am)+` / `dbf`. **This is the three-cursor rewrite Compact Pro and LZH already got in C** (FORMATS.md *What -O3 is worth, and where*), so C may take most of it | as RC4's row |
| **Now Compress's match copy** (`now_newlzss`, inlined into `now_fork`) | 4 instructions, ~42 cycles a byte | 2 instructions, ~22: a second source pointer, `dbf` — bounds already checked per match | as RC4's row |
| **`-lh1-`'s tree-update search** (`lzah_update`'s `while (l + 1 <= LZAH_T && k > freq[l + 1]) l++`; DART, StuffIt method 5, LZH `-lh1-`) | 8 instructions, ~66 cycles a step | 2 instructions, ~18: `cmp.w (An)+,Dn` / `bhi.s`, stopped by the `0xFFFF` already at `freq[LZAH_T]` — which also makes the C loop's bound test redundant | as RC4's row |

**Suggestions, not yet estimated to a cycle:**

- **The header's "inline" helpers are out of line in the overlays.** At `tools/overlay/mkoverlay.sh`'s flags the listing shows `unc_bitin_bit` CALLED in nine files (`fmt_arc`, `fmt_dd`, `fmt_exo`, `fmt_lzh`, `fmt_sit`, `fmt_spacesaver`, `unc_huffman`, `unc_lzah`, `unc_lzdyn`), `unc_bufin_next` in six, `unc_bitin_fill` in two, `unc_fork_put` in `fmt_sit` — in `unc_lzah.c` a `jsr` per tree level, ~100 cycles. DECISIONS.md 2026-09-19 (*the tree that was Yoshizaki's*) says that coder kept "the engine's inline bit reader"; the listing says otherwise. **The listing is read, the cost is not measured.** `always_inline` needs no assembly.
- **Canonical Huffman decode as one routine with the bit state in registers** (`unc_canon_decode`): the table-hit path is ~45 instructions, ~630 cycles a symbol from the `-Os` listing, ~180 of them the call (`movem.l` of six registers each way, argument loads, `bitbuf`/`nbits` round-tripped). It also crosses an overlay boundary every symbol through a generated forward (`tools/overlay/mkforwards.py`, ~40 cycles), so no decoder can inline it. `unc_huff_decode` has the same shape. A fast path copied into each decoder, calling through the vector only on a miss, removes both.
- **The LSB-first table hit reverses its peek every symbol** (`unc_rev_fast`; StuffIt method 13, SpaceSaver method 3, ARC): 12 instructions, ~170 cycles a symbol. Building the LSB-first table at bit-reversed indices, as zlib does, removes it. **Needs no assembly.**
- **BinHex as a whole-line decoder.** 2,386 engine ticks for ~253 KB out, **~1,200 cycles an output byte**; `bh_next` is a call per byte with a five-register `movem` each way, and `bh_fork` makes a second CRC pass. A 64-character line → 48 bytes through RLE90 with the CRC folded in would be ~100 a byte. It changes truncated-mid-group behaviour (*What is still hot*), so the robustness variants grade it.
- **base64 and uuencode have BinHex's shape** (`unc_b64_feed` per input character into a four-byte queue): the same routine with a different table would serve all three.
- **Window clears as `movem.l`.** `clr.b` loops (~28 cycles a byte) in `fmt_lzh`, `unc_lzah`, `unc_lzdyn`; `clr.l` loops (~9) in Compact Pro, DiskDoubler and method 14; eleven registers by `movem.l` is ~2.2. A `uint32_t` C loop gets the first group to ~9 (allocations are even).
- **LZW's code reader shifts by a register** (`lzw_code`): ~130 cycles of shifting a code at 12–16 bits. compress writes codes in groups of eight filling exactly `n` bytes, so an unpacker per width with constant shifts takes eight at once.
- **Arsenic's symbol decoder as one routine** (`ars_decode`, through a pointer per symbol): the call alone ~200 cycles (`movem.l` of eight registers each way); each search step ~80 cycles where `sub.l (An)+,Dn` / `bcc` is ~24. With the renormalisation row and the divisions, the whole arithmetic decoder is put at ~20,000 cycles an output byte (FORMATS.md, *StuffIt 5: the Arsenic block size*).
- **Arsenic's two divisions** (`range / total`, `code / renorm`) go through `__udivsi3`; `m68kprof` counts each `divu` as one instruction against ~140 cycles, so its 0.72 % undercounts. **Inference, not measured:** dropping the signed wrapper alone was 1.4 % on the ISBN fixture, so the divisions are likely several times that. `total` is always < 2^16, so the first is two `divu` inline; the second's normalising shift can be computed once per symbol.
- **Byte-copy loops on `dbf`**: ~6 cycles a byte over `cmp.l`/`bcs` wherever the count fits 16 bits.
- **Arsenic's MTF shift** (`ars_mtf_decode`): `move.b -(An),-(Am)` / `dbf`. 34.5 % of a2basic before the two-level rewrite; not re-profiled since.
- **The repeating-key XOR ciphers take a `%` per byte** (ARC's password, PackIt's PMa5 `% 7u`): `__umodsi3`, ~400 cycles, where a compare-wrapped index is ~30. **Needs no assembly.**
- **ZipCrypto's per-byte key update** (`unc_crypt_zip_update`): two `unc_crc32` calls of one byte and a `__mulsi3` for `k1 * 134775813`; inline, roughly ~800 → ~400 cycles a byte, counted loosely.
- **DES's variable rotates** (`des_crypt`): `rol.l Dn` at 8 + 2n cycles in every S-box step. Low value: StuffIt 2.x `DES`/`NewDE` are graded only on this project's own archives (*StuffIt 2.0*, above).

- **The BinHex probe, for SIZE rather than speed** (`src/fmt_binhex_probe.c`). Its **644 B** (568 + 76, the control's `-Os` objects) are in the sniffer's image since 2026-10-21 (CORRECTED 2026-10-24: this read "resident" and 522 B); DECISIONS.md 2026-10-14 (*the BinHex before 4.0*) and 2026-10-24 (*the format before BinHex*) added 298 and 122 of them. A per-character C rewrite came out LARGER, 556 B against 522. A hand-written scan might be 50–100 B smaller — **unestimated: nothing is written or counted**.
- **zlib's `inflate_fast`** — 52.65 % of a gzip extraction (DECISIONS.md, *Why zlib stays*); vendored zlib is unmodified. Amiga 68000 inflaters exist (Keir Fraser's public-domain `inflate.S`) — **a hypothesis: none fetched or read here**; licence, provenance and correctness unchecked.

## Real-hardware verification (Mini vMac — System 6.0.8, 4 MB Mac Plus, 68000)

The Retro68 shell replayed against a 74-fixture sample spanning all seventeen
formats on **System 6.0.8 / 4 MB Mac Plus** under Mini vMac. Each extraction
is verified ON THE MAC: both forks read back through the File Manager and
CRC-32'd, painted to the framebuffer and cross-checked against the Linux
output (`tools/vmac_check.py`, `tools/decode_grid.py`).

**74/74 byte-exact PASS on the v0.37.0 source**, 2026-10-10: 72 in one sweep
(`dist/` application rebuilt byte-identical; 507 files, 6,846,866 data and
3,458,384 resource-fork bytes), `4alnch11.zip` (the first Implode archive on a
Macintosh; 4 files, 103,043 data bytes, 22,740 B peak) alone the same day, and
`synth-sqp-clear.pkg` (the first AppleLink package; 2 files, 36,300 data
bytes, 41,274 B) on 2026-10-11. Resource forks exact bar the OS-stamped
reserved header (bytes 16–127). `n/44`, `n/55`… figures elsewhere are earlier,
smaller samples, left as measured; four names listed twice until 2026-08-21
are BLOCKERS.md's. The nine THINK C / leading-`~` fixtures added 2026-08-08
are the only ones that distinguish a fork-naming mistake (DECISIONS.md
2026-08-08, *the Mac-side checkers stopped guessing at sidecars*).

**The archive comment is written on the Mac too** (2026-08-23):
`derived-DigSim_RAM_Circuit.cpt` gives 11 files, the extra a `TEXT`/`ttxt`
`Comments` holding *"Hi there."*, byte-exact against the CLI. Verified alone,
not in the sample.

    tools/vmac/setup.sh                              # once
    tools/vmac/run.py --list tools/vmac/sample.txt   # 4 MB

`EMUDIED` is a harness event, not a Mac-side failure (`tools/vmac/README.md`).
An earlier 39/44 record is withdrawn (BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*). This
emulator runs **all out**: correctness only, **no timing claim about a 7 MHz
68000 can be drawn from it**.

On-Mac engine peak heap by format, matching Linux `--stats`:

| Format | on-Mac peak heap |
|---|---|
| MacBinary / BinHex / Tar | 8 KB |
| PackIt | 9 KB |
| Compact Pro | 24 KB |
| Zip | 12–53 KB (re-measured 2026-08-09, was 12–65: see ¶) |
| Gzip | 51 KB |
| StuffIt classic | 19–72 KB‖ |
| Unix compress (.Z) | 15–219 KB§ |
| StuffIt 5 (Arsenic) | 0.63–2.53 MB when measured (block *fill* dependent — see †); the vector is three bytes an entry since 2026-09-19, not re-run |
| LZH / LHA | 4–41 KB over the sample (4,112 B stored, 41,364 B for `-lh7-`); 74 KB is the format's ceiling, for an entry that fills a 64 KB window |
| DiskDoubler / Now Compress | extracted and byte-exact on both Mini vMac tiers since 2026-08-15 (see above), and on Mac OS 9 since 2026-08-27 |

**The Out volumes are read back whole**: after the 2026-09-22 round
`checkdisk.py` read all **73 kept Out volumes byte-exact, names included** —
which the grid (file count and summed CRCs) cannot (TESTING.md).

## System 7 tier (Mini vMac — System 7.0.1, 4 MB Mac Plus, 68000)

**System 7.0.1, not 7.1** (7.1 survives only as floppy sets and a beta ISO).
What matters is System 7: Standard File returns a **working-directory**
reference in `SFReply.vRefNum`, and the Process Manager governs the launch by
the `SIZE` partition. Same 68000.

**74/74 byte-exact PASS at 4 MB on the v0.37.0 source**, 2026-10-10
(`4alnch11.zip` and `synth-sqp-clear.pkg` each alone after the 72), engine
peak identical to System 6.0.8 and System 3.0 at 4 MB on every fixture.
**Re-run 2026-08-31 at the 20 KB stack: 69/69** — the tier that closes the
stack change, since the Process Manager honours `SIZE` (*Stack*).

    tools/vmac/run.py --system Sys7.img --list tools/vmac/sample.txt

**Where this tier and the 1 MB tier disagree is the whole point of the
adaptive inverse BWT.** Same binary, same fixtures, both byte-exact:

| fixture | System 7, 4 MB | System 6, 1 MB |
|---|---|---|
| `derived-a2basic.dmg.sit` | 829,041 B (transform table) | 703,565 B (sampled index) |
| `derived-Library_ISBN_Barcoder.sit` | 1,353,789 B (transform table) | 644,858 B (sampled index) |

4 MB column 2026-10-10, 1 MB column 2026-10-09 (*Floor target
verification*). Nothing selects the path but the allocator saying no.

**This tier is 4 MB.** **The 1 MB floor target is System 6, and only
System 6**, and it is demonstrated by the 74/74 run below.

Not in `sample.txt`: both are payloads inside envelopes.
