## Floor target verification (Mini vMac — System 6.0.8, **1 MB** Mac Plus, 68000)

The 74-fixture sample on a Mini vMac built for **1 MB of RAM**, one boot per
fixture, on-Mac CRC-32 verification (`tools/vmac/run.py --mem 1M --list
tools/vmac/sample.txt`); the engine budget is derived from `FreeMem` at
startup. **74/74 byte-exact PASS**, re-measured whole on 2026-10-23
(BLOCKERS.md, *Status*, which keeps the run history). There are no
memory-budget refusals left at the floor target. The six StuffIt 5 rows, peak
heap as the Mac itself reports it on 2026-10-09 — identical to the byte under
System 3.0 at 1 MB the same day. `derived-Cool_Tools__.sit` peaked at
**535,199 B** on 2026-09-22 with every decoder resident, 44 bytes under what
`--budget` predicts on Linux.

**The three heaviest were re-run at 1 MB on 2026-08-29** after the end-of-run
report took ~8 KB of static RAM and 5.6 KB of application (*Name limits*):
**3/3 byte-exact**, at 472,137 B, 559,805 B and 482,861 B (Troi-ClipSave,
Library_ISBN, a2basic). That the peaks fall with the budget is a
**hypothesis, not measured**: Arsenic picks its transform strategy from what
the allocator will give it, so a smaller budget moving a peak down a doubling
step is what the design does. The 2026-08-26 rows (160,766, 181,774, 269,084,
560,589, 614,134 and 660,262 B) all moved by 2026-10-09, three down and three
up, across a budget that grew with the overlays — consistent with the
hypothesis, not a test of it.

| Fixture | Format | Outcome at 1 MB | Peak heap |
|---|---|---|---|
| derived-RWAsm_Folder.sit | StuffIt 5 | **all 38 entries byte-exact** | 131,312 B |
| derived-KeyGraphics.sit | StuffIt 5 | all 1 entry byte-exact | 148,844 B |
| derived-PuzzleGetter_0.8.sit | StuffIt 5 | **all 26 entries byte-exact** | 222,388 B |
| derived-a2basic.dmg.sit | StuffIt 5 | **all 1 entry byte-exact** | 703,565 B |
| derived-Library_ISBN_Barcoder.sit | StuffIt 5 | **all 34 entries byte-exact** | 644,858 B |
| derived-Troi-ClipSave-Plug-in1.1.sit | StuffIt 5 | **all 13 entries byte-exact** | 530,656 B |

`a2basic` and `Library_ISBN` passed from 2026-08-07: they need the *sampled*
inverse BWT, chosen automatically when the per-byte table's allocation fails.

**What those three entries hold**, measured 2026-08-03 by instrumenting
`numbytes` where `sit_arsenic.c` sizes the transform vector (the withdrawn
"each fills a 512 KB block" is in BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*):

| fixture | largest block fill | capacity by doubling | peak |
|---|---|---|---|
| `derived-Library_ISBN_Barcoder.sit` | 323,384 B | 512 KB | 1,854,208 B |
| `derived-a2basic.dmg.sit` | 197,515 B | 256 KB | 1,088,588 B |
| `derived-Troi-ClipSave-Plug-in1.1.sit` | **124,637 B** | 128 KB | 666,004 B |

The peak column is the fast path; `~35 KB parser + capacity + 4 × fill`
reproduces all three to within a rounding step, the 4× transform vector
(487 KB, 772 KB and 1,263 KB) being the dominant term. Under the sampled
index the same entries extract fully from **245,897 B**, **393,028 B** and
**167,455 B** (bisected minima). The per-entry refusal machinery is unchanged;
nothing in the sample reaches it.

**The CLI's `--budget` simulation agrees with the Mac allocator to 48 bytes**
(re-validated 2026-08-24): `macextract --budget 597036` predicts 482,297 B and
559,241 B for Troi-ClipSave and Library_ISBN; the Mac measures 482,345 B and
559,289 B. Every other budget figure in this file is a Linux number.

**This tier went 39/44 → 44/44 in six days**, one DECISIONS.md entry per step:

| date | what changed | tier |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-02 | the shell stopped reading the whole archive into RAM | 39/44 → 40/44 |
| 2026-08-03 | the Arsenic block stopped being allocated at its declared 512 KB | 40/44 → 41/44 |
| 2026-08-06 | the block's first allocation sized from the entry, so growth strands no heap | 41/44 → 42/44 |
| 2026-08-07 | the transform vector is not built when it does not fit | 42/44 → 44/44 |

`derived-RWAsm_Folder.sit` went 595.7 KB → **160,766 B** across the first two.

**`popper-1.7.tar.Z` and `PCCTS.tar.gz` pass** since the Mac sink stopped
accumulating whole output forks (their 174 KB and 287 KB outputs are the
sample's largest); the old path stays buildable as `UNC_MAC_STAGE_WRITES`.
BLOCKERS.md, *Corrected figures*, and DECISIONS.md for the HFS param-block
rewrite and the withdrawn `HOpen` attribution.

## System 3.0 tier (Mini vMac — System 3.0 / Finder 5.1, Mac Plus, 68000)

The oldest system a Macintosh Plus can boot (System Tools 1.0, an 800K HFS
floppy). **74/74 byte-exact PASS at 1 MB and at 4 MB** (BLOCKERS.md,
*Status*), replayed by `tools/vmac/sys30.py`.

**The application is launched by double-click**: System 3.0 ignores the boot
blocks' shell name (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-30, *the shell name System 3.0 does
not read*). Launching unloads the Finder: **12 bytes** of difference on
System 6.0.8 between a boot-shell launch and a double-click one.

The four heaviest allocations as the Mac reports them (1 MB column
2026-10-09, 4 MB column 2026-10-10; the 4 MB figures are identical on System
6.0.8 and 7.0.1). At 4 MB `derived-Cool_Tools__.sit` is second, 848,721 B; no
1 MB figure for it has been recorded since 2026-09-22:

| fixture | 4 MB peak | 1 MB peak | out |
|---|---|---|---|
| `derived-Library_ISBN_Barcoder.sit` | 1,353,789 B | 644,858 B | 34 files, 641,115 B |
| `derived-a2basic.dmg.sit` | 829,041 B | 703,565 B | 205,019 B |
| `derived-Troi-ClipSave-Plug-in1.1.sit` | 530,656 B | 530,656 B | 13 files, 796,486 B |
| `synth-dd-m5.dd` | 405,520 B | 405,520 B | 1,145 B |

The largest output on the tier is `derived-XferIt_1.5b4.sea`, 948,175 B across
10 files, at 24,217 B of peak heap. Troi-ClipSave's 1 MB peak is the System
6.0.8 floor's to the byte, as a `FreeMem`-derived budget predicts.

**The application end to end**: Finder launch, Standard File, the Drive
button, the destination and progress dialogs, *"Extraction cancelled."* from
Cmd-. and *"Extraction complete."*, then the extracted application
double-clicked — `tools/vmac/interactive.py --e2e --system Sys30.img`, ~133 s
of emulated Mac Plus time on `derived-ScrollbarPattern.sit`.

### 512 KB machines: the RAM is enough, the ROM is not

**A Macintosh 512Ke (512 KB, the Plus's 128K ROM) under System 3.0: 72/74**
(BLOCKERS.md, *Status*; `sys30.py --emu mv-512Ke`), and **68/72 with every
decoder resident** (2026-10-09). The two still refused fail per entry, as
designed: `derived-Library_ISBN_Barcoder.sit` writes 33 of 34, and
`synth-dd-m5.dd`'s one entry asks for one 405,520 B block on every tier.

**The overlays recover `derived-a2basic.dmg.sit` and `derived-Cool_Tools__.sit`**
— the only tier where they open an archive rather than just grant heap
(*Overlays*). They need 240,898 B and 242,464 B in one block against the
resident build's budget; the shipped build grants 82–105 KB more.

**The resident build's budget was 205,476 B, measured 2026-10-09**: a 225,956 B
free heap (`sys30.py --emu mv-512Ke -D UNC_OVL_RESIDENT -D UNC_AT_BUDGET`,
slots swapped per `tools/vmac/README.md`) less the flat 20,480 B reserve.
Re-taken on the machine at **201,590 B** on 2026-10-22; `tools/run_tiers.py`
now asserts **198,926 B**, derived from the control build's growth since
(CORRECTED 2026-10-25: this read "205,476 B, the figure run_tiers.py
asserts"). History of the figure: *Budget*.

**68/72 is the resident build's ceiling**, a property of four archives
against that budget. `tools/run_tiers.py` reproduces it fixture for fixture,
and with a budget per format (*What each format is granted*) the shipped
build's refusals by name. Minimum budgets, bisected 2026-09-22:

| fixture | needs | of which | against 205,476 B (the measurement) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `derived-a2basic.dmg.sit` | **240,898 B** | one 205,020 B BWT block — the entry's whole 205,019 B output in a single block — plus the walk state | 1.17x |
| `derived-Cool_Tools__.sit` | **242,464 B** | one block of the same shape, over a 206,582-byte resource fork | 1.18x |
| `derived-Library_ISBN_Barcoder.sit` | **388,033 B** | one 352,161 B block, same shape | 1.89x |
| `synth-dd-m5.dd` | **401,408 B** | 1,536 B per distinct context byte, all 256 of them | 1.95x |

A block a BWT inverts must be resident, and DiskDoubler method 5's 256 splay
trees are adaptive state, so no allocation strategy reaches these; a smaller
archive would, not a smaller engine. The line is the BLOCK SIZE the
compressor chose, not the format: the sample's other five StuffIt 5 fixtures
pass, `derived-Troi-ClipSave-Plug-in1.1.sit` needing 162,618 B.

**What is refused is one entry's working set, never the archive**, and the
demand is the codec's, not the file size's: `synth-dd-m5.dd` outputs 1,145 B
and asks for 405,520 B. **Nor is the boundary a number**:
`derived-PuzzleGetter_0.8.sit` wants 270,043 B at 1 MB and passes here at
162,567 B because StuffIt 5's block renegotiates downward;
`derived-a2basic.dmg.sit` wants 247,687 B and is refused at 37,372 B because
its block is under the renegotiation floor.

**System 6.0.8 on the same machine: 68/74 shipped** (BLOCKERS.md, *Status*),
**63/72 resident** (`run.py --emu mv-512Ke --system System6.hfv`, 2026-10-09,
the nine `run_tiers.py` named). System 3.0's failures are a strict subset of
System 6's, and the difference is the SYSTEM HEAP: free heap at extraction
**225,956 B** under System 3.0 against **130,556 B** under System 6.0.8 (both
2026-10-09), a gap of 95,400 B — the same to the byte the sixth time taken —
against System files of 181,913 B and 1,221,287 B. The reserve is the same
constant on both, so the budget gap is the same 95,400 B. (It read "~74 KB"
until 2026-09-22: the difference between two peaks on one fixture.)

**The shipped System 6 tier was PREDICTED before it was run**:
`tools/run_tiers.py --tier sys6-512K --shipped` said 66/71 on 2026-09-27 and
named five; the machine refused those five and no others. On 2026-10-09 the
model was wrong — it passed `a2ps.V4.3.tar.Z`, which the machine refused
(granted 81,938 B, needs 86,080) — because its bases were 16,888 B stale;
re-taken, it names all six (BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*). The six:
`a2ps.V4.3.tar.Z`, `derived-Cool_Tools__.sit`,
`derived-Library_ISBN_Barcoder.sit`, `derived-a2basic.dmg.sit`,
`popper-1.7.tar.Z` and `synth-dd-m5.dd`; the two `.Z` files are envelopes and
keep the whole bundle (*What each format is granted*). A 2,926 B discrepancy
under that model was the stale base (DECISIONS.md 2026-09-27, *the residue
that was two numbers*).

**The reserve is a flat 20 KB on every tier**: `budget = FreeMem() - 20,480`,
reproduced to the byte by the 2026-09-22 and 2026-09-27 probes on all three
machines. Replacing the quarter rule recovered
`derived-Troi-ClipSave-Plug-in1.1.sit` here (65/69 → 66/69); under System
6.0.8 the quarter always fell under the old 32 KB floor, so only the stack cut
moved that tier (BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*). **A kilobyte of
application costs the engine a kilobyte here too.** On
`derived-Troi-ClipSave-Plug-in1.1.sit` with every decoder resident the two
systems wrote 11 entries at 163,332 B against 6 entries at 89,613 B.

### A true Macintosh 512K: the machine boots, the application bombs

**A 64 KB ROM cannot boot HFS** (MEASURED; "cannot be reached at all" was
withdrawn 2026-08-31, BLOCKERS.md). On HFS the machine dies at startup, **Sad
Mac `0F0064`**, with the 128K ROM image (`28BA61CE`) and the 512K's own
`28BA4E50` alike; a blank HFS volume with no boot blocks is declined, not
fatal, so what fails is the boot code. On **MFS** it boots:

| volume | result on `mv-512K` (64 KB ROM, 512 KB of RAM) |
|---|---|
| `Sys30.img` — Apple's 800K HFS System 3.0 floppy | Sad Mac `0F0064` |
| `Sys11.img` — Apple's 400K MFS System 1.1 floppy | boots to Finder 1.1 |
| `Sys11clone.img` — the same two files, volume rebuilt by `mkmfs.py` | boots to Finder 1.1 |
| `Sys30Boot.img` — System 3.0 + Finder 5.1 lifted out of `Sys30.img` onto MFS, with `Sys30.img`'s own boot blocks | boots the floor system |
| `Sys30MFS.img` — **the 400K MFS floppy Apple shipped System 3.0 on**, volume "System Software" | **boots the floor system**, and is what `setup.sh` fetches |

`setup.sh` gets Apple's MFS disk by unpacking Macintosh Garden's
`System_3.x.sit` with this repository's CLI. Its System file is **140,299 B
against the 800K HFS disk's 181,913 B**, 41,614 B smaller.

**`MaxApplZone` is what bombed** (`ID = 03`), under System 1.1, System 3.0
and as the boot shell alike. `tools/vmac/traptest` paints a mark per call in
the application's order; one disk, two machines differing only in ROM:

| call, in the application's order | 512Ke, 128 KB ROM | 512K, 64 KB ROM |
|---|---|---|
| `SetApplLimit(ApplLimit − kStackBump)` | returns | returns |
| `MaxApplZone()` | returns | **bomb** |
| `NGetTrapAddress` (what `TrapAvailable` uses), twice | returns | returns, and answers correctly |
| `PBHGetVInfoSync` — an HFS parameter block | returns | returns |
| `PBGetCatInfoSync` | returns | **bomb, `ID = 12`, unimplemented core routine** |

The 512Ke column is the control. The last three rows are reachable on a 64K
ROM only with `-DTRAPTEST_NO_MEM`, which drops the first two.

**`MaxApplZone` is `[Not in ROM]`** (Inside Macintosh II-30); Retro68 emits
its 128K-ROM trap inline (`M68K_INLINE(0xA063)`, `Multiverse.h`). Not a
Retro68 defect: it targets System 6 and a 128K ROM. **The bomb's ID is not
evidence**: the same call reports `ID = 03`, `10` or `28` depending on the
build (Inside Macintosh II-365). The second wall is `PBGetCatInfo`'s
`_HFSDispatch` selector, which the application uses in five places.

**Since 2026-10-16 the application starts on a 64K ROM**, on every machine
alike: `GrowApplZone()` replaces `MaxApplZone` with `_MaxMem`, one `NewPtr`
and its `DisposPtr` (Inside Macintosh II-36, II-38). Measured: the zone ends
exactly at `ApplLimit` (System 6.0.8, 1 MB), and the budget is +4, +4 and +6 B
against `MaxApplZone`'s on the three asserted tiers (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-15,
*the purge MaxApplZone never did*). The maintainer's LC 475 (System 7.6.1)
launched it and extracted an archive, 2026-10-16.

On the emulated 512K under Apple's MFS System 3.0 (2026-10-15): Standard
File, the destination dialog, and a non-archive sniffed and refused with
*"This file could not be extracted."* **A real StuffIt archive still bombs
`ID = 12`** at an HFS call. So a 64K-ROM Macintosh is **one call short, and
that call is HFS**, which Apple's `Hard Disk 20` file supplies ("the
hierarchical (128K ROM) version of the File Manager", Inside Macintosh IV,
*The Floppy Disk Interface*). Whether that closes it is **untested and cannot
be tested here** (MANUAL_TESTS.md §6).

**Without that file there is no HFS**, measured under both the 140,299 B and
the 182 KB System 3.0: an HFS floppy gets *"This is not a Macintosh disk"*.
**It cannot be loaded here**: Apple's *HD 20 Startup* disk (in the same
`System_3.x.sit`), and a disk built here with only that file added, stop on
the 512K at an empty dialog frame, byte-identical from 45 s to 210 s — a
deadlock, not a timeout. *Hypothesis, not measured:* Mini vMac's 128K model
lacks the hardware the driver looks for.

Harness facts: **System 3.0 ignores the boot blocks' shell name on MFS as on
HFS**, so a probe is launched by being NAMED `Finder` (`mkmfs.py ...
probe.bin:Finder`); **System 1.1 honours it** (`mkmfs.py --shell TrapTest`),
the only system here that does. **Cmd-. does not cancel Standard File on
System 3.0** (the frame is unchanged, while the same chord cancels this
application's progress dialog); the Cancel button works, and on System 6.0.8
the chord closes the dialog.

### A Macintosh 128K: the RAM stops it before the ROM does

**The 64K-ROM build bombs `ID = 03` on a Macintosh 128K, for memory rather
than ROM.** MEASURED 2026-09-01, `mv-128K` (`setup.sh`, `-m 128K -mem 128K`),
with the 512K on the same volume as the control. `tools/vmac/memtest`, as
the boot shell:

| | Macintosh 128K | Macintosh 512K |
|---|---|---|
| `MemTop` | 131,072 | 524,288 |
| `ApplZone` | 19,968 | 51,968 |
| `ApplLimit` | 98,536 | 491,752 |
| `CurStackBase` | 106,728 | 499,944 |
| `FreeMem` | 4,846 | 4,846 |
| **largest single `NewPtr`** | **63,744** | **424,960** |
| a 142,152 B block — `CODE` 2 on 2026-09-01, before the overlays; it is 38,948 B since (below) | **refused** | granted |

**`FreeMem` is not the instrument**: it reads the same on both, because the
zone grows on demand; a probe must ask for the largest block `NewPtr` returns.

**The segment was the reason until the overlays (2026-09-25); the heap total
is now.** The shipped binary's two `CODE` resources total **45,704 B**
(`CODE` 0 24 B, `CODE` 1 45,680 B — code, data and relocations in one segment
since 2026-10-22's `--mac-single`), read off `dist/MacExtract.bin`'s resource
map 2026-10-25 (CORRECTED: 48,288 B on 2026-10-22's build). That fits inside
the 128K's largest block. It is not the `CODE` figure *Three configurations,
one tree* gives: that is `build/mac/MacExtract.code.bin`, the image
`tools/build_mac.sh` ratchets (38,588 B ceiling, 2026-10-25), without the
data and relocations. 63,744 B was measured against 2026-09-01's build;
*derived, not measured* for this one.

**What does not fit is the sum.** On 2026-10-22's build the heap held `CODE`
1 at 46,896 B once its relocations were given back, `CODE` 0's 24 B and the
10,692 B bss block — **57,612 B** against an application heap of at most
`ApplLimit − ApplZone` = **78,568 B**, leaving 20,956 B for every buffer
(*derived*; DECISIONS.md 2026-10-22, *the runtime the application did not
need*). `CODE` has since fallen 2,584 B; the sum has not been re-derived. The
eager loader then asks for the `'OVLY'` bundle as ONE **184,160 B** block
(*Overlays*). The bound is the RAM under the screen buffer, so no smaller
System moves it (*What the application costs the engine*); the margin is
kilobytes, which is why MANUAL_TESTS.md §6's HD20 question is about a
**512K**.

**What would fit, measured by building it** — cutting rows from the sniff,
format and segment scheme tables and letting `--gc-sections` run.
`m68k-apple-macos-size`, MEASURED 2026-09-01:

| build | code | data + bss | static total |
|---|---|---|---|
| shipping that day — every decoder resident | 147,568 | 44,096 | 191,664 |
| MacBinary + BinHex + StuffIt classic + Compact Pro + PackIt | 59,944 | 29,112 | 89,056 |
| MacBinary + BinHex + StuffIt classic | 52,092 | 29,060 | 81,152 |
| **MacBinary + BinHex** | **34,452** | **23,724** | **58,176** |

The first row is not today's build (*Three configurations, one tree*); the
subset rows have nothing to overlay and stand (*derived*). Against ~88 KB
between `ApplZone` and the screen buffer, stack and buffers included, the
two-envelope build leaves ~30 KB and **fits** (*derived*); adding StuffIt
classic leaves ~7 KB and does not. The shell is 17,358 B of every row, so the
fixed cost breaks it, not the third format; a 128K build would also need HFS
(*A true Macintosh 512K*).

## Mac OS 9 tier (QEMU PowerPC — Mac OS 9.2, `mac99`, 512 MB, 68LC040 emulation)

The top of the System 6 → Mac OS 9 range, and the only tier where the 68k
binary runs through the system's **68LC040 emulator** rather than a 68k CPU.

**22/22 on 2026-10-22**, after the runtime was trimmed (BLOCKERS.md,
*Status*). **22/22 in ONE sweep on each of two routes, on the v0.37.0
source**, 2026-10-10: `--launch` (the interactive application, byte-identical
to `dist/`'s payload; the table's totals) and `--autotest` (onto a plain-HFS
`Out`, the report file read as well as the volume). The routes differ on one
row: the autotest extracts one layer, so `export-hc.hqx` gives the 1,615-byte
`Export.sit`, not the 6,944 bytes inside it. A `-D UNC_AT_BUDGET` sweep of the
same 22 (`tools/vmac/README.md`, *Why an entry is refused*): `e1`, `wa`, `kf`,
the shortfall and the refusal reason 0 on all 22, and `fc=1` — the 68LC040
emulator offers `_HWPriv`, called once for the bundle and once per kept image.
The `UncOptions`-zeroing fix (2026-09-04) cost **147,548 B of code before,
147,544 B after**, 4 B moving to the A5 world; before it every DiskDoubler
archive bombed (DECISIONS.md 2026-09-04, *the field nobody assigned*;
BLOCKERS.md, *Withdrawn claims*). The five off-sample DiskDoubler archives:
**5/5 byte-exact** (2026-09-04). `tools/vmac/os9sample.txt`, one fixture per
format, each in its own boot from a pristine copy of the master image:

    tools/vmac/os9.py --setup                                   # once
    tools/vmac/os9.py --list tools/vmac/os9sample.txt -o <dir> --launch

| fixture | format | files | data bytes | rsrc bytes | result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `export-hc.hqx` | BinHex, and the StuffIt inside it | 1 | 6,944 | 0 | ✅ byte-exact |
| `derived-keyAhead.cpt` | Compact Pro | 1 | 0 | 1,233 | ✅ byte-exact |
| `synth-exo-Z.Z` | compress | 1 | 2,200 | 0 | ✅ byte-exact |
| `synth-exo-gz.gz` | gzip | 1 | 2,200 | 0 | ✅ byte-exact |
| `stuffit151mstr.bin` | MacBinary | 1 | 0 | 365 | ✅ byte-exact |
| `synth-packit-2.pit` | PackIt | 5 | 3,050 | 2,020 | ✅ byte-exact |
| `derived-Export.sit` | StuffIt | 1 | 6,944 | 0 | ✅ byte-exact |
| `derived-DDT.sit` | StuffIt with a resource fork | 2 | 1,473 | 3,504 | ✅ byte-exact |
| `derived-KeyGraphics.sit` | StuffIt 5 / Arsenic | 1 | 0 | 33,484 | ✅ byte-exact |
| `derived-XferIt_1.5b4.sea` | Compact Pro self-extracting | 10 | 679,423 | 268,752 | ✅ byte-exact |
| `derived-DandStoQIF2.sea` | DiskDoubler | 3 | 20,918 | 24,562 | ✅ byte-exact; this tier's refusal case until 2026-08-27 |
| `synth-exo-tar.tar` | tar | 4 | 7,808 | 0 | ✅ byte-exact |
| `synth-exo-zip-deflate.zip` | zip | 4 | 7,808 | 0 | ✅ byte-exact |
| `synth-arc-mixed.arc` | ARC, all nine methods | 9 | 16,200 | 0 | ✅ byte-exact |
| `synth-uu-mac.uu` | uuencode, UUTool's two-fork shape | 1 | 1,350 | 768 | ✅ byte-exact |
| `lhasa-maclha-l1_lh5.lzh` | LZH, MacLHA's MacBinary payload | 1 | 18,092 | 0 | ✅ byte-exact |
| `derived-SD__Utilities.x` | Alysis SuperDisk! | 3 | 9,916 | 14,389 | ✅ byte-exact, including two names holding the `':'`/`'/'` swap |
| `synth-os9-btoa.btoa` | btoa | 1 | 504 | 0 | ✅ byte-exact |
| `synth-os9-mime.mim` | multipart/appledouble | 1 | 320 | 176 | ✅ byte-exact, both forks and the type |
| `synth-now-mixed.now` | Now Compress | 1 | 15,096 | 0 | ✅ byte-exact — added 2026-09-04, the third folder-walking format |
| `derived-800saywhat301j.sit` | StuffIt 5, UTF-8 names | 19 | 141,510 | 103,343 | ✅ byte-exact — added 2026-09-22; three names MacRoman cannot spell come out as 7, 7 and 13 `_`, and `Icon\r` comes out invisible with its 51,781-byte resource fork |
| `synth-os9-rar.rar` | a RAR, so not an archive: must REFUSE | 0 | — | — | ✅ declined, as on Linux; the alert is photographed. `unknown` since 2026-10-18, the same alert (*Archives this engine does not have*) |

**22/22**, 71 files, 941,756 data bytes and 452,596 resource-fork bytes
compared in one `--launch` sweep on 2026-10-10.

`derived-800saywhat301j.sit` found two defects in the HARNESS and none in
the application: `mac_leaf` folded the composed spelling, and `_walk_volume`
(`hls` without `-a`, `rstrip()` on `Icon\r`) could not see invisible entries
on any fixture this tier had run (DECISIONS.md 2026-09-22, *the fold, on a
Macintosh*). The colour icon members and the refusal alert are photographed
(DECISIONS.md 2026-08-24, *the icon*).

This tier reads its result **off the disk**: both forks of every file lifted
out with hfsutils and compared byte for byte against `macextract x`, so it
compares **names, folder structure and 8-bit characters** too, which caught
the gzip/.Z fallback-name defect on its first sweep (TESTING.md).
`derived-XferIt_1.5b4.sea` lands 10 files three folders deep; PackIt's `Appé`
is a MacRoman name.

**The sample is small on purpose:** a fixture costs a whole boot (~110 s plus
a 2 GB image copy) against Mini vMac's ~5 s, so it samples for breadth of
format; memory-budget spread is a 1 MB question.

**The Finder is driven from the keyboard.** The pointer works only stepped,
never through `usb-tablet` (`tools/vmac/os9mouse.py`); the "five primitives at
zero effect" reading is withdrawn, BLOCKERS.md.

### Cancelling, and an 8-bit name, on Mac OS 9 (2026-08-28)

`--cancel` interrupts the extraction with Cmd-., which `ProgressPump()`
catches:

| | measured |
|---|---|
| subject | `derived-Library_ISBN_Barcoder.sit` — StuffIt 5, 34 entries |
| Cmd-. chords needed | **1** |
| entries written | **1 of 34**, `Contents/Info.plist`, **1,381 B byte-exact** |
| entries never started | 33 |
| the entry in flight | left **nothing** on the volume |
| alert | "Extraction cancelled." |
| afterwards | SFGetFile repaints, and Cmd-. there quits the application |

The pass condition is a strict, non-empty subset: a cancel after the last
entry or before the first fails as a useless test (`tools/vmac/README.md`).

**The Cancel BUTTON** — the `DialogSelect` branch of `ProgressPump()` — same
fixture, one boot each:

| | measured |
|---|---|
| `--cancel-click`, on the button at (340,168) | cancels on the **first** press: **1 of 34** entries, 1,381 B byte-exact |
| the control, `--click-at 150,168` (blank dialog background) | does **not** cancel: **34 of 34** written, and the run FAILS as a cancel that arrived too late |
| what the alert reads | "Extraction cancelled." (`16-result.png`, cursor still on the button in `15-progress.png`) |

The pointer is parked on the button while the destination dialog is still up,
because positioning costs ~40 s (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-28, *clicking a dialog
that does not exist yet*).

**The test found a defect on its first outing**: Now Compress never called the
progress callback, so the application pumped no events for the whole
extraction. Fixed the same day (BLOCKERS.md, *withdrawn claims*; TESTING.md,
*The progress callback*); `derived-SyntheticReferences2.0.sea` then cancels on
the FIRST chord, 1 of 2 entries written and byte-exact.

**`19-names.png` photographs an 8-bit name**: `synth-packit-2.pit` carries
`App` + MacRoman `CC A9`, and Mac OS 9 draws it as `AppÃ©` in the Finder's
icon view and in Standard File's list — the pair of characters the Linux CLI
prints after converting MacRoman to UTF-8.

**From the same runs:** `synth-packit-2.pit` (five entries, both forks)
completes within 13 s of the Return, against a ~110-130 s boot; and
`derived-SyntheticReferences2.0.sea` extracts byte-exact, including a
**9,949,696-byte data fork**, the largest single fork this project has
compared off a Macintosh volume.

### An encrypted archive, through the dialog (2026-08-28)

**Nothing in the corpus is encrypted** (*Encryption*, above), so
`os9.py --encrypted` generates its subject with `tools/synth_crypt.py` —
StuffIt classic, DES, three entries — carried as MacBinary because its
verifier `'MKey'` 0 lives in the **resource fork**:

| | measured |
|---|---|
| the wrong password, `wrongone` | shown as **eight bullets**, refused; the dialog returns reading *"That password was refused."* (`15c-refused.png`) |
| `hushnow`, second attempt | accepted; the whole archive extracts |
| what came off the volume | **3 of 3** entries byte-exact against `macextract x -p` — `Both Forks` 510 B data **and 260 B resource**, `Rsrc only` 0 B data and 160 B resource, `Secret Memo` 264 B |

A wrong password that decrypted anything fails the byte comparison. It puts
the `'MKey'` path on a Mac, read through the File Manager rather than a
MacBinary envelope.

### SpaceSaver on Mac OS 9, and the extension the shell was eating (2026-08-28)

Its only specimens are written by Aladdin's own modules under `qemu-m68k` and
cannot be committed; they run by path:

    os9.py ~/.cache/macextract/vmac/specimens/spacesaver/modules/m4.ss --launch

| | measured |
|---|---|
| subject | `m4.ss`, 6,656 B — method 4, the preset-dictionary one |
| output | **76,800 B byte-exact** against `macextract x`, one boot |
| the name | the Mac writes **`archive.sit~1`**: SpaceSaver compressed a file in place, so the output is named after the archive, and the archive is still sitting in the destination folder |

**The first run found a shell defect**: `mac/macextract_mac.c` stripped the
last extension where `cli/main.c` exempts SpaceSaver, so `Report.txt` came
out as `Report` on the Mac only. Fixed the same day; this tier is the
regression test (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-28, *the exception one front end had*).

### Onto HFS+ (the same machine, the boot volume instead of the transfer disk)

`--hfsplus` renames the HFS+ boot volume to `Out` and runs the **autotest**
build against it: the application CRC-32s both forks after reading them back
through the File Manager.

    tools/vmac/os9.py fixtures/derived-Berkeley_Packet_Monitor.sit \
                      -o <dir> --hfsplus

| fixture | files | data | rsrc | peak heap | result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `derived-Berkeley_Packet_Monitor.sit` | 31 | 372,301 B | — | 779.1 KB | ✅ CRC + length identical to Linux |
| `derived-XferIt_1.5b4.sea` | 10 | 679,423 B | 268,752 B | 18.8 KB | ✅ CRC + length identical to Linux |

Both `ret=0`, zero per-entry errors, and every name is what the 31-character
rule predicts, including the Berkeley entry whose name is 34 characters: the
classic File Manager caps a 68k application at 31 on HFS+ as on HFS.

- **The answer comes back as a file**, `at_report_file()` on the plain-HFS
  input volume, because the framebuffer channel is invisible on OS 9's 32-bit
  screen (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-02).
- **That the output landed on HFS+ is measured**: the HFS wrapper still holds
  exactly one file, `Where_have_all_my_files_gone?`, after the run. A
  signature word proves nothing here (BLOCKERS.md).

The Mac's **unmasked** resource-fork CRC equals Linux's **masked** one: Mac
OS 9 leaves the 112-byte reserved header area alone where System 6 stamps it.

### The `SIZE` partition under OS 9's Process Manager

Built with a 600 KB preferred partition, against `derived-KeyGraphics.sit`
(~656 KB needed): **1 entry refused, 0 files, `ret == UNC_OK`, peak heap
35.5 KB** on a 512 MB machine. 35.5 KB is parser state alone; the 512 KB
Arsenic block was declined at request time.

| System | Outcome | Engine peak heap |
|---|---|---|
| System 7.0.1 (4 MB) | **1 entry refused, 0 files** | 35.5 KB |
| Mac OS 9.2 (512 MB) | **1 entry refused, 0 files** | 35.5 KB |
| System 6.0.8 (4 MB) | 1 file, byte-exact | 656.8 KB |

System 6 without MultiFinder ignores `SIZE`; the 656.8 KB row is the control,
the 4 MB sweep's figure. Method in `tools/vmac/README.md` ("The `SIZE`
partition").

**Not covered:** names HFS+ permits that HFS does not — probably unreachable
through the classic File Manager, unproven either way.

## Compared with StuffIt Expander 4.0.2 (Aladdin, April 1996)

The free tool a user would otherwise run, and the external yardstick
(DECISIONS.md 2026-08-15). **The coverage and capability tables are its
documentation against our measurements**, except rows saying *measured*:
since 2026-10-11 the rows on StuffIt Deluxe 2.0's archives, encrypted Compact
Pro, multipart BinHex and MIME are runs of 4.0.2 itself (*Run over the hard
cases*). The times are both applications on the same emulated Mac Plus
(*Timed on the same Mac Plus*).

**Source of the Expander column**: `stuffit_expander_4.0.2.sea_.bin` from
Macintosh Garden (MacBinary around a `.sea` around a 400K HFS image).
`macextract x` produced an image byte-identical to `unar`'s (410,624 B, md5
`888c0ca0b93f6ee0ff67e89926e20f83`); every quotation is read out of that
image. Web sources are used for dates and later versions, never for what
4.0.2 does.

### Coverage over this corpus

805 files in `fixtures/` (the corpus before M13), classified by INNERMOST
format — `x -nr`, then `macextract sniff` — measured 2026-08-15:

| Innermost format | files | Expander 4.0.2 | MacExtract 0.10 |
|---|---|---|---|
| StuffIt classic, incl. `.sea` | 454 | ✅ | ✅ |
| Compact Pro, incl. `.sea` | 216 | ✅ | ✅ |
| envelope over a non-archive | 34 | ✅ | ✅ ✧✧✧ |
| StuffIt 5 / Arsenic | 62 | ❌ postdates it | ✅ |
| zip, gzip, `.Z`, tar | 16 | ⚠️ paid add-on, System 7 only | ✅ |
| PackIt | 5 | ❌ not in its list | ✅ |
| DiskDoubler, Now Compress | 14 | ❌ | ✅ since 2026-08-15 |
| Alysis SuperDisk! | 2 | ❌ not in its list | ✅ since 2026-08-20 |
| LZH, StuffIt X | 2 | ❌ | LZH ✅ since 2026-08-23; StuffIt X detect-only, named |
| **total** | **805** | **704 alone, 720 with the add-on** | **804 extract, 1 named** |

✧✧✧ 31 envelopes whose payload is a resource-only file, and 3 whose payload
is a document: two HyperCard `STAK`s and one damaged Compact Pro archive whose
index offset points past its own end (`unar` declines it too).

The 62 StuffIt 5 fixtures are a date, not a capability: 4.0.2 shipped two
years before the format, and answers *"You may need a newer version of the
StuffIt Engine to work with this archive."*

### Capability by capability

| | StuffIt Expander 4.0.2 | MacExtract |
|---|---|---|
| Floor system | System 6.0.5 (its Read Me) | System 3.0 on a 68000 with 512 KB, measured on a Macintosh 512Ke; 1 MB for every archive (BLOCKERS.md, *Status*) |
| Everything beyond `.sit`/`.cpt`/`.hqx`/`.bin` | needs DropStuff with Expander Enhancer, *"a **System 7-only** shareware product"* | in the same free application, on System 6 |
| Installed pieces | application + StuffIt Engine extension + the add-on | one 232 KB application (237,328 B, the resource fork of `dist/MacExtract.bin`, v0.44.0), no extension, no installer |
| StuffIt segmented sets | with the add-on, so System 7 only | yes, System 6 and up |
| Compact Pro segmented sets | *"StuffIt Expander cannot expand encrypted or segmented Zip or Compact Pro files"* | yes |
| Spanned zip sets | same sentence — no | yes |
| Plain `.001`/`.002` splits | not claimed | yes |
| Parts still inside per-part envelopes | not claimed | yes, StuffIt sets |
| How a part is identified | not stated | by its header, never by its filename (zip and `.NNN` sets excepted — see "Segmented archives") |
| Segment joining, known defect | *"if the `Delete segments after joining` preference is selected, and you run out of disk space during a join operation, the first segment will be deleted even though the joining is not complete"* | parts are never deleted; the Mac shell removes only what it wrote |
| Encrypted archives | yes — it prompts, and knows *"You have entered an incorrect password"*. Measured 2026-10-11: Compact Pro 1.51's encrypted archive refused with no prompt; StuffIt Deluxe 2.0's five ciphers prompted for, then refused (*Run over the hard cases*) | yes, since 2026-08-23: StuffIt, StuffIt 5, PackIt, zip (PKWARE and WinZip AES), ARC — and **Compact Pro, which Expander cannot** |
| btoa (`.ascii`) | with the add-on: a `btoa/atob Translator` ships in `Extensions:Aladdin:Translators` | yes, both framings (*btoa*, above) |
| Multipart BinHex | *"Multipart BinHex files, such as those appearing in Internet newsgroup postings (e.g. comp.binaries.mac), are fully supported"*. Measured 2026-10-11: a three-part posting with a mail header before each part, in one file, decodes byte-exact and chains into the archive inside | several documents in one file since 2026-08-15 (††, above); a document broken across FILES joins where the parts are named `.001`, `.002` … |
| Archive comments | written out beside the extracted items | saved since 2026-08-23 for Compact Pro and 2026-08-26 for zip: the Mac shell writes a `TEXT` document `Comments` into the destination folder, the CLI writes `<archive>.comment.txt` and prints it on `l` and `t` |
| `.lzh` / `.lha` (LZH / LHA) | not named in its documentation, no translator ships for it | yes since 2026-08-23, every method, all four header levels, MacLHA's MacBinary payload split back into two forks (*LZH / LHA*, above) |
| `.arc` (ARC / ArcMac) | with the add-on | yes since 2026-08-15, methods 1-9; ArcMac's two-fork payload since 2026-08-16 (★★★ above) |
| uuencode (`.uu`) | with the add-on | yes since 2026-08-15, plain and UUTool's two-fork shape |
| AppleSingle / AppleDouble | with the add-on: an `AppleSingle` translator ships; **not run on 4.0.2 alone**. Deluxe 5.5.1's and 7.0.3's Expanders are measured below | yes, both forks and every field RFC 1740 holds (*Finder info in the archive*, above) |
| base64 and MIME (`.b64`, `.mim`) | no translator for either ships, neither is named. Measured 2026-10-11, 4.0.2 alone: an mpack message and an mpack `message/partial` set each produce nothing and no alert | yes since 2026-08-26, including multipart/appledouble (*base64 and MIME*, above) |
| AppleLink `.pkg` | with the add-on, recognised by its EXTENSION (its `STR# 129` carries `.pkg` for stripping, not matching) | extracted since 2026-10-05, recognised by the file rather than its name (*AppleLink packages*, above) |
| StuffIt SpaceSaver | with the add-on | yes since 2026-08-22; every method (0–4) since 2026-08-25, its dictionary built in (*StuffIt SpaceSaver*, above) |
| Chained expansion (`.sit.hqx`) | yes, and can be turned off | yes, `-nr` turns it off |
| System 7 conveniences | drag-and-drop of files, folders and whole disks; watch folders; background expansion; Apple events | an archive dropped on the application's icon opens it, whatever its type (System 7 and later, measured: DECISIONS.md 2026-08-31, *the drop the Finder does not make*); otherwise Standard File, one archive at a time; no watch folders, background expansion or Apple events |
| Self-mounting disk images (`.img`, `.smi`) | **no, read from its own files 2026-10-11**: the `open` 128 resource lists the 21 types it accepts (`SIT!`, `SITD`, `PACT`, `HBSF`, `TEXT`, five segment types, `SPSV`, `mArc`, `m@rc`, `ZIVU`, `ZIVM`, `Gzip`, `pZIP`, `ZIP `, `fold`, `disk`, `****`) and none is a disk image; no disk-image string occurs in the application or its documents. Not run on a `.img` | no — disk images are out of scope (OPEN_ITEMS.md, Part 1) |
| StuffIt Deluxe 2.0's Optimizers and ciphers | **measured 2026-10-11**: `Text 1` extracts, byte-identical to this engine's; the other four Optimizers and all five ciphers are refused with *"Unable to decompress some items due to use of optimizers."* | all ten (*StuffIt 2.0's pluggable modules*, above) |
| Verification | none published | 980 fixtures byte-exact against `unar`/`macunpack` — bar the three uuencode fixtures, which `unar` cannot read at all: one is its generator's input with `hexbin` agreeing, and the two wild ones are graded by `hexbin` and by the CRCs of the archive one of them decodes to |
| Licence | free, closed | free, LGPL-2.1-or-later |
| Memory partition | **512 KB preferred, 384 KB minimum** (`SIZE` (-1), measured 2026-08-29; BLOCKERS.md) | 2,048 KB preferred, 400 KB minimum (`SIZE` (-1) of `dist/MacExtract.bin`, re-read 2026-10-25); engine peak heap per format in the matrix above |

### The paid box: StuffIt Deluxe 3.5.1 (2026-09-27)

**The paid StuffIt of the 68k era**, read off the installed image
`~/.cache/macextract/vmac/spacesaver/ssdeluxe.hfv` (volume `System_7_0_1`,
DECISIONS.md 2026-08-22, *it runs, and it will not compress on demand*) and
its `Read Us First!` folder. No row is a head-to-head run: Deluxe has never
been timed. README.md's table is 4.0.2, 5.5.1 and 7.0.3.

**One purchase**: `Drag & Drop Applications` holds `DropStuff™` 3.5.2,
`StuffIt Expander™` 3.5.2, `DropConvert™` and `DropCompress™` beside
`StuffIt Deluxe™`; the installer adds `~Magic Menu™` and `~StuffIt
SpaceSaver™` (Control Panels) and `StuffIt Engine™` and `Viewer Engine™`
(Extensions), all listed in its `What was installed(and where)`.

**Thirteen translator files, fourteen Translate-menu entries.**
`System Folder:Extensions:Aladdin:Translators` holds AppleSingle, BinHex4,
btoa/atob, CompactPro, DeArc, MacBinary, PKG Expand, tar, Text, Unix Compress,
UnPack, UnZip and UUCode, identical on all 42 `ss*.hfv` images. The menu
(`specimens/spacesaver/deluxe/12-menu-x300.png`) adds **Segmenting**, which
has no file.

**What the box expands**: `.sit`/`.sea`, the thirteen translators, SpaceSaver
through its control panel, StuffIt segments, and **gzip** — *"Added support
for gzip expansion. This can be found by choosing UnZip from StuffIt's
translate menu."* (`What's New in Deluxe 3.5.1`).

**What it does not**: StuffIt 5; `.lzh`, Now Compress, SuperDisk!, base64 and
MIME, none named — and **DiskDoubler, which it stopped expanding**:
*"DDExpand removed (Symantec no longer supports this feature)"*, same file.

**Which version could, measured 2026-09-28.** **3.0.7**
(`stuffitdeluxe307_dsk.zip`, md5 `60ac40fb4cdf8f1ccfaa94944578bf07`) carries a
**`DD Translator`** (`SITo`/`SIT!`) beside an `AppleLink Package Translator`,
which 3.5 **replaces** with `PKG Expand`, a different build whose code
resource is named `CompactPro` (*AppleLink packages*, above). DiskDoubler stays
gone through **4.0** (3.5.1's thirteen plus a `Claris Translator`), **4.5**
(`LHa` and `MIME` arrive, `UnPack` and `PKG Expand` go) and **5.0.1** (4.5's
thirteen). **It comes back at 5.5**: *"DiskDoubler expansion now supported ...
through all available expansion methods"*, its translator carrying `.dd`;
survives into 6.x (the 6.0.1 updater names `:Translators:DiskDoubler
Translator`); gone by 7.0.3. **Expandable by 3.0.x, again from 5.5, and by
nothing Aladdin shipped in between or after.**

**Encryption and comments.** *"With the exception of StuffIt 3.X archives,
StuffIt Expander will not expand encrypted or password protected archives"*
and *"All other expansion methods … WILL save comments within StuffIt archives
to a separate TeachText file called ``Comments about [name of archive]''"*
(`StuffIt Deluxe Read Me`). Deluxe wrote the three encrypted StuffIt archives
this engine is graded against (*Encryption*, above).

### The last 68k box: StuffIt Deluxe 5.5.1 (2026-09-28)

**The last StuffIt for a 68k Macintosh, and the widest Macintosh coverage.**
*What's New in Deluxe 6.0*: *"This has prompted us to also drop our 68k Mac
support for this version. For users of 68K Macs that wish to have StuffIt
technology, simply download any 5.5 version of Deluxe, DropStuff, or
Expander"*. 5.5.1's Read Me: *"The recommended system configuration for
StuffIt Deluxe 5.5 is a Macintosh with System 7.5.3 or greater ... StuffIt
Expander and DropStuff require System 7.1.1 or greater"*, pointing 7.5.3
users at the **CFM-68K Runtime Enabler**. `STUFFITDLX551.SIT`, md5
`27a0b498f5cd7a892e44241db30b2a42`.

**Seventeen translators**: `AppleSingle`, `BinHex`, `btoa:atob`, **`BZip`**,
`CompactPro`, `DeArc`, **`DiskDoubler`**, `LHa`, `MacBinary`, `MIME`, `tar`,
`Text`, `Unix Compress`, **`UnPack`**, `UUCode`, **`WinSEA`** and `Zip`
(`Zip` holds `.zip` and `.gz`, `UnPack` `.pit` and `PackIt`, `BZip` `.bz2`).
Expander 5.5 adds `AppleLink (.pkg)`, `Disk Images (.img, .smi)` and
`Private File (.pf)`, and *"Expand StuffIt SpaceSaver Files"*; 6.0's Read Me:
*"StuffIt SpaceSaver is no longer a part of the StuffIt Deluxe package"*.

**5.5 is the one version with PackIt, DiskDoubler, AppleLink `.pkg`,
SpaceSaver and Compact Pro at once**, plus `.lzh`, MIME, bzip2, zip and gzip.
It lacks Now Compress, SuperDisk!, Compact Pro encryption, mail-split archives
and non-StuffIt segments (the middle two measured on its Expander 2026-10-11,
with StuffIt 2.0's archives, of which it opens two ciphers out of ten modules:
*Run over the hard cases*).

**Processor floors.** **4.5** names none (*"the minimum system is now
7.1.1"*); **5.0.1**: *"The minimum hardware requirement is a 68020 processor,
8MB of installed RAM, and a hard disk drive."*; **5.5 names none**, so the
`68020` in README.md's row is carried from 5.0.1 — an **inference, not a
quotation**. **Expander 5.5** (*"tested to run on any Mac OS from System 7.1.1
through 9.0"*, *"... of only 384K"*) names no processor and may be the last
68000-capable StuffIt — untested, since this machine's System 7 is 7.0.1. The
newest StuffIt measured on a 68000 here is **Expander 4.0.2**, with **Deluxe
3.5.1** (DECISIONS.md 2026-08-22). 5.5 cannot be timed: every emulator harness
here is a Mac Plus.

### The last classic box: StuffIt Deluxe 7.0.3 Classic (2026-09-28)

**The last StuffIt for classic Mac OS; no 68000 Macintosh runs it.**
`STFDLX703CLASSIC.SIT`, md5 `7cec7ff83e2bbe984a2b5a152c466e8d`, fetched with
`tools/vmac/spacesaver.py`'s recipe (`-A Mozilla/5.0 -e <the Garden page>`
against `old.mac.gdn`). Every executable is PEF `pwpc` with no `CODE`; the
Read Me asks for *"at least Mac OS 8.6, Mac OS 9.1 or later is recommended"*
and *"at least 32mb of RAM installed"*.

**Twenty-three format plug-ins**: `apple`, `arc`, `arj`, `binhex`, `btoa`,
`bz2`, `compress`, `cpt`, `freeze`, `gz`, `lha`, `macbin`, `mime`, `pack`,
`pf`, `rar`, `sco`, `sit`, `sitseg`, `sitx`, `tar`, `uu`, `zip`. `pf` is
Private File. `pack`, `sco` and `freeze` are unidentified; `pack` is **not**
PackIt, which 7.0.3 names nowhere.

**The Users Guide** (ch. 18, *Supported Archive Formats*): creates `.sit`,
`.sitx`, `.zip`, `.tar` and `.lzh`; expands `.arj`, `.arc`, `.cpt`, StuffIt
3.x, StuffIt 1.5.1, `.pf` and `.rar`; compresses gzip, bzip and Unix compress;
encodes MacBinary, UUEncode, BinHex and AppleSingle; expands MIME/Base 64.
Expander adds disk images and btoa. **PackIt, SpaceSaver, AppleLink `.pkg`
and DiskDoubler appear nowhere.** It does not do those four, Compact Pro
encryption, mail-split archives or non-StuffIt segments (*"does not currently
support segmented or spanned Zip archives"*); the middle two and StuffIt 2.0's
archives are measured in *Run over the hard cases*.

**It found StuffIt 5 method 14 missing from this engine** (84 of the
installer's 258 entries refused on v0.34.0); **closed 2026-10-03**, all 258
extract (*StuffIt 5's method 14*, above).

### Run over the hard cases: 4.0.2, 5.5.1 and 7.0.3 (2026-10-11)

**Measured.** Expander 4.0.2 alone on System 6.0.8 (`tools/vmac/exp402opt.py`,
Mini vMac); 5.5.1's and 7.0.3's own Expanders with their own Engine and
translators on Mac OS 9.2 (`tools/vmac/compat9.py`). Every output read off
the disk and compared with this engine's. Subjects: StuffIt Deluxe 2.0.1's
own archives (`tools/run_sitopt.py`'s specimens, password `swordfish`),
Compact Pro 1.51's encrypted `cptmade/Made.cpt` (`ohsecret`), and
`MacROT13.cpt` (out of `mac-rot13.hqx`) as an mpack `message/partial` set of
three, one unsplit mpack message, and a three-part BinHex posting in one file.

| subject | Expander 4.0.2 | Deluxe 5.5.1's Expander | Deluxe 7.0.3's Expander |
|---|---|---|---|
| plain 2.0.1 archive (control) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| `Text 1` | ✅ silently, byte-identical to this engine's, which `run_sitopt.py` grades against 2.0.1's own | ❌ a 1-byte file, *"The integrity of the data fork of "Subject" could not be verified. Use this file with caution."* | ❌ *"(Format error). Error #17540"*, an empty file left |
| `MacPaint 1`, `Sound 1`, `Photoshop 1` | ❌ nothing written, *"Unable to decompress some items due to use of optimizers."* | ❌ each data fork 1 byte, the same integrity alert (both forks for `MacPaint 1`); the untouched resource forks are right | ❌ error #17540; resource forks only |
| `JPEG Lossy` | ❌ as above | ❌ 14,127 B where the file is 196,608, integrity alert | ❌ error #17540 |
| `DES`, `NewDE™` | ❌ password prompted for and accepted, then the optimizer alert | ✅ both, byte-exact | ❌ `DES` prompts, then *"(The archive may contain damaged content). Error #17538"*; `NewDE` gets #17540 with no prompt |
| `PassLock` | ❌ as `DES` | ❌ prompts for a password the format does not use, writes 546 B of wrong data, two integrity alerts | ❌ prompt, then #17538 |
| `NewDEx`, `DESex` | ❌ as `DES` | ❌ prompt, then *"Some items were encrypted or compressed with an optimizer that the StuffIt Engine does not currently support. They have been skipped."* | ❌ prompt, then #17538 |
| encrypted Compact Pro | ❌ no prompt: *"Unable to decompress this archive because the file is password protected."* | ❌ the same sentence, word for word; an empty folder | ❌ no prompt: *"(Unspecified StuffIt Engine internal error). Error #17999"*; one empty file |
| `message/partial`, part 1 opened | ❌ nothing, no alert | ❌ *"Decoding was incomplete because there appears to be a missing segment."*, and an empty `MacROT13.cpt` | ❌ nothing, no alert |
| `message/partial`, all three opened together | not tried | ❌ *"Unable to find the first part of the multiple-part archive "rot13.mime.02"."*, then error −43; nothing written | ❌ nothing, no alert |
| the same payload as ONE mpack message | ❌ nothing, no alert | ✅ byte-exact, and on into the archive | ✅ byte-exact, under the alert #17538† |
| the three-part BinHex posting | ✅ byte-exact, and on into the archive | ✅ byte-exact, and on into the archive | ❌ a 0-byte `MacROT13.cpt`, no alert |

† *Hypothesis*, untested: the message's `Content-MD5` was not MD5 — our LP64
mpack wrote both MIME subjects (DECISIONS.md 2026-10-11, *the digest our own
oracle could not compute*); 5.5.1 raised none on the same file.

**Every StuffIt opens some of 2.0's archives and none opens most.** Deluxe
3.5.1's Engine handles `Text 1` as 4.0.2 does and is the only one of the four
not run here (TESTING.md, *The optimized StuffIt archives*). **No StuffIt
joins a `message/partial` set.** 4.0.2's multipart BinHex claim holds; 7.0.3
fails it.

**What this cannot see.** Each box's Deluxe APPLICATION (5.5's will not start
on an assembled installation, `tools/vmac/README.md`, *What `--probe`
measured*; 7.0.3's was not driven); 4.0.2 *with* DropStuff with Expander
Enhancer, which carries no MIME translator (*The paid box*); and more than one
subject per row.

### Zip methods in the three boxes (2026-10-11)

**Measured** with `compat9.py --set zip`; outputs checked against the stored
CRC-32, with Expander's CRLF→CR conversion of `TEXT` undone. Subjects: wild
Reduce archives `123MACRO.ZIP` (16 entries, all method 5) and `COVER.ZIP`
(one Shrink, one method 5) (*Shrink*, above), `4acalc.zip` (Implode), and
`synth-exo-zip-deflate.zip` as the control.

| subject | Deluxe 5.5.1's Expander | Deluxe 7.0.3's Expander |
|---|---|---|
| Reduce, `123MACRO.ZIP` | ✅ 16 of 16, no alert | ❌ *"(The archive may contain damaged content). Error #17538"*, then *"unexpectedly quit ... error of type 2"*: one entry written with 2,033 of its 2,180 bytes wrong, the other 15 never written |
| Shrink + Reduce, `COVER.ZIP` | ✅ 2 of 2 | ❌ the Shrink entry right, the Reduce entry wrong (2,167 of 2,304 bytes), alert #17538 |
| Implode, `4acalc.zip` | ✅ 3 of 3 | ✅ 3 of 3 |
| deflate (control) | ✅ | ✅ |

**Both claim Reduce; only 5.5.1 decodes it.** 7.0.3's `zip` plug-in carries
`algorithm::zip::reduce_decompressor`; 5.5.1's Engine and 3.5.1's `UnZip
Translator` say *"Only stored, shrunk, reduced and deflated formats are
supported."*, and 5.5.1 extracts Implode anyway. **4.0.2 is not measured**
(zip needs the add-on, not on this machine). Only method 5 was run; wild
method 2–4 entries are counted under *Reduce*.

### Finder metadata and Mac OS X zips in the three boxes (2026-10-19)

Measured: each Deluxe's own Expander on emulated Mac OS 9.2, every result read
off the volume (`hfscat.py`), comments searched for in `Desktop DB` (a
hand-typed Get Info comment found there first, the control). Archives from
the maintainer's hardware pass: `Ascii Converter.sit`,
`NorbikNudleys_Icons.sit`, `Comment.as`, `Locked.pit`, `synth-U1.sit`,
`Aqua_Candy_Two.zip`, `Spotlight.zip`. Expander 4.0.2's StuffIt column is
*Finder info in the archive*'s.

| | Deluxe 5.5.1 | Deluxe 7.0.3 Classic |
|---|---|---|
| folder windows | classic SIT exact; StuffIt 5 162/162 | classic SIT all 0; StuffIt 5 view zeroed on 151/162 |
| file icon positions | classic SIT 4/7 (the 3 one folder down misplaced, inited lost); StuffIt 5 153/153 | classic SIT 0/7; StuffIt 5 2/153; AppleSingle 0/1 |
| custom folder icons | ✅ (`Icon\r` typed `TEXT/ttxt`) | ✅ |
| lock | classic SIT 2/2 | classic SIT 2/2; AppleSingle dropped |
| AppleSingle comment | format not read, silently | ✅ |
| Mac OS X Finder zip | refused, "Unknown zip header format encountered" | extracts; forks, types, creators and comments lost; `__MACOSX` left |

**PackIt and AppleSingle in 5.5.1's Expander wrote nothing, silently**, from a
real `PMag` archive typed either way. README's PackIt ✅ for 5.5.1 rests on
its `UnPack` translator's resources (*The last 68k box*) and is kept; that a
default preference disables it is a hypothesis, not tested.

### AppleSingle and AppleDouble in 7.0.3, by type, creator and name (2026-10-23)

Asked by MacStamp. Measured with `tools/vmac/as703.py`:
`fixtures/synth-as-meta.as` (the maintainer's `Comment.as` — "Kept Whole",
data 416 B, resource 286 B, comment, lock) placed nine times, each in its own
folder. "Extracted" is `Kept Whole` `TEXT/ttxt` with both forks byte-exact,
in a `Comment Folder` beside the source.

| | name | type/creator | handed over by | 7.0.3 Expander |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| a1 | `Comment.as` | `BINA/SITx` | Finder, Cmd-Down | extracted |
| a2 | `Comment.as` | `????/SITx` | Finder | extracted |
| a3 | `Comment` | `BINA/SITx` | Finder | extracted |
| a4 | `Comment` | `????/SITx` | Finder | extracted |
| a5 | `Comment.as` | `TEXT/SITx` | Finder | extracted |
| a6 | `Comment.as` | `????/????` | File ▸ Expand (Navigation Services lists it) | extracted |
| d1 | `._Comment` + data file | header `BINA/SITx` | Finder | refused, *"Unspecified StuffIt Engine internal error"*, Error #17999 |
| d2 | `%Comment` + data file | header `BINA/SITx` | Finder | refused, same |
| d3 | `._Comment` alone | `BINA/SITx` | Finder | refused, same |

**AppleSingle is recognised by its magic; no type, creator or `.as` is
needed.** **AppleDouble is not a format 7.0.3 has**: its resources name only
AppleSingle, and each refused run left a 367-byte stub named by the bytes
`16 07 00 02 00` — the header less its first 128 bytes. That it took the
header for MacBinary is a hypothesis from the stub's shape. Other routes, the
same day (`as703.py --set boxes`; *What the pointer cannot select* in
tools/vmac/README.md):

| program, route | AppleSingle | AppleDouble header |
|---|---|---|
| Expander 7.0.3, dropped on its icon | extracted, from `????/????` | refused, Error #17999, the same 367-byte stub |
| Deluxe 7.0.3, `Translate ▸ Expand…` | extracted, from `????/????` | refused, *"An error occurred while decoding"*, Error 17999, the same stub |
| Deluxe 7.0.3, `File ▸ Open…` | not listed: its kinds are StuffIt, self-extracting, zip and tar | — |
| Tiger 1.1 (1992), Finder double-click on `BINA/TIGR` | extracted | not asked |

Every extraction is `Kept Whole`, both forks byte-exact. Read off the catalog,
Deluxe and the Expander keep type, creator, flags, the three dates and the
extended info's script, comment ID and put-away folder, and lose the icon
position, icon ID and lock. Tiger keeps type, creator, flags and the folder
word only. The Get Info comments were not read.

**`BINA/TIGR`** (`--set tigr`). Mac OS 9.2's Internet Config table (74
entries) maps `application/applefile` to `BINA/TIGR`, *"Tiger"*, flags
`0x0D`, **post-processed by `SITx`**. **Mac OS 8.1's** (MacStamp's
`fixtures/databases/ICMapping.bin`, 278 entries): flags `0x05` and no
post-processor. Nothing in 5.5.1 or 7.0.3 names `TIGR`; the Expander's bundle
claims `BINA` (`FREF` 135). Double-clicked on 7.0.3, the Finder's
alternate-program dialog offered **StuffIt Expander as the one recommended
choice**, which extracted it byte-exact.

**MacExtract is in that dialog only if its bundle claims the exact type**
(measured 2026-10-23): with `APPL` and `'****'` alone it is not listed; one
`FREF` for `BINA` (27 B of resource fork, no code) lists it first, above the
Expander, and opening with it wrote `Kept Whole` byte-exact (DECISIONS.md
2026-10-23, *the format 7.0.3 sniffs*). **Shipped since v0.44.0: twenty-nine
claimed types**, `FREF`s 130–158 in `mac/macextract_icons.r`, the types
MacStamp offers MacExtract for: `BINA`, `ApSi`, `TEXT`, `TARF`, `PIT `, `SITD`,
`SIT!`, `SIT5`, `PACT`, `DDA2`, `DDAR`, `Gzip`, `ZIP `, `ZIVU`, `LHA `,
`NCar`, `mArc` (+459 B, 232,513 → 232,972), `ZIVM`, `HBSF`, `CSIT`, `SPSV`
(+108 B, 232,130 → 232,238), and on 2026-10-24 `SDar`, `Seg1`, `Seg2`,
`Seg3`, `SegN`, `SegM`, `DDSP` and `ARC7` (+216 B, 232,238 → 232,454; `Part`
added and dropped the same day, as an unfinished expansion rather than a
segment) — no code in any of them. Measured with the shipped binary
(`as703.py --set claims`): the `BINA/TIGR` file lists MacExtract above the
Expander; `derived-Export.sit` typed `SITD/SIT!` on a volume with no StuffIt
application lists MacExtract alone and wrote `Export` (`STAK/WILD`, 6,944 B,
the manifest's SHA-256). With the Expander present a `.sit` never reaches the
dialog: its second `BNDL` (129) carries signature `SIT!`. The price of `TEXT`
(BinHex) is that an ownerless text file's dialog offers MacExtract.

**Icons.** Every claim names icon local ID 1; since 2026-10-25 that is the
archive icon (`ICN#` 129, `mac/macextract_archive_icons.r`), not the
application's — +2,336 B of resource fork, +72 B of resource map, no code
(DECISIONS.md 2026-10-25, *the archives that drew as the application*).
Measured 2026-10-23 (`as703.py --set icons`): before the claims named a mapped
icon, `Single.as` typed `ApSi/MExt` drew a blank document; MacCompress's own
`ZIVM/LZIV` file offers MacExtract alone and extracts with both forks
byte-exact (renamed `Alpha~1`). `as703.py --set sdar`:
`derived-SD__Utilities.x`'s data fork alone, typed `SDar/MExt`, launches
MacExtract on a double-click, its three files identical in both forks to the
Linux CLI's.

### Timed on the same Mac Plus (re-measured 2026-10-19)

Both applications on Mini vMac `mv-4M-1x` (7 MHz, 4 MB, System 6.0.8), same
archive, same volume, timed from the action that starts the work to the last
change of the Mac's screen buffer, on the emulated Mac's own `Ticks` read at
50 Hz (`tools/vmac/macclock.py`); ours is `dist/MacExtract.bin`, v0.41.0.
`tools/vmac/expander.py --ours dist/MacExtract.bin --repeat 2`; the
instrument and what it cannot see are in `tools/vmac/README.md`.

| Fixture | Format | In | MacExtract | Expander 4.0.2 | Expander ÷ ours |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `derived-Export.sit` | StuffIt | 1.6 KB | **1.9 s** | 2.4–2.5 s | 1.26x |
| `derived-EmpireBuilderDemo.sit` | StuffIt | 278 KB | **52.4 s** | 54.4 s | **1.04x** |
| `derived-FirstClass_Demo.cpt` | Compact Pro | 249 KB | **79.5 s** | 155.2 s | **1.95x** |
| `macburger-helper.hqx` | BinHex → StuffIt | 307 KB | **245.9 s** | 423.7 s | **1.72x** |

**Two runs of each agree to two ticks** (0.03 s): 117/117, 146/148,
3,150/3,150, 3,274/3,274, 4,781/4,781, 9,333/9,334, 14,788/14,789 and
25,483/25,483 ticks; host ÷ Mac seconds 0.994–1.001 on all sixteen. **Ours is
faster on all four.**

**Corrected 2026-10-19:** the earlier screenshot-poll figures were upper
bounds, not lower bounds; the StuffIt row's "1.00x" is withdrawn (BLOCKERS.md,
*Corrected figures*; DECISIONS.md 2026-10-19, *the clock inside the Mac*).

**Not comparable with *Measured on a 7 MHz Mac Plus***, which times the
autotest build (`RunExtract` once, CRC read-back): for `macburger-helper.hqx`
46 s there is the BinHex decode alone, 246 s here includes the 312-file
archive inside.

**They wrote the same names** on every fixture. Defaults differ: Expander
gathers a multi-item archive into a `<archive> Folder`, writes a preferences
file into the System Folder, and leaves the intermediate `MacburgerHelper.sit`
on the volume, where the shell deletes each envelope once the next layer is
out.

**Our figures include a `FlushVol` after every fork and Expander's include
none** (measured 2026-08-30 off the image being written): when the screen
stops changing, Expander's files are not on the volume; Command-Q commits them
within a second, 1.5–1.6 s on the screen clock in every 2026-10-19 run. On
this emulator's seek-free disk that costs little; a real Mac Plus is not
measured (MANUAL_TESTS.md).

  name records — neither this engine nor `unar` reads an InstallerMaker 1.x

  listing, the plug-in bundles, `Documentation/StuffIt Deluxe Read Me!` and
